# Ts-245

### <span class="fac">[134\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/134.webp)</span>

… and in any case, must we, in order to achieve communication with others, resort to indirect representation? We tell ourselves: It is impossible for us to have an experience that is essentially related to things that are completely outside the sphere of immediate perception without adding something to the figure in our imagination. One could, for example, say: “You claim that you see the figure as a wire frame. Do you also know whether it is copper wire or iron wire? And why should it be _wire_? – This shows that the word “wire” does not necessarily belong to the description of the experience.

### <span class="fac">[134\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/134.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">690.</span> Let us now consider this type of explanation: If one holds one's nose while eating, the foods lose all taste except for sweetness, bitterness, saltiness, and acidity. So, let us say that the particular taste of bread, for example, consists of this ‘taste’ in the narrower sense and the aroma, which is precisely what is lost when we do not breathe through our nose. Why shouldn't something similar happen when seeing something as something? For example: The _eye_ does not distinguish the figure as a wire frame from the figure as a box, etc. This is, as it were, the aroma that the brain adds to what is seen. In contrast, the eye also distinguishes different aspects: it phrases, as it were, the visual image; and _one_ phrasing is more appropriate to _one_ interpretation, the other to the other. (More appropriate in terms of _experience_.) Think, for example, of certain _involuntary_ interpretations that we give to one or another part of a piece of music. We say: this interpretation is forced upon us. (This is, after all, an experience.) And the interpretation can be explained by certain purely musical _relationships_. – Well, but we don't want to explain it, we want to describe it.

### <span class="fac">[134\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/134.webp),[135\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/135.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">691.</span> Look at the triangle so that c is the base and C is the apex; and now look at it so that b is the base and B is the apex. – What are you doing? – Above all: – Do you know what you are doing? No. “Well, perhaps it is the gaze that first rests on the ‘base’ and then moves to the ‘apex.’” But can you say that in another context the gaze could not move in exactly the same way without you having seen the triangle in this way? Try this as well. Look at the triangle so that it (like an arrow tip) points in the direction A, once in the direction B.

### <span class="fac">[135\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/135.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">692.</span> Of whom do we say that he sees the triangle as an arrow pointing to the right? Of the one who has simply learned to use it as such an arrow and has always used it in that way? No. This, of course, does not mean that we say of such a person that he sees it _differently_, or that we do not know how he sees it. It is not yet a question of seeing it in one way or another here. – But what about a case in which I correct the other and say, “What is there is not an arrow pointing to the right, but one pointing upwards,” and now I explain the practical consequence of this interpretation. He now says: “I have always understood the triangle as an arrow pointing to the right.” – Is this a question of seeing? No, because it can also mean, “When I encounter this sign, I have always followed it _in this way_.” Whoever says this would not even understand the question, “But did you _see_ it as an arrow pointing to the right?”

### <span class="fac">[135\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/135.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">693.</span> We say of _him_ that he sees the triangle one way, then another, when he states this himself, when he utters these words with the sign of understanding, or hears them; but also of the one who perhaps says, “Now the triangle points in this direction, before it pointed in the other direction,” and who, when asked whether the triangle has changed its shape or position, answers: that is not the case. Etc.

### <span class="fac">[135\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/135.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">694.</span> Let us consider the case of the picture of the counter-rotating wheels. First, I can see the movement in the picture as _one_ or the _other_. Secondly, I can also _take_ it to be one or the other.

### <span class="fac">[135\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/135.webp),[136\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/136.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">695.</span> This _somewhat strange_ phenomenon of seeing something one way or another only really appears when one realizes that the visual image remains the same in _one_ sense, & something else, which one might call “perception,” can change. If I consider the image to be this or that, let’s say two wheels moving in opposite directions, then this doesn’t yet involve any division of the impression into visual image & perception. – Should I therefore say that the separation is the phenomenon that interests me? Or should we ask: Which _reaction_ interests me? The one that shows that someone considers a bowl to be a bowl (that is, also that he considers a bowl to be something else)? Or the one that shows that he observes a change & at the same time that nothing has changed in the visual image?

### <span class="fac">[136\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/136.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">696.</span> It is also possible that I say: “I always thought it was a bowl; now I see that it is not” – without being conscious of a change in ‘aspect’. I simply mean: I now see something different, now have a different visual impression. Suppose someone shows me something and asks what it is. I say, “It is a cube.” Then he says: “So, that is how you see it.” – Must I understand these words differently than: “So, that is what you take it to be”?

### <span class="fac">[136\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/136.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">697.</span> When I look at the objects around me, I am not conscious that there is such a thing as visual interpretation.

### <span class="fac">[136\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/136.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">698.</span> “I see this figure as a spatial corner”: why don’t you simply take it as true – if he can speak German and is credible? – I do not doubt that it is true. But what he said is a temporal sentence. Not one about the essence of this phenomenon; rather, it says: this has taken place.

### <span class="fac">[136\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/136.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">699.</span> The expression of the experience is: “I now see this as a pyramid; now as a square with the diagonals.” – What then is ‘this’, which I see one way, then another? Is it the drawing? And how do I know that it is the same drawing both times? Do I only know it, or do I also see it? – What if it could be shown that the drawing has always changed a little when one sees it as something else; or that the visual image is then a little different. It looks, for example, as if a line is a little stronger or thinner than before.

### <span class="fac">[136\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/136.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">700.</span> Should I say that the different aspects of the figure are associations? And what would that help me?

### <span class="fac">[136\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/136.webp),[137\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/137.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">701.</span> It seems that something changes in the visual image of the figure; and yet nothing changes. And I cannot say: “A new interpretation occurs to me again and again.” Yes, it is probably that; but it is also immediately embodied in what is seen. A new aspect of the drawing occurs to me again and again – which I see as remaining the same. It is as if it is always given a new dress, and as if every dress is nevertheless the same as the other. One could also say: “I not only interpret the figure, but I also put the interpretation on it.”

### <span class="fac">[137\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/137.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">702.</span> I say to myself: What is this? What does this phrase really say? What does it really express?” – It seems to me that there must be an even clearer understanding of it than what I have. And this understanding would be achieved by saying a lot about the environment of the phrase. As if one wanted to understand an expressive gesture in a ceremony. And to explain it, I would have to analyze the ceremony, as it were, and show how that would influence the role of that gesture.

### <span class="fac">[137\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/137.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">703.</span> I could also say: It seems to me that there must be parallels to this musical expression in other areas.

### <span class="fac">[137\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/137.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">704.</span> The question is actually: Are these sounds not the best expression for what is being expressed here? Probably. But that does not mean that they cannot be explained by examining their environment.

### <span class="fac">[137\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/137.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">705.</span> Is it a contradiction if I say: “This is beautiful and this is not beautiful” (while pointing to different objects)? And should one say that it is not a contradiction because the two words “this” have different meanings? No; the two “this” have the _same_ meaning. “Today” has the same meaning today as it had yesterday, “here” has the same meaning here and there. It is not like in the sentence “Mr. White became white.” “This is beautiful and this is not beautiful” _is_ a contradiction, but it has no use.

### <span class="fac">[137\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/137.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">706.</span> The fundamental problem with Russellian logic, as well as with my own in the Tractatus Logico-Philosophicus, is that what a sentence is, is illustrated with a few commonplace examples, and then it is assumed to be understood in a general way.

### <span class="fac">[137\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/137.webp),[138\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/138.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">707.</span> But is it not clear that the two “this” have different meanings, since I can replace them with different proper names? – Replace? “This” does not even mean A one time and B another time. – Of course not alone; but together with the pointing gesture. – Yes; but that only says that a sign, consisting of the word “this” and a gesture, has a different meaning than a sign, consisting of “this” and another gesture. But that is just quibbling: you are saying that your sentence “This is beautiful and this is not beautiful” is not a complete sentence because gestures are also part of the words here. – But why is it not a complete sentence then? It is a sentence of a different kind than, for example, “The sun rises,” the way it is used is very different. But there are simply countless such differences in the realm of sentences.

### <span class="fac">[138\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/138.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">708.</span> “A. Schweitzer is not Swiss.” If I say that, I mean the first S. as a proper name, the second as a generic name. Does something different happen in my mind when I pronounce the two words “S.”? – The word functions in the sentence in different ways both times. That would be like comparing the word to a part of a machine and the _sentence_ to the machine. Completely inaccurate. One could rather say: language is the machine, the sentence is the part of the machine. That would then be something like this: This crank has two holes of the same size. One sits on the shaft, the other holds the crankpin.

### <span class="fac">[138\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/138.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">709.</span> Try to mean the first “S.” as a generic name, the second as a proper name: How do you make the attempt?

### <span class="fac">[138\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/138.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">710.</span> “The concept of S. is not S.” Is that nonsense? Well, I don’t know what someone who says that wants to say with it: i.e. how he wants to use the sentence. I can think of some obvious uses for it. – “But you _cannot_ use it in such a way, or even think of it in such a way, that the words “the concept of S.” and the second “S.” mean the same thing, as you _usually_ mean with these words. That is where the mistake lies. Here, one thinks as if this comparison were before one: The words in the sentence fit together, i.e., one can write down the nonsensical sequence of words; but the meaning of each word is an invisible _body_, and these bodies of meaning do _not_ fit together. ((“The meaning gives the sentence another dimension.”))

### <span class="fac">[138\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/138.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">711.</span> Hence the idea that one cannot think the sentence; for in thought, I would now have to put the meaning of the words together into a sense, and that is not possible. (jigsaw puzzle).

### <span class="fac">[138\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/138.webp),[139\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/139.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">712.</span> But is the contradiction not forbidden by the law of contradiction? – “non (p & non p)” certainly does not forbid anything. It is a tautology. But if we forbid a contradiction, we exclude contradictory forms from our language. We eliminate these forms.

### <span class="fac">[139\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/139.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">713.</span> One can think: “How remarkable that the _one_ meaning of the word “to feel” (and other psychological verbs) is composed of heterogeneous components, the meanings of the _first_ and _third_ person.” But what could be more different than the profile and the full-face view of a face; and yet the concepts of our language are formed in such a way that one appears as only a variation of the other. And it is, of course, easy to point out the _reasons_ for this formation of concepts. (Heterogeneous: the pipe bowl and the pipe stem.)

### <span class="fac">[139\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/139.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">714.</span> If the formation of concepts can be explained by facts of nature (psychological and physical), is not the description of our concepts actually a disguised natural science? Should we not be interested in what justifies them in nature, rather than in grammar? We are, of course, also interested in the correspondence between our grammar and general (rarely expressed) facts of nature. But our interest does not lie in these _possible_ causes. We are not conducting a natural science: our _goal_ is not to predict something. Yes, we are not even conducting natural history, since we invent natural-historical facts for other purposes.

### <span class="fac">[139\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/139.webp),[140\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/140.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">715.</span> We are, for example, interested in establishing that in our environment certain forms are not bound to certain colors. That, for example, we do not always see green in connection with the circular form, red with the square form. Imagine a world in which forms and colors are always linked in this way, and one would find concept systems understandable, in which the fundamental _division_ – form and color – does not exist. Here are a few more examples:

It is, for example, important that we are accustomed to drawing with a pen, pencil, or the like, and that therefore the elements of our representation are lines and dots (in the sense of “small dots”). Had people not drawn, but always painted (so that the concept of the _contour_ of forms played no major role), there would be a common word, say “line,” which no one would associate with _strokes_, i.e., something very thin, but always only with the boundary of two colors, and one would never think of “dot” as something tiny, but only as the intersection of two color boundaries; perhaps some developments in geometry would have been prevented. If we saw one of our primary colors, say red, only very rarely, only in tiny amounts, if we could not produce paint colors, if red only occurred in certain combinations with other colors, for example only at the tips of the leaves of certain trees that gradually turn from green to red in autumn, then it would be perfectly natural to call red a degenerate green. Think of the circumstances under which white and black appear to us as _colors_, and on the other hand as the absence of a color. Suppose all colors could be washed away and the ground would then always be white, and there was no white paint. It is easier for us to reproduce and recognize a pure red, green, etc. from memory than a shade of brown-red.

### <span class="fac">[140\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/140.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">716.</span> But I am not saying: If the facts of nature were different, we would have different concepts. This is a hypothesis. I have no use for it, and it does not interest me. I only say: If you believe that our concepts are the right ones, the ones appropriate for intelligent people, who have other concepts would simply not understand what we understand, then imagine certain general facts of nature differently than they are, and other concept formations will seem _natural_ to you.

### <span class="fac">[140\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/140.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">717.</span> “Naturally,” not “necessarily.” Is everything we do expedient? Is everything that cannot be called expedient, inexpedient?!

### <span class="fac">[140\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/140.webp),[141\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/141.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">718.</span> The familiar face of a word; the sensation that a word is, as it were, a picture of its meaning; it has absorbed its meaning within itself – there could be a language in which all of this is foreign. And how are these sensations expressed in us? In how we choose and value words. ((Goethe on personal names. If-feeling.))

### <span class="fac">[141\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/141.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">719.</span> The cases in which we can rightly say that we are _interpreting_ what we see as this and that are easy to characterize.

### <span class="fac">[141\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/141.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">720.</span> (()) If one explains “I associate this object with the figure,” this does not make anything clearer.

### <span class="fac">[141\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/141.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">721.</span> How is “wollen” really used? In philosophy, one is not conscious of the fact that one has invented a completely new use of the word for it, by adapting it to that of “wünschen,” for example. It is interesting that one constructs word uses specifically for philosophy, by wanting to claim a more developed use for words that seem important to us than they actually have. “Wollen” is sometimes used in the meaning of “trying.” “I wanted to get up, but I was too weak.” On the other hand, one wants to say that, whenever an arbitrary movement is made, it is wanted. So, if I walk, speak, eat, etc., then I should now want to do that. And here it cannot mean “trying.” Because if I walk, that does not mean that I am trying to walk and succeeding. Rather, I usually walk without trying. One can, of course, also say “I walk because walking wants to happen,” if this distinguishes the ordinary case of walking from the one in which I am pushed, or electric currents move my leg muscles.

### <span class="fac">[141\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/141.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">722.</span> Philosophy has devised a use of the word that is, as it were, a consistent implementation of certain features of ordinary use.

### <span class="fac">[141\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/141.webp),[142\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/142.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">723.</span> “The word ‘x’ has two meanings” means: it has two kinds of use. Should I say: “If you describe the use of this word in our language, you will see that it has two uses and not just _one_”?

### <span class="fac">[142\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/142.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">724.</span> Couldn’t we imagine that people explained that the word “bank” always has the same meaning. A bank is always something like this: 🖵

That, however, they also use the word for a financial institution; but say that because it is a bank, it is still something of the kind of our depiction.

### <span class="fac">[142\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/142.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">725.</span> Do the words “gehen” and “ging” have the same meaning? Do the words “gehen” and “gehst” have the same meaning? Does the word “go” in “I go” and in “you go” have the same meaning?

### <span class="fac">[142\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/142.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">726.</span> Should I say: “Two different meanings involve two different explanations of the meaning”?

### <span class="fac">[142\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/142.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">727.</span> Imagine a language with a group of sentences, each of three signs. The sentences describe the work that a _particular_ person performs. The first sign (from left to right) is the name of the person, the second denotes an activity (such as sawing, drilling, filing), and the third denotes the workpiece. Such a sentence could then read “a a a,” namely if “a” is the name of a person, a workpiece, and an activity.

### <span class="fac">[142\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/142.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">728.</span> What does it mean to say that the sign “a” has a different meaning in “x a y” and in “a x y”? One could also say that it has different meanings depending on its _position_. (Like a digit in a decimal system.) Imagine a chess game played with uniformly shaped pieces. One would then always have to remember where a particular piece was located at the beginning of the game. And one could say: “This piece and that piece have different meanings”; I cannot move one in the same way as the other. Similarly, I infer from the “a” in the first position that it refers to _this_ person (I might point to him), from the “a” in the second position that he is doing _this_ work; etc. The “a” could be in three tables, which serve to arrange it in certain pictures that explain its meaning. And I would then look in a different table, depending on the position of the “a,” in order to interpret the sentence.

### <span class="fac">[142\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/142.webp),[143\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/143.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">729.</span> What does it mean to say: “Investigate whether ‘f(f)’ makes sense if ‘f’ has the same meaning in both places”?

### <span class="fac">[143\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/143.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">730.</span> One searches, has not yet found, but one knows what one is looking for. – But it is also possible that one looks around without knowing what one is looking for; finally, one picks up something and says “This is what I wanted.” One could call this “searching without knowing what one is looking for.”

### <span class="fac">[143\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/143.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">731.</span> One could talk about “functional states.” (e.g., I am very irritable today. If someone says this and that to me today, I always react in this way. In contrast: I have had a headache all day.)

### <span class="fac">[143\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/143.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">732.</span> How did one come to use an expression like “I believe…”? Did one suddenly become aware of a phenomenon that involves belief?

### <span class="fac">[143\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/143.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">733.</span> Did one observe oneself and thus discover this phenomenon?

### <span class="fac">[143\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/143.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">734.</span> Did one observe oneself and other people and thus discover the phenomenon of belief?

### <span class="fac">[143\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/143.webp),[144\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">735.</span> In the language of a tribe, there might be a pronoun that we do not possess and for which we have no practical use, a pronoun that refers to the punctuation mark in which it is written. I will write it like this: îĉĥ. The sentence “îĉĥ am 10 centimeters long” is therefore tested for its truth by measuring the punctuation mark. The sentence “îĉĥ contains four words,” for example, is true, as is the sentence “îĉĥ does not contain four words.” “îĉĥ is false” corresponds to the paradox of the Cretan liar. – The question is: What use do people make of this pronoun? Well, the sentence “îĉĥ am 10 cm long” could serve as a standard; the sentence “îĉĥ is beautifully written” as a paradigm of beautiful writing. What interests _us_ is: How is the word “îĉĥ used in a _language-game_? For the sentence is only paradoxical if we disregard its use. I could imagine that the sentence “îĉĥ is false” is used in the nursery. When children read it, they begin to reason: “If that is false, then it is true, so it is false, etc. etc.” Perhaps people have found that this reasoning is a beneficial exercise for children.

### <span class="fac">[144\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">736.</span> How would people who cannot _see_ a triangle in the same way that we do differ from us? – If we were to come to a tribe that does not have these experiences, how would we notice it? How would we notice it if people could not _see_ depth? That is, if they were as Berkeley believed we are.

### <span class="fac">[144\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">737.</span> How many squares 🖵 fit into a square if the standard according to which the small square is to be understood has not been determined? Now, if someone were to come along and say: one cannot say with certainty how many fit in, but one can at least estimate it!

### <span class="fac">[144\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">738.</span> “The expression is similar to the feeling” – bitter food is similar to bitter grief. “Similar to the point of being indistinguishable” – what if they were not only similar, but identical?

### <span class="fac">[144\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">739.</span> “Grief and worry are similar feelings”: is this an empirical fact?

### <span class="fac">[144\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">740.</span> Should I say: “A rabbit can look like a duck”? Would it be conceivable that someone who knows a rabbit but not a duck says: “I can see the drawing as a rabbit, and also differently, even though I don’t have a word for the second aspect”? Later, he learns about a duck and says: “I saw the drawing as _that_ back then!” – Why is this not possible?

### <span class="fac">[144\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">741.</span> Or imagine someone saying, “This rabbit has a self-satisfied expression.” – If someone didn’t know anything about a self-satisfied expression, could _something_ strike him, and later, when he has learned about self-satisfaction, could he say that it was that expression that struck him back then?

### <span class="fac">[144\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">742.</span> The _apt_ word. How is it found? Describe it in contrast to this: I find the right designation for a curve after I have made certain measurements on it.

### <span class="fac">[144\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">743.</span> I see that the word is apt, even before I know, and even if I never know, _why_ it is apt.

### <span class="fac">[145\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/145.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">744.</span> I would not _understand_ someone who said that he saw the picture as a rabbit, but could not say so, because he did not know about the existence of such a being at that time.

### <span class="fac">[145\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/145.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">745.</span> Should I say: “The picture-rabbit and the picture-duck look exactly the same”?! – Something resists this. – But can I not say: They look exactly the same, namely like this – and now I create the ambiguous drawing? (The miller grinds, the painter also paints). If I were to give reasons against this way of speaking, what would I have to say? That one sees the picture differently each time, when it is once a duck and once a rabbit – or that in the case of the duck _that_ is the beak, which in the case of the rabbit are the ears, etc.?

### <span class="fac">[145\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/145.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">746.</span> Imagine the ambiguous picture being used in a comic strip: Then it is, for example, not possible that another animal encounters the duck and mistakes it for a rabbit; but it would be possible that someone mistakes the duck for a rabbit in profile in the dim light.

### <span class="fac">[145\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/145.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">747.</span> “I can see the rabbit and the duck at the same time as little as I can mean the words ‘Wotan, be gone!’ in both of their meanings at the same time.” – But that would not be correct; rather, it is not natural for us to utter these words in order to tell Wotan to go away, and at the same time communicate to him that we prefer soft eggs. And yet one could imagine such a use of words.

### <span class="fac">[145\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/145.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">748.</span> The _facts_ of human natural history that shed light on our problem are difficult for _us_ to find, because our language _passes them by_ – it is occupied with other things. (So we say to someone “Go to the store and buy…” – not: “Put your left foot in front of your right foot, etc., etc., then put the money on the counter, etc., etc.”)

### <span class="fac">[145\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/145.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">749.</span> If I do not believe in an inner state of seeing, and the other person says, “I see…,” then I believe that he either does not know German, or is lying.

### <span class="fac">[145\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/145.webp),[146\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/146.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">750.</span> What did he say, the one who claims that whoever sees the drawing once as a rabbit and once as a duck has completely different visual experiences? The tendency to say that becomes very strong when, for example, one makes a line in the drawing that emphasizes the rabbit's mouth, and then sees how that line now plays a completely different role in the duck image. – Or think of seeing the facial expression of the rabbit, which completely disappears in the other picture. For example, I first see a haughty face and then I see no haughty face. And what does the one do who admits that I see something completely different each time?

### <span class="fac">[146\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/146.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">751.</span> “How do I know that I am smiling at _this_ facial expression?”

### <span class="fac">[146\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/146.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">752.</span> “I have a very specific facial expression, which I call the rabbit's, and a completely different one, which I call the duck's.” Let me just call one A and the other B: How could I, without referring to a rabbit and a duck, explain the meaning of A and B to someone? For example, it would be possible _like this_: I say “A” and imitate the facial expression of a rabbit with my face, etc..

### <span class="fac">[146\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/146.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">753.</span> “‘_To see_’ does not mean: to react _in this way_, – because I can see without reacting.” Of course. Because neither does “I see” mean: I react, nor does “he sees” mean: he reacts, nor does “I saw” mean: I reacted, etc.. And even if I always say “I see” when I see, these words would not say: “I say ‘I see.’”

### <span class="fac">[146\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/146.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">754.</span> I point to a specific spot in the picture and say, “That is the eye of the rabbit or the duck.” How can something in this drawing be an _eye_?

### <span class="fac">[146\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/146.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">755.</span> “Can one really see depth?” – “Why shouldn't one be able to see depth if one sees colors and shapes?! The fact that the image on the retina is two-dimensional is not a reason for the contrary.” – Certainly not: but the answer does not address the problem. The problem arises from the fact that the description of what is seen, what we call the “description of what is seen,” is of a different kind when I describe color and shape, perhaps through a transparent object, than when I represent the depth dimension through a gesture or a side view.

### <span class="fac">[146\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/146.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">756.</span> A remark that the arrangement in the depth dimension is a property of the ‘seen’, like any other, does not help.

### <span class="fac">[147\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/147.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">757.</span> What does it mean that the cavity in the tooth, which the dentist is examining, feels much larger to the patient than it is? For example, I point with my fingers and say that I thought it was _this_ big. According to what do I measure the distance between my fingers? – Do I measure it at all? Can one say: “I first know how big the cavity seems to me, then I show it with my fingers”? Well, in some cases one could say it; if, for example, I think the cavity is 5 mm wide and explain _this_ by showing the distance. – What if I was asked: “Did you know how big the diameter seemed to you before you showed it?” – I could answer: “Yes. Because if you had asked me earlier, I would have given you the same answer.” – To know something is not the same as _thinking a thought_.

### <span class="fac">[147\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/147.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">757.</span> When I say what I know, how do I say _that_, what I know?

### <span class="fac">[147\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/147.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">758.</span> What is the description of what I see? (That does not just mean: With what words can I describe what I see? – but also: “What does a description of what I see look like? What should I call it?”)

### <span class="fac">[147\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/147.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">759.</span> The actual feeling that the recurrence of a refrain gives us. I would like to make a gesture. But the gesture is not really characteristic of the recurrence of a refrain. Perhaps I could find a _word_ that better characterizes the situation, but it would not explain why the refrain seems like a joke to me, why its recurrence evokes laughter or a grin in me. If I could dance to the music, I could best express just _how_ the refrain affects me. Yes, there certainly could not be a better expression. I could, for example, put the words “as I said” before the refrain. And that would certainly be apt; but it does not explain why the refrain gives me such a comical impression. Because I do not always laugh when “as I said” is in the right place.

### <span class="fac">[147\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/147.webp),[148\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/148.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">760.</span> The ‘content’ of the experience, of the Erlebnis: – I know what toothache is, I am familiar with toothache, I know what it is like to see red, green, blue, yellow, I know what it is like to feel sorrow, hope, fear, joy, affection, to want to do something, to remember having done something, to intend doing something, to see a drawing alternately as the head of a rabbit and of a duck, to take a word in one meaning and not in another, etc. I know what it is like to see the sound _a_ as gray and the sound _ü_ as dark violet. – I also know what it means to imagine these Erlebnisse. When I imagine them, I am not imagining types of behaviour, or situations. – So, I know what it means to imagine these Erlebnisse? And what does it mean? How can I explain it to someone else, or to myself?

### <span class="fac">[148\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/148.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">761.</span> The concept of ‘word’ in linguistics. How is “the same word” used? “‘have’ and ‘had’ are the same word.’ ‘He says the same word twice, once loudly, once softly.’ ‘Are ‘bank’ (the banks) and ‘bank’ (the benches) the same word?’

‘They are _etymologically_ the same word.’ ‘Is it the same word ‘have’ in both cases when one says “I have a house” and “I have built a house”?’

### <span class="fac">[148\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/148.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">762.</span> Consider: a tribe that we have subjugated, that we want to make into a tribe of slaves. The behaviour, conduct of these people is of interest to us for that very reason. We want to describe it, describe various _aspects_ of this behaviour. We observe and study, for example, pain-behaviour, pleasure-behaviour, etc. Their behaviour also includes the use of language. And also includes behaviour that is learned, no less than that which is not learned, such as the crying of a child. Yes, they not only have a language, but also, within it, psychological forms of expression. – Ask yourself: How are these taught to the children of this tribe? – I now assume that the people have expressions such as the following: “I have black hair,” “He has black hair”; “I have money,” “He has money”; “I have a wound,” “He has a wound.” And now they use this grammatical construction in _psychological_ statements.

### <span class="fac">[148\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/148.webp),[149\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/149.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">763.</span> “When I heard ‘bank,’ the meaning of a financial institution floated before me.” It is as if a seed of meaning was experienced, and then interpreted. Well, is that an Erlebnis? One could even say: “I had an Erlebnis in which the seed was for this use.” That could be the natural way we express ourselves.

### <span class="fac">[149\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/149.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">764.</span> Taking a liking to something is also something that one can learn.

### <span class="fac">[149\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/149.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">765.</span> A tribe that we want to enslave. The government and the scientists declare that the people of this tribe have no minds; one can therefore use them for any purpose without scruples. Of course, their language is still of interest to us, because we must, for example, give them commands and receive reports from them. We also want to know what they say to each other, as this is related to their other behaviour. But what in them corresponds to our **‘psychological utterances’** must also interest us, because we want to keep them able to work, and therefore their utterances of pain, discomfort, depression, joy in life, etc. are of importance to us. Yes, we have also found that these people can be used with good results as test subjects in physiological and psychological laboratories, because their reactions – including the language reactions – are exactly the same as those of people with minds. _I assume_ that it has also been found that one can teach these automatons our language instead of theirs by a method that is very similar to our ‘instruction’.

### <span class="fac">[149\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/149.webp),[150\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/150.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">766.</span> These entities now learn, for example, to calculate, to do arithmetic in writing or orally. But we somehow bring it about that they can tell us the result of a multiplication after they have sat quietly for a while, without writing or speaking. Here, the image suggests that the process of calculation is, as it were, submerged and is now taking place _under_ the surface of the water. (Think of the sense in which water ‘consists’ of H and O.) We must, of course, have a command for various purposes of the kind: “Calculate this in your head!”; a question “Have you calculated it?”; yes, even “How far have you got?” A statement by the automaton “I have calculated ‥‥”; etc. etc. In short, everything that _we_, among ourselves, say about mental arithmetic is also of interest to us when they say it. And what applies to mental arithmetic also applies to other _forms_ of thinking. – – If someone among us expresses the opinion that these entities must have some kind of mind in which this and that is going on, we laugh at him. And if it even happens that the _slaves_ spontaneously form the expression that this or that has taken place _in_ them, this seems particularly strange to us.

### <span class="fac">[150\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/150.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">767.</span> We also play with these beings the game “Think of a number! – Multiply it by 5! – ‥‥.” – Does this prove that something is happening in them after all?

### <span class="fac">[150\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/150.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">768.</span> And now we observe a phenomenon – which we could interpret as the expression of the experience; we see a figure now as this, now as that. We now show them, for example, an ambiguous figure. They find the solution; and then they say something, point to something, draw something, etc., and we can teach them our expression “I now see the picture this way”. Or they have learned our language and the usual use of the word “to see” and now spontaneously form this expression.

### <span class="fac">[150\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/150.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">769.</span> What interest, what importance does this phenomenon, this reaction, have? It may be quite unimportant, quite uninteresting, or it may also be important and interesting. Some people associate certain colours with our vowels; some can answer the question which days of the week are fat and which are lean. These experiences play a very subordinate role in our lives; but I can easily imagine circumstances in which what is unimportant to us would acquire great importance.

### <span class="fac">[150\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/150.webp),[151\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/151.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">770.</span> The slaves also say: “When I heard the word ‘bank’, it meant to me ‥‥”. Question: against the background of what linguistic technique do they say this? Because that is what matters. What had we taught them, what use of the word “to mean”? And what, if anything, do we derive from their utterance? Because if we cannot do anything with it, it might interest us as a curiosity. Let us imagine people who do not know what dreams are, and who hear our accounts of dreams. Imagine one of us comes to this tribe that does not dream and gradually learns to communicate with the people. – Perhaps you think that they would never understand the word “to dream”. But they would soon find a use for it. And the doctors of the tribe might very well be interested in our dreams and draw important conclusions from the dreams of the stranger. – – One cannot say that for these people the verb “to dream” could mean nothing other than: to tell a dream. Because the stranger would use both expressions: “to dream” and “to tell a dream”, and the people of our tribe should not confuse “I dreamed ‥‥” with “I told the dream ‥‥”.

### <span class="fac">[151\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/151.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">771.</span> We ask ourselves: “What interests us about the psychological utterances of people?” – Don’t take it for granted that these verbal reactions interest us.

### <span class="fac">[151\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/151.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">772.</span> “Why does the chemical formula of a substance interest us?” “Well, of course, because we are interested in its composition.” – Here we have a similar case. The answer could also have been: “Because we are interested in its inner nature.”

### <span class="fac">[151\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/151.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">773.</span> “You will not deny that rust, water, and sugar have an inner nature!” “If one did not already know it, science would have irrefutably shown it.”

### <span class="fac">[151\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/151.webp),[152\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/152.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">774.</span> Is the hearing or thinking of a word an _actual experience_ beyond its meaning? – How is one to judge this? – – What speaks against it? Well, that one cannot discover any _content_ of this experience. It is as if one were to express an experience, but then be unable to recall what the experience actually was. As if one could sometimes recall an experience that is simultaneous with the one we are looking for, but what we get to see is only (as it were) a garment, and where the clothed thing should be, we see an emptiness. And then one is inclined to say: “You must not look for any _other_ content.” The content of the experience is simply to be described by the _specific expression (of the experience)_. But even this does not satisfy. For why do we still feel that there is _no_ content there? And is this only the case with the experience of meaning? Not also, for example, with that of remembering? If one asks me what I have done in the last two hours, I answer directly and do not read the answer off from an experience. And yet one says that I have _remembered_, and this is a mental process.

### <span class="fac">[152\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/152.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">775.</span> One might almost wonder that one can answer the question “What did you do this morning” – without searching for historical traces of one’s activity, or the like. Yes, I answer, and would not even know that this is only possible through a special mental process, remembering, if I were not told.

### <span class="fac">[152\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/152.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">776.</span> But there is, of course, an “I believe I remember it,” whether rightly or wrongly – and here the subjective aspect of the psychological comes to the fore.

### <span class="fac">[152\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/152.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">777.</span> If I now say that the experience of remembering and the experience of pain, for example, are of different kinds, this is misleading, as one might think of “experiences of different kinds” as a difference like that of a pain, a tickle, and a feeling of the commonplace. Whereas the difference we are talking about is more comparable to that of the numbers 1 and i.

### <span class="fac">[152\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/152.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">778.</span> Where does one take the concept of the ‘content’ of an experience? Well, the content of the experience is the private object, the sense-datum, the ‘object’ that I immediately grasp with the mental eye, ear, etc., etc. The inner image. – But where does one need this concept?

### <span class="fac">[152\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/152.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">779.</span> Why, when I share my subjective memory, am I not inclined to say that I have described the content of my experience?

### <span class="fac">[152\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/152.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">780.</span> Yes, if I say “Memories of those days are emerging in me,” then it seems different. Then I am inclined to speak of a content of the experience, and I imagine something like words and images that appear before my mind.

### <span class="fac">[153\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/153.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">781.</span> I can show someone what a certain pain, an itch, a tingling sensation, etc., _is_ by evoking the feeling in him and _observing_ his reaction, the description he gives of it, etc. But can I do something like this in the case of the experience of remembering? – In such a way that he can now say: “Yes, now I know what it is ‘to remember something.’” Yes, I can, of course, teach him what we call “remembering something”; I can teach him the use of these words. But _can_ he then say: “Yes, now I have experienced what it is!” (“Yes, now I know what being chilled is!”) If he said that, we would _wonder_, and think: What must he be feeling? Because we don't feel anything special.

### <span class="fac">[153\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/153.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">782.</span> If someone says, “Now I know what being chilled is,” we know that he knows it from the ‘expression of the sensation’: he shivers, makes a certain sound, says what we would also say in this case, finds the same description fitting, _as we_ do.

### <span class="fac">[153\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/153.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">783.</span> And so one could also really speak of a feeling of “long, long ago,” and these words are an expression of the sensation, but not the: “I remember having met him often.”

### <span class="fac">[153\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/153.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">784.</span> “If it passes, then it was not true love.” Why _was_ it not? Is it our experience that only this feeling and not that is lasting? Or do we use a picture: we examine the love for its _inner_ nature, which the immediate feeling does not reveal. But this picture is important to us. Love, therefore, the important thing, is not a feeling, but something deeper, which only manifests itself in the feeling. We have the word “love” and now give this title to the most important thing. (As we give the title “philosophy” to a _certain_ mental activity.)

### <span class="fac">[153\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/153.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">785.</span> We assign words, as we assign already existing titles.

### <span class="fac">[153\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/153.webp),[154\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/154.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">786.</span> “A newborn child has no teeth.” – “A goose has no teeth.” – “A rose has no teeth.” The latter is obviously true! Even more certain than that a goose has none. And yet it is not so clear. For where should a rose have teeth? The goose has none in its _beak_. And it certainly has none in its wings, but that is not what anyone means when they say it has no teeth. Yes, how would it be, if one said: The cow chews grass with its teeth and then fertilizes the rose with it, so the rose has teeth in the mouth of an animal. This is absurd because, from the start, one wouldn't even know where to look for teeth on the rose. ((This is somehow connected to the problem that the sentence “The Earth has existed for more than 100,000 years” has a clearer sense than the sentence: “the Earth has existed in the last 5 minutes.” For, if someone said _that_, I would ask him: “What observations are you referring to? What kind of observations would contradict your sentence?” Whereas I probably know what range of thoughts, what observations, the first sentence belongs to.))

### <span class="fac">[154\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/154.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">787.</span> “You see, that's how it is when one remembers something.” _Is_ it? How? – – Can one imagine someone saying: “I will never forget this experience (namely, the act of remembering)!”?

### <span class="fac">[154\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/154.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">785.</span> Is remembering an experience? _What_ does one experience? And is it an experience if the word “bank” means one thing or another to one? Again: _What_ does one experience? – One is inclined to answer: I have this and that in front of me, I have imagined it. So _that's_ all I say – that the word means this to me – and nothing has happened? Were they just words? – Not just words; and one can also say that something happened that corresponded to them – but one cannot explain that it was not just words by saying that something happened that corresponded to them. For the two expressions simply mean the same thing.

### <span class="fac">[154\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/154.webp),[155\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/155.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">786.</span> The feeling that one has already been in exactly the same situation before. I have never had this feeling. When I see a good acquaintance, his face is certainly familiar to me; it is much more familiar than if it only ‘seems familiar’ to me. But what is the familiarity? Do I feel this familiarity all the time while I am looking at him? And why wouldn't one say that? One would like to say: “I don't have any particular feeling of familiarity, no feeling that corresponds to my familiarity with him.” If I say that he is very familiar to me, because I have seen and spoken to him countless times, that should not describe a _feeling_. And what is it that this does not describe a feeling? – If, for example, someone claims that he has such a feeling all the time while he is looking at the familiar object – or if he says that he _believes_ he has such a feeling. – should I simply say that I don't believe him? – Or should I say that I don't know what kind of feeling it is? I see a good acquaintance, and someone asks me if his face seems familiar to me. I will say: no. His face is that of a person I have seen a thousand times. “And don't you have the experience of familiarity – even when you have it with a face that is barely familiar to you??” How does it show that I am not expressing a feeling when I say: of course, his face is familiar to me, yes, as familiar as possible?

### <span class="fac">[155\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/155.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">787.</span> Why is it absurd to talk about a continuous feeling of familiarity here? – “Well, because you don't feel it.” But is _that_ the answer?

### <span class="fac">[155\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/155.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">788.</span> A feeling of familiarity would be something similar to a feeling of well-being. Why does it seem right to talk about a feeling here, and not there? – This reminds me of the special expression of well-being. The purring of a cat, for example.

### <span class="fac">[155\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/155.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">789.</span> And can’t I also imagine a case in which I would say that someone has a continuous feeling of familiarity with an object? Imagine someone walking around in a room that they haven’t been in for a long time, and enjoying the familiarity of all the objects. Couldn’t one speak of a feeling of familiarity here? And why? – Do I recognize this feeling _in myself_? Do I find _that_ it makes sense to speak of this feeling?

### <span class="fac">[155\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/155.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">790.</span> I imagine that all of his actions have a familiar tone. – But how will I know that? – Well, because he tells me. He must therefore use certain words, for example, say “Everything feels so familiar,” or give some other, specific, expression of the feeling.

### <span class="fac">[155\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/155.webp),[156\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/156.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">791.</span> Feelings of the unreality of the surroundings. I once had this feeling, & many have it before the outbreak of mental illness. Everything seems somehow not _real_; but not as if one _saw_ the things unclearly, or blurred, everything looks quite as usual. And how do I know that another person has felt what I have felt? Because he uses the same words that I also find fitting. But why do I choose precisely the word “unreality” to express [it]? Not because of its sound. (A word with a very similar sound but a different meaning would not do.) I choose it because of its meaning. But I didn't learn to use this word in the meaning _of a feeling_! No; but I learned it in a certain meaning & now I use it spontaneously _in that way_. One could say – although this can be misleading –: If I learned the word in its usual meaning, then I choose _it_ now as a simile for my feeling. But of course, this is not about a simile, about a comparison of the feeling with something else.

### <span class="fac">[156\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/156.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">792.</span> The fact is simply that I use a word, the carrier of a certain technique, as an expression of feeling. I use it in a new way. And what is this new way of use? Well, one thing is that I _say_: I have a ‘feeling of unreality’ – after having learned the use of the word “feeling” in the usual way. Also: the feeling is a state.

### <span class="fac">[156\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/156.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">793.</span> Anger. “I hate ‥‥” is obviously the expression of hate, “I am angry” is rarely the expression of anger. Is anger a feeling? And why isn’t it one? – Above all: what does one do when one is angry? How does one behave? In other words: when does one say that someone is angry? In such cases, one learns to use the expression: “I am angry.” Is it the expression of a feeling? – And why _should_ it be the expression of a feeling, or of feelings?

### <span class="fac">[156\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/156.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">794.</span> So, is anger not an experience? – Is it an experience when I, let’s say, clench my fist, or utter a sentence, or write it down?

### <span class="fac">[156\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/156.webp),[157\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/157.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">795.</span> Consider the various psychological phenomena: thinking, pain, anger, joy, wish, fear, intention, memory, etc. – and compare the behaviour that corresponds to each. – But what constitutes behaviour here? Only the play of facial expression and gestures? Or also the environment, so to speak, the cause of this expression? And if one now also includes the environment, – how then is behaviour in anger and in remembering, for example, to be compared?

### <span class="fac">[157\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/157.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">796.</span> Isn’t this the same as saying: “Make a comparison of different states of water” – and meaning by that its temperature, the speed at which it flows, the color, etc.?

### <span class="fac">[157\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/157.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">797.</span> Human behaviour, of course, includes not only what people do without ever having learned a behaviour, but also what they do (that is, for example, what they _say_) after they have received training. And this behaviour has its importance in relation to the particular training. – For example, if someone has learned to use the words “I am happy” in the same way that another uses the words “I am afraid,” then we will draw different conclusions from the same behaviour.

### <span class="fac">[157\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/157.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">798.</span> “But can’t he be afraid, even if he never _expresses_ it?” – What does this “can” mean? Is it meant to say: “Does it happen that someone is afraid without ever saying so?” – No. Rather: “Does it make sense, for example, to ask this question?” – Or: does it make sense if a novelist tells us that someone was afraid, but never expressed it? Well, it makes sense. But in what way? What I mean is: – Where and how is such a sentence used? When I ask, “What sense does it have?” – I don’t want to be answered with a picture, or a series of pictures, but with a description of situations.

### <span class="fac">[157\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/157.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">799.</span> “But depression is a _feeling_; you surely don’t mean to say that you are depressed and don’t feel it? And where do you feel it?” Here, it depends on what one calls “feeling.” If I direct my attention to my bodily feelings, I notice a very slight headache, a slight discomfort in my stomach; perhaps a certain tiredness. But do I mean _that_ when I say that I am deeply depressed? – And yet I say again: “I feel a weight on my mind.” “Well, I can’t express it any other way!” – But how remarkable that I say it that way and cannot express it any other way!

### <span class="fac">[157\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/157.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">800.</span> My difficulty is very similar to that of a person who invents a new calculus (such as differential calculus) and seeks a symbolism.

### <span class="fac">[157\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/157.webp),[158\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/158.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">801.</span> Depression is not a bodily feeling: Because we do _not_ learn the expression “I feel depressed” under the circumstances that characterize a particular bodily feeling.

### <span class="fac">[158\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/158.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">802.</span> “But depression, anger, is surely a particular feeling!” – What kind of sentence is that? Where is it used?

### <span class="fac">[158\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/158.webp),[159\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/159.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">803.</span> The uncertainty: whether a person really has this feeling, or is only pretending. But of course, it is also uncertain whether he is only pretending to pretend. Only, this pretending is less common and has more easily understandable reasons. – But what is this uncertainty based on? Am I really always in doubt as to whether someone is really angry, sad, happy, etc.? No. No more than I am in doubt about whether I have a writing book in front of me and a pen in my hand, or about whether the book will fall if I let it drop, or about whether I have not made a miscalculation when I say that 25 × 25 is 625. But this is true: I cannot give criteria that would eliminate all doubt about the presence of the sensation; and this means: such criteria do not exist. – But what is this fact? Is it a _psychological_ one, relating to sensations? One might want to say that it lies in the nature of the sensation, or of the expression of the sensation. I could say: it is a characteristic of our language-game. – But even if that is true, it still overlooks a major point: in _certain_ cases, I am in doubt as to whether the other person is in pain or not; for example, I do not feel completely secure in my sympathy for him, and _no_ utterance can resolve this uncertainty. – I then say, for example: “He could still be pretending.” But why must it be the case that he is pretending; for pretending is only a very specific case of someone expressing pain and not feeling it. A certain poison could put him in a state in which he ‘acts as an automaton’, does not pretend, but feels nothing, although he expresses feelings. I imagine, for example, that this poison causes him to repeat all the actions of his illness, in the correct sequence, some time after the actual illness, while the objective illness, the causes of the pain, for example, have ceased to exist. We then feel as little sympathy for him as we do for someone under anesthesia. We say that he repeats all the expressions of pain, etc., purely automatically, and is of course not pretending.

### <span class="fac">[159\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/159.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">804.</span> “I can never _know_ what is going on in him; _he_ always knows.” Yes, if one thinks philosophically, one would like to say that. But what situation does _this statement_ correspond to? We hear every day that one person says of another that he is in pain, is sad, happy, etc., without the slightest doubt; and relatively rarely that one does not know what is going on in him. So it is not so bad with the uncertainty. And it also happens that one says: “I know that you felt that way at the time, even if you don't want to admit it now.”

### <span class="fac">[159\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/159.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">805.</span> The picture “He knows it – I don’t know it” is one that makes ignorance seem particularly irritating. It is similar to when one searches for an object in different shops, and says to oneself that God knows all the time _where_ it really is, and that we are searching these shops in vain.

### <span class="fac">[159\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/159.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">806.</span> “Every person knows that he is in pain” – and does he also know exactly how strong his pain is?

### <span class="fac">[159\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/159.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">807.</span> The uncertainty of the statement “He is in pain” could be called a constitutional one.

### <span class="fac">[159\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/159.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">808.</span> The child who learns to speak learns the use of the words “to have pain” and also learns that one can pretend to be in pain. This is part of the language-game that he learns. Or rather: he learns not only the use of “He is in pain,” but also of “I think he is in pain.” (But of course not of “I think I am in pain.”)

### <span class="fac">[159\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/159.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">809.</span> “He can also pretend to be in pain” – that is, he can behave as if he had it; without actually having it. Certainly; and such a sentence certainly emphasizes a particular picture; but does this influence the use of “He is in pain”?

### <span class="fac">[159\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/159.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">810.</span> But what if someone were to say: “Experiencing pain and feigning pain are very different states of mind, which may have the same expression in behaviour”?

### <span class="fac">[159\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/159.webp),[160\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/160.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">811.</span> So feigned pain and real pain have the same expression? And how does one distinguish them? How do I know that the child, to whom I am teaching the use of the word “pain,” is not misunderstanding me and always calling “pain” what I call “feigned pain”?

### <span class="fac">[160\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/160.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">812.</span> Suppose someone explains the teaching of the use of the word “pain” in this way: When, on certain occasions, the child behaves in such and such a way, I think it feels what I feel in such cases; and if I am not mistaken, the child associates the word with its feeling and uses the word when the feeling occurs again. – This explanation is probably correct; but _what_ does it explain? Or: What kind of ignorance does it remedy? – It tells us, for example, that people do _not_ associate this word with a behaviour, or with an ‘occasion’. Anyone who did not know whether the word “pain” refers to a feeling or a behaviour would be instructed by this explanation. It also says that the word is _not_ sometimes used for one feeling, sometimes for another – as it well could be.

### <span class="fac">[160\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/160.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">813.</span> The explanation says that I am using the word incorrectly if I later use it for a different feeling. A whole cloud of philosophy condenses into a drop of symbolic practice.

### <span class="fac">[160\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/160.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">814.</span> Why should the words “I believe he is in pain” not be mere nonsense? As if someone said, “I believe my teeth are in his mouth.”

### <span class="fac">[160\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/160.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">815.</span> A tribe: The people often pretend, lying on a path, apparently sick and in pain; if one comes to their aid, they attack the helper. The tribe has a specific word for this behaviour.

### <span class="fac">[160\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/160.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">816.</span> Instead of “It is uncertain whether he is in pain,” one could also say: “Be suspicious of his expressions of pain!” – And how does one do that?

### <span class="fac">[160\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/160.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">817.</span> Believing that the other person is in pain, doubting whether he is, are so many natural ways of behaving towards other people; and our language is only a tool and a further development of this behaviour. I mean: our language-game is a development of the more primitive behaviour. (For our language-game is behaviour.)

### <span class="fac">[160\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/160.webp),[161\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/161.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">818.</span> “I am not sure whether he is in pain.” – Now, if someone were to prick himself with a needle every time he says this, in order to have the meaning of the word ‘pain’ vividly before his mind & to know _what_ he is doubting in the other person! Would the sense of his statement now be secured by the fact that he inflicts pain on himself _while making it?_ Would he now know _what_ he is doubting in the other person! – But how will he doubt _what_ he is now feeling in the other person? How will he connect the doubt to his feeling? Yes, what is the way from his pain to the other person? Yes, can he really doubt the other person’s pain better if he himself feels pain while doing so? Must I, in order to be able to doubt whether someone has a cow, have one myself?

### <span class="fac">[161\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/161.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">819.</span> He has, then, the real pain; and the possession of it is what he doubts in the other person. – But how does he do it? – It is as if I said to someone: “Here you have a chair; do you see it? And now translate it into French!”

### <span class="fac">[161\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/161.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">820.</span> So he has the real pain – & now he knows what he should doubt in the other. He has the object before him; & it is _not_ ‘behaviour’, or anything of the kind. (But now!) To doubt whether the other is now feeling pain, _one must_ have the _concept_ of pain; not pain itself. & it is probably true that one could communicate this concept to me by inflicting pain on me.

### <span class="fac">[161\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/161.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">821.</span> It would be just as incorrect to explain the concept of understanding meaning by means of an experience of meaning, as it would be to explain the concept of reality and unreality by means of the experience of unreality; or the concept of a person’s presence by means of the feeling of presence. One could just as well try to explain what chess is by means of a chess feeling.

### <span class="fac">[161\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/161.webp),[162\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/162.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">822.</span> “But one can see the figure as an arrow and as a bird’s foot, even if one never tells anyone.” And that, again, means: it makes _sense_ to say that someone sees the figure one way at one time and another way at another time, without ever telling anyone. – I am not saying that it has _no_ sense, but the sense is not immediately clear. – I know, for example, that people talk about a feeling of unreality; they say that everything seems unreal to them; and now one says: it could happen that everything seems unreal to a person, even if they never told anyone. How does one so readily know that it makes sense to say “it may be that everything seems unreal to this person, even though they never talk about it”? Of course, I have deliberately chosen a very rare experience here. Because it is not one of the everyday experiences, one looks more closely at the use of the words. – I would like to say: it makes sense, if only barely, to exclaim “It’s all unreal!” – and then one knows that that other statement also makes sense! – Or also like this: someone says to me, “Everything seems unreal to me.” I hardly know what that means – and yet I already know that it would make sense to say, etc. etc.. Well, this is of course because he describes an experience with the sentence; i.e., it is a psychological statement.

### <span class="fac">[162\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/162.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">823.</span> That is to say: if someone expresses a state of mind, then they could also have had it without expressing it. This is a statement. But what is the purpose of a sentence that says that N. may have had experience E, but never expressed it? Well, one can certainly imagine an application of the sentence. Suppose, for example, that one finds a trace of the experience in the brain and then says that it shows that he was still thinking or seeing this and that before his death, etc..
