### <span class="fac">[190\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/190.webp),[191\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/191.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">961.</span> I know my way around in a room: that is, I can, without having to think for a moment, find the door, open and close it, use every piece of furniture; I don't have to search for the table, the books, the cupboard, and I don't have to think about what to do with them. The fact that I know my way around will show itself in the freedom with which I move about in the room. It will also show itself in an absence of wondering and doubting. What should I answer to the question: is this being-familiar-with-this-room a state of my mind?

### <span class="fac">[191\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/191.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">962.</span> I am able to answer the question “What is a thermometer used for” _immediately_ and without any difficulty with a long series of sentences. And equally, I can follow the instruction: “Explain the application of the word ‘book’.”

### <span class="fac">[191\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/191.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">963.</span> One can call being familiar with something an experience, and one can also call it something else.

### <span class="fac">[191\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/191.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">964.</span> The use of certain words for the sake of the sentence rhythm. This might be _much_ more important to us than it actually is.

### <span class="fac">[191\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/191.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">965.</span> “What kind of experience is ‥‥?” One will not ask “What is it like to have it?” – because one person might answer one way, and another might answer another way. One will not ask them for a description of the experience, but rather observe how people handle the word that designates the experience.

### <span class="fac">[191\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/191.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">966.</span> I say the word “tree,” then I say a nonsense word. They feel different. In what way? – Two objects are shown to me: one is a book, the other is an object of strange shape that is unfamiliar to me. I say: they not only look different, but I also have a different _feeling_ when I look at them. I ‘understand’ one of the objects, I do not understand the other. “Yes, but it is not only the difference between familiarity and unfamiliarity.” Well, is there not also a difference between kinds of familiarity and unfamiliarity? A stranger enters my room, but it is a human being, I see that immediately. Something disguised enters my room, I do not know if it is a human being or an animal. I see an object that is unfamiliar to me on my table, an ordinary field stone, but I have never seen it on my table before. I see a stone on the path; I am not surprised, although I do not remember having seen it before. I see a strangely shaped object of unknown purpose on my table and I am _not_ surprised: it has always been there, I have never known _what_ it is and have never been interested in it, it is familiar to me.

### <span class="fac">[191\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/191.webp),[192\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/192.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">967.</span> “Well, didn't you understand the word ‘tree’ when you heard it? – – Then something must have happened inside you!” – But what, precisely? – That I understood it. – The question is only: should I say that understanding is something that happens inside me? Something resists this; & that can only mean that we, through this _expression_, connect understanding with other _phenomena_ & blur a distinction that we want to emphasize. But what distinction? – In what cases do we not refuse to say: something happened inside us when hearing the word?

### <span class="fac">[192\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/192.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">968.</span> What would we have to say to someone who told us that, in his case, understanding is an internal process? – What would we reply to him if he said that being able to play chess is an internal process? – Perhaps that nothing that happens in him interests us if we want to know whether he can play chess. And if he now replies that what happens in him does interest us, namely: whether he can play chess – then we could only contradict him by reminding him of the _criteria_ that would prove his ability.

### <span class="fac">[192\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/192.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">969.</span> In order to find one’s way about, one must not only know the correct way from one place to another, but also know where one will end up if one takes this wrong turn. This shows how similar our investigations are to walks through a landscape for the purpose of drawing a map. And it is not impossible that such a map will one day be drawn for the territories we explore.

### <span class="fac">[192\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/192.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">970.</span> Suppose you have a particular experience in understanding; how can you know that it is the one that we call “understanding”? – Well, how do you know that the experience you have is the one that we call “pain”? – That is something different – – I know it because my spontaneous behaviour in certain _situations_ is what one calls the expression of pain.

### <span class="fac">[192\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/192.webp),[193\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">971.</span> When one learns to use the word “Schmerz,” it does not happen by guessing for which of the internal processes, when, for example, one falls, this word is used. It could then also give rise to the problem: _which_ of my Empfindungen is the reason I cry when I injure myself? And in doing so, one looks inward & asks: “Is it now _this_ Empfindung, or _this_?”

### <span class="fac">[193\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">972.</span> “It’s irrelevant whether I have given the sensation the _correct_ name; I have simply given it _a_ name!” – But how does one give something, for example a sensation, a name? Can one give a name to a sensation _in itself_? What happens then; and what is the result of this action? ((Compare the remark about attaching a name tag.)) If one closes a door in one’s mind, is it then closed? And what is the consequence of this? Can no one then enter, in one’s mind?

### <span class="fac">[193\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">973.</span> “How do you know that the experience you have is what we call ‘pain’?” – The experience that I have? Which one? How do I specify it: for myself, and (for) another.

### <span class="fac">[193\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">974.</span> Suppose we could learn what is called a sensation, say a ‘pain,’ and then we were taught to _express_ this sensation. What kind of connection would _this activity_ have with the sensation in order for it to be called its ‘expression’?

### <span class="fac">[193\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">975.</span> Suppose one knew, or guessed, that a child has sensations but no way of expressing them. And now he wants to teach the child how to express those sensations. How must he connect an action with a sensation so that the action becomes its expression?

### <span class="fac">[193\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">976.</span> Can one teach the child: “Look, this is how one expresses something – this is, for example, an expression of that – & now express your pain!”

### <span class="fac">[193\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">977.</span> “Understanding” is simply not used in the same way as a word denoting sensation or feeling.

### <span class="fac">[193\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">978.</span> The confusing picture is this: that we observe a substance – its changes, states, movements; like someone who observes the changes and movements in a melting pot. Whereas we observe and compare the behaviour of people.

### <span class="fac">[193\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">979.</span> Primitive pain-behaviour is a sensation-behaviour; it is replaced by a linguistic expression. “The word ‘pain’ refers to a sensation” means something like: “‘I am in pain’ is a sensation-utterance.”

### <span class="fac">[193\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/193.webp),[194\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/194.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">980.</span> Forms of behaviour can be incommensurable. And the word “behaviour”, as I use it, is misleading in itself, because its meaning also includes the external circumstances – of behaviour in the narrower sense. Can one speak of a behaviour of anger, for example, and of another of hope? (It is easy to imagine an orangutan being angry – but hopeful? And why is it so?)

### <span class="fac">[194\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/194.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">981.</span> If someone says to me, “I now see _this_ point as the tip of the triangle,” I understand him. But what do I do with this understanding? Well, I can, for example, say to him: “Does the triangle now seem to you as if it has fallen over, as if it normally stands on the base line a? Or does it now appear to you as a mountain with B as the peak? Or as a wedge? Or as an ‘inclined plane’? Or as a cone?” You can now ask, “What _is_ it: to see the figure _like_ that?” – and, so to speak, form hypotheses about what is happening in his mind. For example, eye movements, or mental images with which he supplements what he sees – he imagines, for example, a body sliding down the inclined plane – etc. All of this _can_ happen, but it does not have to happen; and if someone tells me that he sees the triangle as a wedge, for example, he is not telling me how his eyes have moved, etc. – No; it is not _what_ is happening that is the question, but: how can one use that statement. What, for example, helps me to understand the communication. _One_ application would be: One can say to him, “Look at the triangle as a wedge; then you will no longer be surprised at ‥‥.” And he might reply: “Yes, _that_ seems more natural to me.” – So I have calmed him down through my _explanation_; or I have helped him to solve a problem more quickly.

### <span class="fac">[194\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/194.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">982.</span> To see the similarity of one face to another, the analogy of one mathematical form to another, a human shape in the lines of a puzzle picture, a spatial form in a schematic drawing, to hear or say “pas” in “ne ‥‥ pas” in the meaning of “step” – – – – all these phenomena are in some way similar, but also very different. (A visual perception of a face, an auditory perception, an olfactory perception, a perception of movement.)

### <span class="fac">[194\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/194.webp),[195\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/195.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">983.</span> In _all_ these cases, one can say that one experiences a _comparison_. For the expression of the experience is that we are inclined to make a comparison. To a paraphrase. It is an experience whose expression is a comparison. But why an ‘experience’? Well, our expression _is_ an expression of experience. – Because we say “I see it as ‥․”, “I hear it as ‥․”? No; although this way of speaking is connected with it. But it is _justified_ because the language-game makes the expression into that of an experience.

### <span class="fac">[195\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/195.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">984.</span> An experience that expresses itself in a comparison. – In order to hear, for example, “Je ne sais pas” in a conscious way, one must know other expressions, such as “not a thing”. The expression of the experience through the comparison is precisely _the_ expression, the immediate expression. Yes, the phenomenon that we observe and that interests us.

### <span class="fac">[195\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/195.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">985.</span> If someone could not hear or experience “pas” in that way, if he did not understand what we mean when we talk about “hearing it in that way,” would he also not understand us if we explained to him that “pas” also means “step” in negation, and if we said that it is analogous to the word “little,” “bit,” “thing,” etc.? But what does someone see when he realizes that the use of the word ‥‥ is analogous to the use of the word ‥‥ _analog_?

### <span class="fac">[195\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/195.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">986.</span> Well, what am I showing him with such an analogy? What do I expect from it? What effect does it have? – It does seem to be an explanation. It _is_ a kind of explanation. One also says: “Yes, now I understand the use of this word.” But one also says: “I know what you mean, but I cannot hear it in that way.”

### <span class="fac">[195\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/195.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">987.</span> “Just as we still do today ‥‥, so did these people ‥‥.” We can consider _this_ use in the light of _that_ one. This can, for example, serve as a heuristic principle.

### <span class="fac">[195\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/195.webp),[196\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/196.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">988.</span> One might say that every word – can have a different character in different contexts, but it always has _one_ character – a face. It does look at us. – One could really imagine that every word is a _small_ face, and that the written characters could be faces. And one could also imagine that the whole sentence would be a kind of group picture, so that the look of the faces would create a relationship between them and the whole would thus be a _meaningful_ group. But what is the experience that a group is meaningful? And would it be necessary for using the sentence that one feels it as meaningful?

### <span class="fac">[196\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/196.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">989.</span> Is it also certain that everyone who understands our language would be inclined to say that every word has a _face_? And, most importantly, what general tendency _in us_ does it correspond to, to have this inclination?

### <span class="fac">[196\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/196.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">990.</span> First, it is clear that the tendency to regard the word as something intimate, soulful, is not always present, or is not present to the same extent. The opposite of the soulful, however, is the mechanical. If someone wants to depict a robot, how does his behaviour differ from our usual behaviour? For example, by the fact that our usual movements cannot be described, even approximately, by means of geometrical concepts.

### <span class="fac">[196\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/196.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">991.</span> Would one, for example, also get the impression of a group picture from sentences in telegram style?

### <span class="fac">[196\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/196.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">992.</span> The prisoner has a number as a name. No one would say of it what Goethe says about personal names.

### <span class="fac">[196\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/196.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">993.</span> One has the idea that the sense of the sentence is composed of the meanings of its words. (Group picture). How is, for example, the sense of “I have not seen him yet” composed of the meanings of the words?

### <span class="fac">[196\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/196.webp),[197\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/197.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">994.</span> Even the word “have” has a face; for the word “the possessions” certainly has a _different_ face. It feels different; so “have” must also feel somehow. – But _must_ “possessions” feel different from “have”? What if someone assured me that these two words feel exactly the same to him? He says, for example: Yes, the conjunction and the verb “but,” they feel different; but not “possessions” and “have.” Should we not believe him? What appeared as a completely self-evident statement, bound to the understanding of the words, (_that_) now appears in the light of a purely personal statement. No different than if someone said that the vowels a and e have the same colour for him. Can I now say: “You are not playing our game”?

### <span class="fac">[197\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/197.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">995.</span> Is _here_ assumed by the sensitive person that he feels the two words “but” differently in all contexts? No. Only when they are spoken, experimentally, does one expect that.

### <span class="fac">[197\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/197.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">996.</span> Imagine people calculating with ‘extremely complicated’ numerical signs. However, these appear as figures that arise when one writes our numerical signs on top of each other. For example, they might write up to the fifth digit like this: 🖵. Someone observing them would find it difficult to guess _what_ they are doing. And they themselves might not be able to explain it. This numerical sign, written in a slightly different script, can change its appearance (_for us_) to the point of being unrecognizable. And what the people are doing would appear to us purely intuitively.

### <span class="fac">[197\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/197.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">997.</span> I say, therefore, that one misjudges the psychological interest of the if-sensation if one sees it as a matter of course, a correlate of the meaning of the word; rather, it must be seen in a different context, in the context of the specific circumstances under _which_ it occurs.

### <span class="fac">[197\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/197.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">998.</span> Say: “It is difficult to separate the two things” and pronounce the last word with the feeling of the conjunction! Practice, for example, in ordinary speech, pronouncing a word with a double meaning with the inappropriate feeling! (If it is not associated with an inappropriate expression of the voice, it does not harm communication.)

### <span class="fac">[197\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/197.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">999.</span> Now say to yourself: the conjunction “but” is actually the same as the verb (just as weg = way and trotz = in spite of), and pronounce the sentence “It is not better, but worse” with “but” in the meaning of the verb!

### <span class="fac">[197\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/197.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1000.</span> Are you also sure that there is _one_ if-feeling? Not perhaps several? Have you tried pronouncing the word in very different contexts? (For example, when it carries the main stress of the sentence, and when the next word carries it.)

### <span class="fac">[197\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/197.webp),[198\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/198.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1001.</span> Does anyone ever have the if-sensation when he does not pronounce the word “if”? It would certainly be strange if only _this_ cause should produce the sensation. Has James ever wondered whether, and where, one might have it otherwise? – And that is generally the case with the ‘atmosphere’ of a word: – why does one see it as self-evident that only _this_ word has this atmosphere?

### <span class="fac">[198\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/198.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1002.</span> The signature of Goethe has a Goethean quality. In that respect, it is like a face, because I could say the same about the face. It is like a _reflection_. Does this phenomenon belong to _the_ “I have been in the same situation before”? Or do I ‘_identify_’ the signature with the person by, for example, loving to look at the signature of a loved one, or having the signature of someone I admire framed on my desk? (Magic that is associated with pictures, hair, etc.)

### <span class="fac">[198\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/198.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1003.</span> The atmosphere, _inseparable_ from the thing, – so it is not an atmosphere. What is intimately associated with each other, what _was_ associated, seems to _fit_ together. But how does it seem to fit together? How is it expressed that it seems to fit? Perhaps like this: We cannot imagine that the man who was called that, looked like that, and signed his name like that, did not produce _these_ works, but perhaps quite different ones (those of another great man)? Can we not imagine that? Let us try!

### <span class="fac">[198\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/198.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1004.</span> It could be _like_ this: Imagine a painter wanting to design a picture: “Beethoven writing the Ninth Symphony.” I can easily imagine what might be seen in such a picture. But what if someone wanted to depict what Goethe would have looked like while writing the Ninth Symphony? Then I could not imagine anything that would not be _highly inappropriate_ and ridiculous.

### <span class="fac">[198\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/198.webp),[199\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/199.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1005.</span> Look at a well-known piece of furniture, in its usual place, in your room! “It is part of an organism,” one might say. Or: “Take it away, and it is no longer the same” and so on. And of course, one does not think of any _causal_ dependence of a part on the other parts. Rather, it is _like_ this: I could give this thing a name and say something about it, such as that it has moved from its place, has a stain, is dusty, etc.; but if I wanted to take it _completely_ out of its present context, I would say that it has ceased to exist, and something else has taken its place.

### <span class="fac">[199\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/199.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1006.</span> Yes, one could also feel that: “Everything belongs to everything else.” (Internal and external relation.) Move a piece and it is no longer what it was. This table is this table only in this environment. Everything belongs to everything. Here we have the inseparable atmosphere. And who is it that says this? What kind of representation does he propose? – Is it not that of a painted picture? – If, for example, the table has moved, you paint a new picture of the table _with_ its surroundings.

### <span class="fac">[199\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/199.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1007.</span> “A quite specific expression” – this also includes the fact that, if one changes the smallest thing on the face, the expression immediately changes.

### <span class="fac">[199\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/199.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1008.</span> His name seems to fit his works. – _How_ does it seem to fit? Well, I might say something like this. – But is that all? – It is as if the name, together with these works, forms a whole. When we see him, the works come to mind, and when we think of the works, the name. We utter the name with reverence. The name becomes a gesture; an architectural form.

### <span class="fac">[199\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/199.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1009.</span> Anyone who could not understand this might be described as ‘prosaic’. And is _this_ what the ‘blind to meaning’ would be?

### <span class="fac">[199\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/199.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1010.</span> Any other combination would seem wrong to us. Through our habit, these forms become a paradigm; they acquire, as it were, the force of law. (‘the power of habit’?)

### <span class="fac">[199\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/199.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1011.</span> Anyone who cannot understand and learn to use the words “to _see_ the sign as an arrow” is what I call “blind to meaning”. It would not make sense to tell him, “You must try to _see_ it as an arrow,” and he could not be helped _in this way_.

### <span class="fac">[199\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/199.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1012.</span> But what about such an expression: “When you said it, I understood it in my heart”? In this case, one points to one’s heart. And does one not _mean_ this gesture?! Of course, one does. Or is one conscious that one is only using a picture? Certainly not!

### <span class="fac">[199\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/199.webp),[200\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/200.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1013.</span> When the child learns to speak, when does it develop the ‘feeling for meaning’? Does one take an interest in this when one teaches it to speak, when one observes its progress in speaking?

### <span class="fac">[200\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/200.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1014.</span> One can also say, when observing an animal, for example, a monkey, examining and tearing apart an object: “One can see that something is going on inside it.” How strange that is! But no stranger than that we say: love, conviction is in our hearts!

### <span class="fac">[200\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/200.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1015.</span> So, when and with what does the human being begin to express feelings of meaning? In what games will it show itself?

### <span class="fac">[200\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/200.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1016.</span> Is not the inclination to _think_ of a ‘body of meaning’ similar to the inclination to _think_ of a place of thinking? – _Would_ every person have the inclination to say that he thinks in his head? – This expression is taught to him as a child. (“Mental arithmetic”) But from this, at any rate, the _inclination_ develops (or from it the expression arose). At any rate, the inclination is then present. And so is the inclination to talk about a body of meaning (or something similar), however it may have arisen.

### <span class="fac">[200\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/200.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1017.</span> Do we also speak of a ‘_feeling_’ of thinking in one’s head? Wouldn’t this be similar to the ‘feeling of meaning’? Also: can someone who doesn’t have this feeling think? Yes; someone who philosophizes or engages in psychology might say: “_I_ feel that I am thinking in my head.” But he won’t be able to say what that means. He won’t be able to say _what_ this feeling is; rather, he will simply use the expression: he ‘feels’ it, as if he were saying: “_I_ feel this sensation _here_”. He is therefore not conscious that there is still something to be investigated here, namely what his expression “I feel” means here, i.e., what consequences we can draw from this utterance. For example, the same consequences that we would draw from the utterance “I feel this pain here.”

### <span class="fac">[200\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/200.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1018.</span> One could also say: “_I_ feel the rising prices in my head.” And is that _nonsense_? To what chapter of psychology would this feeling belong? Not to the chapter on sensory impressions, – unless someone said: “Whenever I feel this pain in my head, prices always rise.”

### <span class="fac">[201\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/201.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1019.</span> Couldn’t someone say: “_I_ have a feeling of a place when thinking. I can, for example, think the thought once in my head and once in my heart.” – And would this show that a thought has a place? I mean: would it describe the experience of thinking more closely? Not just a _new_ experience? “I would like to say: ‘I thought it in my head.’”

### <span class="fac">[201\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/201.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1020.</span> One can obey the command “Don’t think of anything!”, “make your mind a blank!”

### <span class="fac">[201\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/201.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1021.</span> Just as one has learned the phrase “in one’s head,” in connection with thinking, so too has one learned: “the word has this (‘a’) meaning,” and all the phrases related to it. Also, the way of speaking: “these two words only sound the same, but otherwise have nothing to do with each other,” and many similar phrases. And the experience of meaning actually follows exactly these idioms. (Which could also have a completely different form – the French “vouloir dire,” for example.)

### <span class="fac">[201\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/201.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1022.</span> Is the experience of meaning therefore just an illusion? Well, if it is also an illusion, then the experience of this illusion is not _less_ interesting because of it.

### <span class="fac">[201\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/201.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1023.</span> It is, by the way, remarkable that the word “association” plays such a minor role in my investigations. I believe that this word is used in an extremely vague, blurred way, and for quite dissimilar phenomena.

### <span class="fac">[201\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/201.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1024.</span> One can say a lot about a subtle aesthetic difference – that is very important. That is, the first utterance is, of course, only “_This_ word fits, _this_ one does not,” or something like that; but now all the far-reaching connections that each of these words evokes can be discussed. That is, it is not simply dismissed with that first judgment, but rather, it is the _field_ of each word that _matters._

### <span class="fac">[201\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/201.webp),[202\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/202.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1025.</span> Why should the experience of meaning be important?! He says the word, says that he has now said it with this meaning, then with that one. _I_ say the same. It obviously has nothing to do with the ordinary and important use of the expression “I meant _that_ with the word.” So what is the remarkable thing? That we say something like that? That is, of course, interesting. But the interest here does not lie in the concept of ‘meaning’ of a word, but in the series of similar psychological phenomena that, in general, have nothing to do with word-meaning.

### <span class="fac">[202\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/202.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1026.</span> Someone says, for example, in language instruction, “Let’s talk about the word ‘switch.’” I ask: “Do you mean the verb, the adjective, or the noun?” – He: “I mean the noun.” – Does he have to have had an experience of meaning, or do I? No. But that images were present in our minds during this conversation is probably the case. They would play a role similar to doodling while speaking. Someone who is used to doodling on paper during a conversation might, for example, draw a switch, an egg, or write the word “switch.” And if a switch were being discussed and he drew an egg, that might distract him from the conversation; but if he drew tracks, he would stay on topic.

### <span class="fac">[202\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/202.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1027.</span> In what way can one compare ‘doodling’ with the play of images? – Imagine people who, from childhood on, produce drawings on all occasions when we would say they are imagining something. If you give them a pen, they draw with great speed. But doesn’t the ordinary person do something very similar? He doesn’t draw, but ‘describes his image,’ that is, instead of drawing, he _speaks_. Or he uses gestures to represent, for example, a person he is imagining! Must I assume that he ‘reads’ this description, these gestures from something?! What speaks for that? – Well, he _says_, for example, “I see him in my mind!” and then he represents him. But if, instead of saying this, I had taught him to say “Now I know what he looks like,” or “Now I can say what he looks like,” or “Now I will tell you what he looks like,” – then the dangerous image would be _eliminated_. (Tennis without a ball.)

### <span class="fac">[202\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/202.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1028.</span> To go into depth, one does not need to travel far; indeed, one does not need to leave one’s immediate and ordinary surroundings.

### <span class="fac">[202\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/202.webp),[203\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/203.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1029.</span> How do I find the ‘right’ word? It is as if I were comparing words based on subtle differences in taste. _This_ is too much ‥‥, _this_ is too much ‥‥; – _this_ is right. But I don’t always have to judge, explain why this or that word doesn’t fit. It simply doesn’t fit yet. I am just continuing the search, I am not satisfied. Finally, I come to rest, I am satisfied. _That_ is what searching looks like; and _that_ is what finding looks like.

### <span class="fac">[203\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/203.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1030.</span> “_I am developing what is in him_.” – How do I know that it was _in_ him? – It is not like that. One cannot ask: “How do I know that I _really_ dreamed it?” – It is in him because I _say_ that it is in him. Or better: because I am inclined to say ‥․. – And what is this strange experience: being inclined to say ‥‥? No experience at all.

### <span class="fac">[203\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/203.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1031.</span> But if I had died before I could develop all of this, – would it not be contained in my experience? – The answer “No” to this question is wrong; the answer “Yes” must also be correct. “No” would mean: If someone does not tell a dream, it is wrong to say that he had it. It would be incorrect to say: “I don’t know if he dreamed; he didn’t say anything about it.” “Yes” would mean: He probably dreamed, even if he doesn’t report it. But this is not supposed to be a _psychological_ statement! So, it is a logical one.

### <span class="fac">[203\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/203.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1032.</span> “Can someone not dream and not communicate it to anyone?” – Certainly: he can dream _and_ communicate it to someone.

### <span class="fac">[203\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/203.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1033.</span> We read in a story that someone had a dream & did not share it with anyone. We do not ask how the author could have learned this. – Do we not _understand_ it when Strachey makes conjectures about what Queen Victoria may have seen shortly before her death? Of course – but did people not also _understand_ the question of how many minds could fit on the tip of a needle? That is, the question of whether we do not _understand_ it does not help us here; we must ask _what_ we can do with such a sentence. – _That_ we use the sentence is clear; the question is _how_ we use it.

### <span class="fac">[204\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/204.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1034.</span> The fact that we use the sentence does not tell us anything, because we recognize the enormous differences in use. So, we see the problem in the _how_.

### <span class="fac">[204\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/204.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1035.</span> Now once again: – People tell us a story after waking up; we teach them the expression “I dreamed that ‥‥” and then comes the story. I sometimes ask them: “Did you dream anything last night?” and sometimes I get an affirmative answer, sometimes a negative answer, sometimes a dream narrative, sometimes none. This is the language-game. (I am now assuming that I myself do not dream. But I also have no feeling of an invisible presence and others do, and I can ask them about their experiences.) Must I now make an assumption in this case about whether these people have deceived their memory or not; whether they _really_ saw these images in front of them during sleep or whether it only seems that way to them after waking up? And what is the point of this question? – And what interest is there?! Do we ever ask ourselves this when someone tells us a dream, and if not, is it because we are sure that his memory will not deceive him? (And suppose he is a person with a _very_ bad memory!)

### <span class="fac">[204\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/204.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1036.</span> And does this mean that it is _nonsensical_ to ever ask the question: whether the dream really took place during the night, or whether the dream is really a memory phenomenon of the awakened person? It depends on _what_ we mean by it, i.e., _which_ use we make of this question. For if we have this picture of the dream: that an image floats in front of the sleeping mind (as it might be depicted in a painting), then it naturally makes sense to ask this question. We are asking: Is it _like this_, or _like that_ – – and each “like this” corresponds to a different image.

### <span class="fac">[204\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/204.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1037.</span> Imagine someone asked: Is the structure of water 🖵 or 🖵? Does it make sense? – If you give it sense, then it makes sense.

### <span class="fac">[204\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/204.webp),[205\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/205.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1038.</span> Back to the language-game of the dream narrative: Someone says to me once, “What I dreamed tonight, I will not tell anyone.” Well, does that make sense? Why not?! Should I, in light of what I have said about the origin of the language-game, say that it has _no_ sense – since the original phenomenon was precisely the dream narrative? Not at all!

### <span class="fac">[205\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/205.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1039.</span> A railway station with all its facilities, telegraph poles and telegraph wire, means to us a far-reaching transport system. But on Mars, this building with everything around it, including a piece of track, means nothing of the kind.

### <span class="fac">[205\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/205.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1040.</span> “It seems that the mind can give meaning to the word” – is that not as if I said: “It seems that in benzene, the C atoms are located at the corners of a hexagon”? This is not an _appearance_; it is an _image_.

### <span class="fac">[205\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/205.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1041.</span> I certainly do not want to give a _definition_ of the word “dream,” but rather do something similar: describe the use of the word. My question is therefore approximately this: “If I were to come to a foreign tribe with a language unknown to me, and the people had an _expression_ corresponding to our “I dream,” “he dreams,” etc., – how would I find out that this is the case; _how_ would I know which expressions in their language I should translate into these expressions in ours? Because finding this out is indeed similar to finding out which of their words I should translate into our word “table.” In this case, I am certainly not asking “What do they call _**this**_?” by pointing to something. Although I could also ask that and perhaps also indicate a symbolic _representation_ of the dream, or of a dreaming person.

### <span class="fac">[205\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/205.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1042.</span> It must also be said that the child does not necessarily have to learn the _use_ of the word “dream” in such a way that it first reports an event upon waking, and then we teach it the words “I had a dream.” It is also possible that the child hears adults say that they dreamed and then says the same about themselves and tells a dream. I am not saying that the child _guesses_ what the adult means; it is enough that it uses the word one day and uses it under the circumstances under which we use it.

### <span class="fac">[205\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/205.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1043.</span> The question is therefore not really: “How does it learn the _use_ of the word?” – but “How does it show that it uses it as we do?”

### <span class="fac">[205\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/205.webp),[206\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/206.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1044.</span> “Eternal gloom descends” – can one say: “Well, it _seems_ as if it is descending”? Do we have a hallucination of something gloomy, etc.? – What makes these words _apt_? – “We understand them.” We say, for example: “Yes, I know exactly what that is like,” and now we can describe our _feelings_ and our _behaviour_.

### <span class="fac">[206\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/206.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1045.</span> “When you talk about dreams, thoughts, _sensations_, do not all these things seem to lose the mysterious quality that appears to be their essential characteristic?” Why should a dream be more mysterious than a table? Why should they not both be equally mysterious?

### <span class="fac">[206\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/206.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1046.</span> “The phenomenon, to see it as an arrow, or in some other way, is after all a genuine visual phenomenon; even if it is not as tangible as that of shape and color.” How could it not be a visual phenomenon?! – Who, when speaking about it (unless they are doing philosophy or psychology), doubts this? Do we not ask a person about it and tell them about it, just as with any other visual phenomenon? I mean: do we talk about it any more hesitantly, with the suspicion that what we say may not have a clear sense? Certainly not. But there are nevertheless differences. Those that we indicate through the expression “less tangible.” Only it is the case that: if I present two substances to someone, I can say: “Feel this one! Don’t you also find that it feels softer?” And if he affirms it, then I say something like: “Yes, I feel that too. There _is_ therefore a difference between them” (i.e.: I didn’t just imagine it). – _It is different_, however, with the psychological phenomena. If I say: “This is less tangible than that” – namely as a timeless sentence – then this is not based on a consensus of judgments, _not_ on the fact that we all feel it too (when we ‘_observe_’ the experience).

### <span class="fac">[206\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/206.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1047.</span> Is the _phenomenon_ not placed in the wrong category? In _it_, it looks ghostly, intangible, alien. When viewed correctly, its ‘intangibility’ strikes us no more than the intangibility of time when we hear: “It is time for lunch.” (The unease of the poorly arranged.)

### <span class="fac">[206\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/206.webp),[207\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/207.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1048.</span> “This coffee has _absolutely no_ taste.” “This face has _absolutely no_ expression.” – The opposite of this is “It has a very definite expression” (although I couldn’t say which one). A _strong_ expression could, for example, immediately give rise to a story. Or the search for a story. When one speaks of the enigmatic smile of the Mona Lisa, doesn’t that presumably mean that one is asking: In what situation, in what story, could one smile like that? And it would therefore be conceivable that someone finds a solution; that he tells a story, and we say: “Yes, _that_ is the expression that _this_ character here would have adopted.”

### <span class="fac">[207\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/207.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1049.</span> Remembering a specific kinesthetic feeling – remembering the visual image of a movement. – Make the same movement with the right and left thumb, and judge whether the kinesthetic sensations are the same! – Do you have a memory-image of the kinesthetic sensation when walking? – If you are tired, or in pain, muscle pain, or a burning sensation on the skin, are the sensations when moving the limb the same as in another state? But are you sometimes in doubt whether you really lifted your leg, because the feeling is so different? – Do you really localize the sensations during the movement in the joints?

### <span class="fac">[207\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/207.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1050.</span> You sometimes hear someone say, “I vividly imagine his posture,” or “his voice” – but never: “I imagine the sensation of this hand movement”? And why not? Does one imagine it and just not say it?

### <span class="fac">[207\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/207.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1052.</span> What should we answer if someone says to us: “If you guide a person’s hand during a movement (e.g.), then you are showing him a certain kinesthetic sensation, which he then reproduces when he repeats the movement on command”? And can one say that he could be guided in this way by the visual image of the movement, but not by a kinesthetic image?

### <span class="fac">[207\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/207.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1053.</span> How important is it that there is a pictorial representation of visual movement, and nothing corresponding to it for ‘kinesthetic movement’? “Make a movement that looks like _this_!” – “Make a movement that produces _this_ sound!” – Make a movement that produces _this_ kinesthetic feeling!” In this case, to correctly copy the kinesthetic feeling would mean to correctly repeat the movement according to its _appearance_.

### <span class="fac">[207\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/207.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1054.</span> Imagine the movement to be very painful, so that the pain overwhelms any other slight sensation in that place.

### <span class="fac">[207\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/207.webp),[208\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/208.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1055.</span> Make a movement (like when playing the piano) with your fingers; repeat it, but with a smaller range of motion. Do you remember which of the two feelings you had yesterday during the first movement? One might say: “No, this movement looked a little different yesterday” – but also: The movement is not quite the same – I didn’t have exactly this kinesthetic sensation.”

### <span class="fac">[208\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/208.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1056.</span> Because we naturally have feelings of movement and we can also reproduce them. Especially when we repeat a movement under the same conditions, after only short pauses. One also localizes the sensations, but almost never in the joints, mostly in the skin. (Puff out your cheeks! where do you do it, and where do you feel it?)

### <span class="fac">[208\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/208.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1057.</span> One could really compare the growth of the analysis with the growth of a seed. And in this case, to say “It was all already in the sensation,” or “it grew out of it like from a seed,” amounts to the same thing. How much truth is there in the fact that one sometimes reproduces an arm movement (for example) from a visual image, but not from a kinesthetic image?

### <span class="fac">[208\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/208.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1058.</span> Does one actually sometimes guide the arm according to a visual image? I can only say: If I did not _see_ that my arm moved after, with my face turned away, I was convinced that I had moved it, I would be confused and would probably trust my _eyes_. Seeing can certainly _teach_ me whether I executed the intended movement accurately, e.g., whether I reached the position I wanted to reach; feeling could not do that. I do feel that I am moving, and I can also judge, approximately according to the feeling, _how_; but I simply _know_ which movement I made, without it being possible to speak of a _sense-datum_ of the movement, of an immediate inner image of the movement. And when I say “I simply _know_…”, here “knowing” means something like “being able to say” and is not again a kind of inner image.

### <span class="fac">[208\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/208.webp),[209\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/209.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1059.</span> “In order to be able to say that feeling teaches me where my arm is now, or how far I am moving it, one would have to have assigned feelings and movements to each other. One would have to be able to say: ‘If I have this feeling…, then, according to experience, my arm is _there_’. Or also: One would have to have a criterion for the identity of the feelings in addition to the one for the executed movement.” But is this condition, if it has any sense at all, fulfilled for _seeing_? Well, one can represent a visual image, e.g., graphically. But one cannot give someone, or oneself, the feeling that is supposed to be characteristic of bending the arm by 30˚, without actually bending the arm. Bend your arm a little! What do you feel? – A tension, or something similar, here and there, and mainly the rubbing of my sleeve. – Do it again! Was the feeling the same? _Approximately_. Approximately at the same places. Does _this_ feeling always accompany this movement, can you say? No. And yet, something about this argument still doesn’t quite fit.

### <span class="fac">[209\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/209.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1060.</span> Imagine that certain movements produce sounds and one says that one can recognize how far we have moved the arm from the sound that is produced. That would be possible. (Playing a scale on the piano.) But what prerequisites would have to be met for this? For example, it would not be enough that sounds accompany the movements; nor would it be enough that they are similar for similar movements. It would not be enough to say: the sound _must_ indeed have the same quality for the same movements, since it is the only sense-datum by which we can recognize the size of the movement.

### <span class="fac">[209\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/209.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1061.</span> But isn’t there a kind of private ostensive definition for feelings of movement and the like? For example, I bend a finger and memorize the sensation. Someone now says to me, “I will produce certain sensations in your finger in the same way, but without it moving; tell me when it is _the_ one that you have when you bend your finger.” Could I not, for my own use, call this sensation “E,” use my memory as a criterion for identity, and now say “Yes, that is E again,” etc.?

### <span class="fac">[209\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/209.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1062.</span> It would then also be conceivable that I recognize the sensation and that it occurs _without_ the accompaniment of the conviction: that the movement has taken place – without the sense of movement.

### <span class="fac">[209\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/209.webp),[210\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/210.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1063.</span> I can certainly, for example, lift my knee several times in a row and say that I had the same sensation each time: not as if I had this sensation _every_ time I lift my knee, nor as if I could recognize the movement from the sensation, but only: I had the same sensation, produced by the movement, three times in this series of knee movements. “The same” here, of course, means the same as “seemingly the same.”

### <span class="fac">[210\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/210.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1064.</span> “I have had the same sensation three times” describes a process in my private world. But how does the other person know what I mean? What do I mean by “the same” in such a case? Does he rely on the fact that I use the word here as I always do? But what, in this case, is the usual, _analogous_ use? No, this difficulty is not a mere artificiality; he does not _really_ know, cannot know, what the same objects are in this case.

### <span class="fac">[210\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/210.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1065.</span> The example of the motor roller with the motor inside the roller is in reality much better and deeper than I have explained. For, when someone presented me with the design, I immediately saw that it could not function, because one could roll the roller from the outside, even if the ‘motor’ was not in operation; but I did not see that it was a rigid construction and not a machine at all. And here there is a close analogy with the case of the private ostensive definition. For also in that case there is, as it were, a direct and an indirect way of recognizing the impossibility.

### <span class="fac">[210\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/210.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1066.</span> I named this sensation of movement with “E”. For the other, it is now the one that I had during this movement. But for me? Does “E” now mean something different? – Well, for me it means _this_ sensation. – But which one is this? because I pointed to my sensation a minute ago – how can I now point to _it_ again?

### <span class="fac">[210\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/210.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1067.</span> → But suppose someone makes a series of arm movements and says, “The sensation I am having in my leg now, I will call ‘E<sub>1</sub>’”, etc. Later, on various occasions, he says: “Now I am having E<sub>3</sub>”. Etc. – Such utterances could be important; if, for example, we observe certain physiological correlates to the sensations and can thus draw conclusions from his utterances.

### <span class="fac">[210\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/210.webp),[211\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/211.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1068.</span> If it is true that we do not judge the type and size of a limb’s movement by _feeling_, how would a person differ from us, in whom this is the case? Well, one could easily imagine that someone might have different, or differently intense, sensations of pain with different movements. He might, for example, say: “I feel this stinging sensation when I bend my arm at about 90˚.”

### <span class="fac">[211\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/211.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1069.</span> Imagine someone who, using a dowsing rod, can determine the depth of a spring by the pull it exerts. He has learned _this_ in the following way: He has walked over springs of various depths and has memorized the pull. (This could have been determined with a spring scale.) He has associated the pull with the depth and now infers the depth from the pull. This might happen in such a way that he indicates the pull – perhaps in kg – and then moves on to the depth, perhaps even using a table. But it is also possible that he knows no other measure of the pull than the depth of the spring. After some practice, he can correctly state the depth. If a pull is exerted on the rod by weights, he will now also say, “This pulls like a spring of such and such a depth.”

### <span class="fac">[211\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/211.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1070.</span> It could, however, be that he is able to correctly state the depth of a spring by the pull of the rod, but not to correctly estimate the pull of the rod. I mean this: it could be that water at different depths under different conditions exerts the same pull; and this dowser now says, for example: “This spring is deeper than the previous one, it pulls less” – and he is right: the spring is indeed deeper, but the pull, measured with the spring scale, was the same, and he had not correctly remembered _it_. – – Should I then say in this case that he judges the depth by the pull?

### <span class="fac">[211\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/211.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1071.</span> He will perhaps say: “This move is that of a source in the depth ‥․”, by, as it were, studying this move – like one weighs a weight in one’s hand. Perhaps, however, he says, “This move I cannot judge – the water is in the depth ‥․” In this (latter) case, one would not say that he judges the depth by the move. (At least not ‘consciously’).

### <span class="fac">[211\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/211.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1072.</span> Suppose someone says that he judges how far he has bent his arm by the strength of a pressure _sensation_ in his elbow. That is to say: when it reaches a certain strength, he recognizes from this that the arm is bent to _that_ degree. Or what else should it mean: that he judges the degree of bending according to the degree of the pressure _sensation_?

### <span class="fac">[211\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/211.webp),[212\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/212.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1073.</span> I mean: how does one know that he is judging something according to _this_ _sensation_? – Is it enough for this that he directs his attention to the _sensation_ when making the estimation?

### <span class="fac">[212\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/212.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1074.</span> If you now say that it is necessary for this that someone can state: “If the pressure is _so_ strong, then my arm is bent by 90˚” – then the ‘_So_’ must allow the strength to be specified. Otherwise, that one judges the bending according to the pressure _sensation_ means at most that one cannot judge the bending if one feels _no_ (or only a very weak) pressure _sensation_. (That is, for example, if one is anesthetized.)

### <span class="fac">[212\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/212.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1075.</span> So there are different cases. One might _say_ that one assesses the bending according to the sensation of pressure or pain, and in doing so, so to speak, listens to this sensation; but otherwise, one may not be able to indicate the degree of the sensation in any way. – Or there may be two independent indications of the degree of the sensation and the bending.

### <span class="fac">[212\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/212.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1076.</span> “If I feel the pressure _so_ strongly, then….” – Doesn’t that make any sense? Someone might even say that they have an entire scale of pressure sensations. I can probably imagine that. Only it would be just as little a _real_ scale as a picture of a thermometer is a thermometer. Although in some respects it has a great similarity to it.

### <span class="fac">[212\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/212.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1077.</span> I give the rules of a game. The other person, in complete accordance with these rules, makes a move that I had not foreseen and that disrupts the game, just as I intended. I must now say: “I gave bad rules”; I must change my rules, or perhaps supplement them. So, did I already have a picture of the game in mind? In a certain sense: yes! It was, for example, possible that I did not foresee that a quadratic equation does not have to have real solutions. The rule thus leads me to something about which I say: “I did not expect this picture; I always imagined the solution _so_: ‥‥”

### <span class="fac">[212\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/212.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1078.</span> What if one were to say: “Not every system of _rules_ determines a _calculus_”? As an example, one could cite division by 0. Let us imagine an arithmetic in which it would be allowed, and therefore it could be proven that every number is equal to every other number.

### <span class="fac">[212\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/212.webp),[213\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/213.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1079.</span> When children play trains, – shall I say, when one child imitates the locomotive, is he seen as a locomotive by another? In the game, it is _understood_ as a locomotive. Imagine I showed an adult the shape 🖵 and asked, “What does it remind you of,” and he answered, “A locomotive” – does that mean he _saw_ it as a locomotive? I take this as the typical game of “seeing something as something” when someone says, “Now I see it as this, now as that.” When he knows different aspects and these are _independent_ of any use of the thing being looked at. I would like to say: I do not regard the use of the image as a sign that it is seen in this way or that way.

### <span class="fac">[213\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/213.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1080.</span> Would a child understand what it means to _see_ the table ‘as a table’? It learns: “This is a table, this is a bench” etc., and it completely masters a language-game, without any indication that this involves an _aspect_.

### <span class="fac">[213\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/213.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1081.</span> “Yes, a child does not analyze what it is doing.” – Once again: we are not talking about an analysis of what is happening, but rather an analysis – and this word is very misleading – of our concepts. And our concepts are more complicated than those of the child; inasmuch as our words have a more complicated use than his.

### <span class="fac">[213\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/213.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1082.</span> “But I _do_ see it this way, even when I am not expressing it.” This would mean that what I see does not change when I express it. If one were to ask: “Does a body have this weight only as long as it is being weighed?” – this would mean: “Does a weight change when we put it on the scale?” And that is, of course, not what we want to ask.

### <span class="fac">[213\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/213.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1083.</span> It is only through the phenomenon of the change of aspect that the aspect seems to be separated from the _rest of seeing_ and _to become_ something else. It is as if, after the experience of the aspect-switch, one could say: “There was, therefore, an aspect!”

### <span class="fac">[213\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/213.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1084.</span> If one scrapes away the _coating_ of a thing, one can say “So there was a coating on it” – – but if the color of an object changes, can I say “So it had a _color_!” – as if I had only just noticed this? Can one say: It only came to my consciousness that the thing had a color when the color changed?

### <span class="fac">[213\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/213.webp),[214\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/214.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1085.</span> Don't think that it is something _strange_ that you see a picture on the wall spatially. It is – I would like to say – as ordinary as it seems. (And this could be said of many things).

### <span class="fac">[214\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/214.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1086.</span> Imagine that the _things_ in our environment – table, books, chairs, etc. – periodically and abruptly changed their colors; their shapes remained the same. Could one then say that we only become conscious of color as a special component of our visual _experience_?

### <span class="fac">[214\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/214.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1087.</span> If I compare field flowers and garden flowers, I can become conscious of the difference in their _character_; but that does not mean that I perceived their _character_ earlier, or that I must have perceived them in some _character_ or other.

### <span class="fac">[214\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/214.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1088.</span> Must I really know that I see with two eyes? Certainly not. Do I perhaps have _two_ visual impressions when I see normally, so that I notice that my three-dimensional visual impression is composed of two visual images? Certainly not. – I cannot, therefore, separate three-dimensionality from seeing.

### <span class="fac">[214\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/214.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1089.</span> If I ask someone “In which direction does an ‘F’ and in which direction does an ‘I’ face for you?” and he answers that an F always faces to the right for him, and an I to the left, – that certainly does not mean that he has a sensation of direction every time he looks at an F. This becomes clearer if one asks _like this_: “Where would you paint an eye and a nose on an F?” – But if one were to say: “So it only faces in this direction for you as long as you think or say this” – is that not the same as asking: “Would you paint the nose on the F in that direction if you were to paint it?”

### <span class="fac">[214\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/214.webp),[215\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/215.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1090.</span> Do I always see a face ‘_as a face_’? I have books in front of me: do I see them all the time ‘_as books_’? Do I mean: do I see them all the time as books, when I am not just seeing them as something else? Or do I often, or usually, only see colors and shapes, without any particular aspect? (apparently not!) We say to someone: “If _this_ is the base, then _this_ is the apex and _this_ is the height.” Or he must answer the question: “Which is the height of the triangle if _this_ is the base?” But we are not insisting that he _sees_ the triangle in this way. – One sometimes says “Imagine it turned over!” (or something similar), and one could also say “See it turned over,” and this remark might help; in the same way that a graphic addition to the image might help, suggesting this aspect.

### <span class="fac">[215\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/215.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1091.</span> Can I, for example, say: I see the armchair as an _object_, as a _unity_? Just as I say, I now see the black cross on a white background, but now the white cross on a black background? If one asks me, “What do you have in front of you?” will I of course answer “An armchair,” and thus treat it as a unity. But can one now say that I _see_ it _as a unity_? And can I look at the cross-shaped figure without seeing it _in one way or another_?

### <span class="fac">[215\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/215.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1092.</span> If I ask someone, “What do you see in front of you?” and he says, “What I have in front of me looks _like this_,” and now he draws the cross-shaped figure, – does he _have to_ have seen it in some aspect? Has he not seen it if he can only describe it graphically?

### <span class="fac">[215\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/215.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1093.</span> Can a child communicate to you that it sees in three dimensions? And imagine it tells you, “I see everything flat,” – what would that tell you? It could see everything _flat_, and through an intuition know that it is not _flat_, and behave accordingly!

### <span class="fac">[215\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/215.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1094.</span> If the child regards this picture as this and that, and I conclude, “So it _sees_ the picture _in this way_,” – what kind of conclusion am I drawing? What does this conclusion _tell_ me? One might say that I am inferring the kind of sense-datum, or visual image; as if the conclusion were: “So the picture is _like this_ in its mind”; and now one would have to represent it plastically.

### <span class="fac">[215\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/215.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1095.</span> Is it _like this_: “I have always read the sign ‘🖵’ as a sigma; now someone tells me that it could also be an inverted M, and I can now see it _like that_; – therefore, I always saw it as a sigma before”? So, I mean, I didn’t just see the figure 🖵 and read it _in that way_, but I also saw it _as that_!

### <span class="fac">[215\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/215.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1096.</span> “But how could I know that I would have reacted in that way if you had asked me?” – How? There is no how. But there are signs that I am right to say that.

### <span class="fac">[215\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/215.webp),[216\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/216.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1097.</span> I want to describe what I see; I create a transparency for that purpose. But now I am asked, “Is _this_ in front and _this_ behind?” So I describe by words, or by a model, what I see in front, what I see behind. And now I am asked, “And do you see _this_ point as the apex of the triangle?” and I must answer that too. – But must I have an answer to that? – Suppose that it is not true that the viewing direction determines the aspect. And in _one_ case, my gaze is always directed at the same point of the image, in another case it moves regularly according to a simple rule, and in a third case it wanders randomly across the object. If, instead of a description of the aspect, we put the description of the viewing direction, would it be a description to say that the viewing direction is random or undefined? And that might even be the usual case. – So the answer to the question “Did you see this point as the apex of the triangle?” can be “I cannot name any particular aspect,” or perhaps “I certainly didn’t see it _like that_.”

### <span class="fac">[216\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/216.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1098.</span> What, incidentally, did the hypothesis concerning the importance of the direction of gaze do for us? – It provided us with a picture of a specific manifold.

### <span class="fac">[216\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/216.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1099.</span> Actually, such a theory is the construction of a psychological model of a psychological phenomenon, & therefore a psychological model. The theory essentially says: “It could be _like this_: ‥‥.” & the benefit of the theory is that it illustrates a concept. However, it can illustrate it better or worse; more or less accurately. The theory is, therefore, in a sense, a notation for this psychological phenomenon.

### <span class="fac">[216\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/216.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2000. 1100.</span> So, if we ‘drop the explanation’ – if we say that, after all, the _explanation_ is of no consequence to us – then a grammatical observation remains. It concerns the use of the statement “I now see a certain facial expression in the picture.”

### <span class="fac">[216\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/216.webp),[217\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/217.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2001. 1101.</span> Does the subject point to nothing other than itself? Oh, yes! But that means: – the impression it makes on me is connected with things in its environment – e.g., with the existence of our language & its intonation, which means, with the whole field of our language-games. If, for example, I say: It is as if a conclusion were being drawn here, or as if something were being affirmed, or as if _this_ were an answer to what came before, – then my understanding presupposes familiarity with conclusions, affirmations, answers.

### <span class="fac">[217\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/217.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2002. 1102.</span> A topic does not have any less of a facial expression than a face.

### <span class="fac">[217\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/217.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2003. 1103.</span> “Repetition is _necessary_.” In what respect is it necessary? Well, sing it, and you will see that it is only through repetition that it gains its _great_ power. – Do we not feel that here there must exist a model for the theme in reality, and that the theme only comes close to it, only corresponds to it, when this part is repeated? Or should I say the foolish thing: “It simply sounds better with the repetition”? And yet there is no paradigm outside of the theme. And yet there is again a paradigm outside of the theme: namely, the rhythm of our language, our thinking, and feeling. And the theme is again a _new_ part of our language; it is incorporated into it; we learn a new _gesture_.

### <span class="fac">[217\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/217.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2005. 1105.</span> The topic is in interaction with language.

### <span class="fac">[217\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/217.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2004. 1104.</span> “An entire world of pain lies in these words.” How _can_ it lie in them? – It is connected with them. The words are like the acorn from which an _oak tree_ can grow. But where is the law laid down according to which the tree grows from the acorn? Well, the picture is, through experience, incorporated into our thinking.

### <span class="fac">[217\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/217.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2006. 1106.</span> “Where do you feel sorrow?” – In the mind. – – And if I had to indicate a place, I would point to the stomach area. In the case of love, to the chest, and in the case of an idea, to the head.

### <span class="fac">[217\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/217.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2007. 1107.</span> “Where do you feel the sorrow?” – In the mind. – – What on earth does that mean? – – What consequences do we draw from this localization? One is that we are not talking about a physical location of sorrow. But we do point to our body, as if the sorrow were in it. Is this because we feel a physical discomfort? I do not know the cause. But why should I assume that it is a bodily discomfort?
