### <span class="fac">[217\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/217.webp),[218\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/218.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2008. 1108.</span> Imagine the following question: Can one think of a pain, perhaps of the quality of rheumatic pain, but without any location? Can one _imagine_ it? If you begin to think about this, you will see how much you want to transform the knowledge of the location of the pain into a characteristic of the _felt_ sensation, into a characteristic of a sense-datum, of the private object that stands before my mind.

### <span class="fac">[218\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/218.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2009. 1109.</span> I say, to the sorrowful person, the whole world seems gray. – But what would be before his mind would not be sorrow, but a gray world; as if it were the cause of the sorrow.

### <span class="fac">[218\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/218.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2010. 1110.</span> To perceive something as a difference in color – and on the other hand, as a shadow with the same color. I ask, “Have you perceived the color of the table in front of you, which you have been looking at the whole time?” He says, “Yes.” But he could have described the table as “brown,” and he did not notice that the green curtain is reflected in its shiny surface. – Did he not have the green visual impression? “Is the wall in front of you uniformly yellow?” – “Yes.” But it is partly in shadow and looks almost gray. What did the person who looked at the wall see? Should I say, a uniformly yellow surface, which is of course irregularly shaded? Or: yellow and gray patches?

### <span class="fac">[218\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/218.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2011. 1111.</span> It is a remarkable fact that we are hardly ever conscious of the vagueness of the periphery of our field of vision. When people, for example, talk about a visual image, they mostly do not think of it; and when one speaks of a representation of the visual impression through a picture, one sees no difficulty in this. This is very important.

### <span class="fac">[218\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/218.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2012. 1112.</span> “What I perceive is _**this**_ –” and then follows a form of _**description**_. One could also explain this as follows: Let us imagine a direct transmission of the experience! – But what is our criterion for the fact that the experience has really been transmitted? “Well, he simply has the same thing that I have.” – But how does _he_ have it?

### <span class="fac">[218\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/218.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2013. 1113.</span> Think of the diversity of physical experiments. We measure, for example, the temperature; but only in a certain general technique is _this_ experiment a measurement of the temperature. – If we were interested in the diversity of (physical) measurements, that is, of the types of measurement, then we are interested in the diversity of methods, of concepts.

### <span class="fac">[218\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/218.webp),[219\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/219.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2014. 1114.</span> How can you _contemplate_ sorrow? By _being_ sorrowful? By not letting anything distract you from your sorrow? So, are you observing the feeling by _having_ it? And if you keep every distraction away, are you then observing precisely _this_ state? or the other, which you were in _before_ the observation. Are you then observing your observing?

### <span class="fac">[219\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/219.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2015. 1115.</span> Imagine someone asked, “What is measured in physics?” One could list: lengths, times, light intensities, weights, etc. But could one not say: you learn more if you ask, “How is it measured?”, instead of “What is measured?” If one does _this_, if one measures _this_ way, then one measures the temperature; if one does that, then one measures _this_ way: an electric current.

### <span class="fac">[219\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/219.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2016. 1116.</span> But is sorrow not composed of all sorts of feelings? Is it not a conglomerate of feelings? Could one say that it consists of the feelings A, B, C, etc. – as granite consists of feldspar, mica, and quartz? – So do I say of someone that he is sorrowful, that he has the feelings ‥‥? And how do I know that he has them? Does he tell us?

### <span class="fac">[219\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/219.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2017. 1117.</span> Sorrow is, after all, a mental experience. One says that one experiences sorrow, joy, disappointment. And then these experiences seem to be really composed and distributed over the whole body. The sigh of relief of joy, the laughter, the cheering, the thoughts of happiness – is not the experience of all this the joy? Do I know that he is happy because he tells me that he feels his laughter, feels and hears his cheering, etc. – or because he laughs and cheers? Do _I_ say “I am happy” because I feel all of that?

### <span class="fac">[219\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/219.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2018. 1118.</span> The words “I am happy” are a joy-behaviour.

### <span class="fac">[219\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/219.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2019. 1119.</span> And how is it that I – as James says – have a feeling of joy when I merely make a joyful face; a feeling of sorrow when I make a sorrowful face? That I can thus evoke these feelings by imitating their external expression? Does that show that the muscular sensations are the sorrow, or a part of the sorrow?

### <span class="fac">[219\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/219.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1120.</span> Suppose someone says: “Raise your arm, and you will feel that you are raising your arm.” Is that a statement of experience? And is it one when one says, “Make a sad face and you will feel sad”? Or did it mean: “Feel that you are making a sad face and you will feel sadness”? And is that a pleonasm?

### <span class="fac">[219\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/219.webp),[220\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1121.</span> Suppose I say: “Yes, it is true: when I make a friendlier face, I feel better right away.” – Is that because the feelings in the face are more pleasant? Or because making this face has consequences? (One says “Keep your chin up!”)

### <span class="fac">[220\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1122.</span> Does one say: “I feel much better now: the feeling in the facial muscles and around the corners of the mouth is good”? And why does that sound absurd, except perhaps if one previously had pain in those parts?

### <span class="fac">[220\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1123.</span> Does one compare, in the same way, my feeling in the corners of my mouth and his – and my state of mind and his? How do I, for example, compare my pressure sensations with his? How do I _learn_ to compare them? How do I compare our kinesthetic sensations, how do I relate them to each other? And how about the feelings of sadness, joy, etc.?

### <span class="fac">[220\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1124.</span> Now, granted – although it is highly doubtful – that the muscular sensation of smiling is a component of the feeling of happiness; – but what about the other components? – Well, in the chest, in the stomach, etc.! – But do you really feel them, or do you just conclude that they _must_ be there? Are you really conscious of these localized feelings? – And if not, – why should they be there at all? Why should you _mean_ them when you say you feel happy?

### <span class="fac">[220\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1125.</span> What would have to be established through an act of _looking_, you certainly did not mean that. That is how “sadness,” “joy,” etc. are _not_ used.

### <span class="fac">[220\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1126.</span> Why does it sound strange: “He felt deep sorrow for a second”? Because that happens so rarely? And what if we thought of people who have this experience often? Or those who often experience alternating moments of deep sorrow and intense happiness for hours.

### <span class="fac">[220\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1127.</span> “Do you not _now_ feel the sorrow…” – is that like asking: “Are you not _now_ playing chess?” In reality, however, the question was personal and temporal, not philosophical.

### <span class="fac">[220\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1128.</span> “‘I hope ‥‥’ – the description of my state of mind”: That sounds as if I were looking at my soul and describing it (as one describes a landscape). If now I say: “I keep hoping that he will come to me” – is that a hopeful behavior? Is it not just as little a hopeful behavior as the words: “I hoped then that he would come”? – Should I not then say that there are two types of the present tense of “hoping”? One, as it were, an exclamation, the other a report?

<span class="series-number">1129.</span> But if I now say to someone, “I very much hope that he will come to our meeting” – does he ask me: “What was that: a report, or an exclamation?” – Does he not understand me if he does not know that? ### <span class="fac">[221\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/221.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1130.</span> “I hope he will come” – one could say – sometimes means as much as the exclamation “He will come!”, spoken in a hopeful tone. In which, when people want to talk about past hope, they quote themselves; for example, saying: “I said, ‘He will certainly come!’”

### <span class="fac">[221\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/221.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1131.</span> One could say: _The_ statement says something about the state of mind, from which one can infer the state of mind. (This sounds sillier than it is.) (“Off with his head!”)

### <span class="fac">[221\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/221.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1132.</span> Is the cry “Help!” a description of my state of mind? And is it _not_ the expression of a wish? Is it not so much that, as anything else?

### <span class="fac">[221\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/221.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1133.</span> I say to myself: “I hope, and I still hope, although ‥‥” – and I shake my head as if in disapproval of myself. This means something quite different from simply “I hope ‥‥!” (The difference in English between “I am hoping” and “I hope.”)

### <span class="fac">[221\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/221.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1134.</span> And what does the one who observes his own hope observe? What would he _report_? Various things. “I hoped daily, ‥‥ I imagined ‥‥ I said to myself every day ‥‥ I sighed ‥‥ I walked this path every day, in the hope ‥‥”

### <span class="fac">[221\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/221.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1135.</span> The word “observe” is poorly placed here. I am trying to remember this and that.

### <span class="fac">[221\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/221.webp),[222\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/222.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1136.</span> The one who remembers his hope, by the way, is not remembering a behaviour, nor necessarily thoughts. He says – he knows – that he hoped at that time.

### <span class="fac">[222\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/222.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1137.</span> The sentence “I want to drink wine” has approximately the same sense as “Wine, please!”. No one will call _this_ a description; but from it one can infer that the person saying it is eager to drink wine, that he can at any moment resort to violence if his wish is denied – and this one will call an inference about his state of mind.

### <span class="fac">[222\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/222.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1138.</span> Is “I believe…” a description of my state of mind?? – Well, what _is_ such a description? Something like: “I am sad”, “I am in a good mood”, perhaps “I am in pain”.

### <span class="fac">[222\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/222.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1139.</span> It would be disastrous to take Moore’s paradox to be something that can only occur in the realm of the mental.

### <span class="fac">[222\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/222.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1140.</span> I want to say first that with the statement “It will rain,” one expresses the belief in it just as much as with the words “Wine, please!” one expresses the wish to have wine. One could also say that “I believe p” means approximately the same as “p”; and that the fact that in the first sentence there is the verb “believe” and the pronoun “I” must not mislead us. We can clearly see from this only that the grammar of “I believe” is very different from that of “I write”. But if I say that, I am not saying that there may not also be great similarities; and I am not saying what _kind_ the differences are. ((real and imaginary unit.)) Note that we are dealing with similarities and differences of concepts, not of phenomena.

### <span class="fac">[222\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/222.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1141.</span> One can say the odd thing: “I believe it will rain” means something similar to “It will rain,” but “I used to believe it would rain” does not mean something similar to “It used to rain.” But what does it mean that the first sentence has approximately the same sense as the second? Does it mean that both evoke the same thought in my mind? (the same _feeling_?) –

### <span class="fac">[222\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/222.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1142.</span> “I want to think _this_ way, and not _that_ way.” And ‘_this_’ and ‘_that_’ are, however strange it may sound, not sharply separated from each other.

### <span class="fac">[222\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/222.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1143.</span> How you use the word “God” shows not _whom_ you mean, but what you mean.

### <span class="fac">[222\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/222.webp),[223\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/223.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1144.</span> “But it must mean that ‘I believed’ in the past means _that_, just as ‘I believe’ in the present means!” It must mean that i is for ‒ 1 what √1 is for 1! That means nothing.

### <span class="fac">[223\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/223.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1145.</span> What does it mean that “I believe p” says approximately the same as ‘P’? If someone says the first and second sentence, we react in approximately the same way; if I say the first sentence and someone doesn’t understand the words “I believe,” I would repeat the sentence in the second form, etc. Just as I would explain “I wish you would go there” with “Go there!”

### <span class="fac">[223\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/223.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1146.</span> Moore’s paradox can be expressed _this_ way: “I believe p” says approximately the same as “P”; but “Suppose I believe p…” does not say approximately the same as “Suppose p…” Can one understand the assumption that I wish for something before understanding the utterance of the wish? – The child first learns to express the wish, and only later learns to assume that it wishes this and that.

### <span class="fac">[223\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/223.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1147.</span> “Suppose I am in pain…” this is not an utterance of pain and therefore not pain-behaviour. The child that learns the word “pain” as an exclamation, that then begins to talk about a past pain, – it can one day say “If I am in pain, the doctor comes.” Has the word “pain” changed its meaning in this process of learning? It has changed its use; but one must be very careful not to interpret this change as a change of the object that now corresponds to the word.

### <span class="fac">[223\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/223.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1148.</span> Imagine “I believe…” depicted through a painting. How could I imagine that? The picture would show me and some image in my head. It doesn’t matter what symbolism it uses. The image of _what_ I believe, e.g., that it is raining – will be in it. My soul will perhaps grasp this image, hold onto it, and so on. – And now let us assume that this image is used as the assertion “It is raining.” Well, there is nothing strange about that. Should I say that there is now much _superfluous_ in the picture? I would not like to say that.

### <span class="fac">[223\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/223.webp),[224\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/224.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1149.</span> “In essence, with these words I describe my own state of mind, – but this description is here indirectly an assertion of the believed fact itself.” – – As I, under certain circumstances, describe a photograph in order to describe what the photograph is a depiction of.

### <span class="fac">[224\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/224.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1150.</span> But if this analogy holds, I must still be able to say that this photograph (the impression on my mind) is reliable. I must therefore be able to say: “I believe that it will rain, and my belief is reliable, so I rely on it.” As if my belief were a kind of sense impression.

### <span class="fac">[224\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/224.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1151.</span> Do you mean to say: “I believe it, and since I am reliable, it will probably be so”? That would be as if one said: “I believe it – therefore, I believe it.”

### <span class="fac">[224\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/224.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1152.</span> Just as, by means of the same activity, one can measure the length of the table, test the standard, or examine the person measuring for their accuracy in measuring, so an assertion can serve me to inform myself about its content, or about the character, or the state of mind of the one asserting.

### <span class="fac">[224\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/224.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1153.</span> One might well say: “He’s coming, but I still can’t believe it!” – “He’s coming! I can’t believe it!”

### <span class="fac">[224\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/224.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1154.</span> Imagine an announcer at a station who, according to plan, announces an arriving train, but – perhaps without reason – is convinced that it will not arrive. He could announce: “Train No. ‥‥ will arrive at ‥‥ o’clock. Personally, I don’t believe it.”

### <span class="fac">[224\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/224.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1155.</span> What if a soldier made military reports that, based on observations, would be justified, but added that he believed them to be incorrect? – Let us not ask what is going on in the mind of the person speaking, but whether others can make something of this report, and what.

### <span class="fac">[224\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/224.webp),[225\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/225.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1156.</span> The report is a language-game with these words. It would create confusion if we said: the words of the report, the reported sentence, have a certain meaning, and that the reporting, the ‘assertion,’ adds something to this. As if the sentence, uttered by a gramophone, belonged to pure logic, as if it had a purely logical meaning here, as if we had the object before us that the logician can take in hand and examine – while the asserted, reported sentence is the thing _in use_. As one can say: the botanist considers a rose _as a plant_, not as an adornment for a dress or room, or as a delicate gesture. The sentence, I want to say, has no meaning outside of the language-game. This is connected with the fact that it is not a kind of _name_. So that one could say: “‘I believe ‥․’ – that is _so_” whereby one (in oneself) points to what gives the sentence its meaning.

### <span class="fac">[225\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/225.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1157.</span> Is it a tautology to report: “The cavalry will arrive immediately; and I believe it”?

### <span class="fac">[225\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/225.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1158.</span> The paradox is this: the _assumption_ can be expressed as: “Suppose that _this_ is the case in me and _that_ outside of me” – – but the _assertion_, that _this_ is the case in me, _says_: that _that_ is the case outside of me. In the _assumption_, the two sentences about the inner and the outer are quite independent; in the assertion, however, they are not.

### <span class="fac">[225\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/225.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1159.</span> Is this in the nature of the concept “believe”? Certainly.

### <span class="fac">[225\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/225.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1160.</span> Imagine someone saying “I wish,” – but does not want his wish to be fulfilled. – (Lessing: “If God had in his right hand ‥‥”) Can one therefore ask God to grant the wish, and not to fulfill it?

### <span class="fac">[225\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/225.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1161.</span> It seems, then, as if the assertion “I believe ‥․” is not the assertion of what the assumption “I believe” assumes!

### <span class="fac">[225\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/225.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1162.</span> Do not take it for granted, but as something very remarkable, that the verbs “believe,” “hope,” “wish,” “intend,” etc., all have the grammatical forms that “eat,” “speak,” “cut” also have.

### <span class="fac">[225\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/225.webp),[226\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/226.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1163.</span> Suppose I were the being that could utter, “I do not believe that it is raining; and it is raining.” – But what is the use of these words? What use do I imagine is made of them? “He is coming. I don’t believe it personally, but don’t let that put you off.” – “He is coming, rely on it. _I_ don’t believe it; but don’t let that put you off.” This sounds as if two people were speaking from within me; or as if one instance within me communicated to the other that he was coming, and this instance wished the other to act accordingly, – while another instance, in a certain sense, announced my own behaviour. It is as if one said: “I know that this way of acting is wrong, but I know that I will act in this way.” “He is coming, but I don’t believe it” can therefore occur in a language-game. Or, better: one can imagine a language-game in which these words would not seem absurd to us.

### <span class="fac">[226\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/226.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1164.</span> Instead of displaying the voltage by means of a pointer and dial, a voltmeter could express it with the help of a gramophone record. The instrument might say, when one presses a button (it asks), “The voltage is ‥‥” Could it also make sense for the voltmeter to say: “I believe the voltage is ‥‥”? – One can certainly imagine such a case. Should I say that the instrument is making a statement about itself, or about the voltage? Should I say that the instrument always makes a statement about itself? And if it can, for example, repeat a higher reading of the voltage: did it _believe_ that the voltage was ‥‥?

### <span class="fac">[226\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/226.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1165.</span> Or let us put it this way: should I say that a voltmeter indicates something about itself, or the voltage? Can I not say both? Namely, each under different circumstances?

### <span class="fac">[226\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/226.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1166.</span> Do “Help!” and “I need help” have different sense? Is it merely a crudeness of our understanding that we regard them as equivalent? Does it always mean saying something: “Strictly speaking, what I meant was not ‘Help!’, but ‘I want help.’” The worst enemy of our understanding here is the idea, the picture, of a ‘sense’ of what we say in our mind.

### <span class="fac">[226\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/226.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1167.</span> The assertion “He will come” does not refer to the person making the assertion. But neither does it refer to the words of the assertion, whereas “‘he will come’ is a true sentence” refers to the words and has the same sense as the sentence that does not.

### <span class="fac">[226\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/226.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1168.</span> Could one speak of the sense of the words “that he will come”? For these words are, in fact, Frege’s ‘assumption’. Well, could I not explain to someone what this word expression means? Yes, in that I explain or show how it is used.

### <span class="fac">[226\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/226.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1169.</span> The difficulty becomes insurmountable if you think that the sentence “I believe ‥‥” says something about the state of my mind. If that were so, one would have to be able to reproduce Moore’s paradox, if one made a statement about the state of the brain, for example, instead of the state of one’s own mind. The point, however, is that no assertion about the state of my brain (or anyone else’s) is equivalent to the assertion that I believe – “He will come,” for example.

### <span class="fac">[227\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/227.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1170.</span> But let us nevertheless regard the assertion “He believes p” as a statement about his _state_, from which it at least follows how he will behave under given circumstances! Is there then no _first person singular present_ for such a statement? Can I not then say of myself that I am now in a state in which certain linguistic and other reactions are likely? It is certainly similar when I say, “I am very irritable now.” Similarly, I could also say, “I now believe any bad news very easily.”

### <span class="fac">[227\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/227.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1171.</span> Would a sentence, which asserts that I – or my brain – am now in such a state that I would answer the question “Will he come?” with “Yes,” and exhibit this and those other reactions – would such a sentence be equivalent to the assertion “He will come”? One could ask here: “How do you imagine that I am informed about this state of mine? – Perhaps through experience? Do I want to predict, from experience, that I will always answer such a question in this way, etc.?” If it is so, and if I make the statement “I believe he will come” in this sense and add “and he will not come,” then this is only a contradiction in the same way as, for example, this is a contradiction: “I cannot pronounce a four-syllable word,” or this: “I cannot say a single German sentence.” If the latter is a kind of contradiction, then it is not the assumption: “Suppose I could not say a single German sentence.”

### <span class="fac">[227\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/227.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1172.</span> That he believes this and that emerges for us from the observation of his person, but he does not make the statement “I believe ‥‥” on the basis of self-observation. And therefore “I believe p” can be equivalent to the assertion of “p.” Therefore, the question “Is it so?” is equivalent to the sentence “I would like to know whether it is so.”

### <span class="fac">[227\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/227.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1173.</span> “This face has a very definite character” actually means: one could say much about it. When does one say this? What justifies one in doing so? Is it a particular experience? Does one already know what one will say; has one already imagined it in silence? Is the situation not similar to this: “Now I know what to do!”

### <span class="fac">[227\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/227.webp),[228\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/228.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1174.</span> We are all familiar with the process of the momentary change of aspect; but how, if one were to ask: “Does A have aspect _a_ constantly before his eyes—if, namely, no change of aspect has occurred? Can the _aspect_ not, as it were, become _fresher_ or _more indefinite_? And how odd that I _ask_ this!

### <span class="fac">[228\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/228.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1175.</span> There is something like a flashing of the aspect. In the same way that one can play something with more or less intense expression. With greater emphasis on the rhythm and the structure, or less.

### <span class="fac">[228\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/228.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1176.</span> _That_ as a variant of _seeing_, hearing. So there is this moment, when, upon seeing A, I _think_ of B, where this seeing, as it were, is acute, & then the time when it is chronic.

### <span class="fac">[228\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/228.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1177.</span> It is difficult not to explain the psychological phenomenon, but rather to accept it.

### <span class="fac">[228\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/228.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1178.</span> “F” as a variation of different figures. If I think that in my mind the paradigm, as whose variation I see the object, is somehow present during seeing, then it could be more or less clearly present, and it could also disappear completely.

### <span class="fac">[228\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/228.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1179.</span> Imagine two people: one learned the letter “F” 🖵 in his youth in this way, – the other, as we did 🖵. If now the two of them read the word “Figur,” – must I say that I have reason to say that they each see the “F” differently? Apparently not. And couldn't it still be that one of them, when he hears how the other learned to write and read this letter, says: “_So_ I never saw it, but always _so_”? And furthermore, there will probably be situations in which I will explain what one of these people does, or says, _so_: “He is, namely, considering this letter as a variant of ‥‥”

### <span class="fac">[228\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/228.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1180.</span> It is certain that one can say: “I have never seen that before.” Here, the “never” is undoubtedly true. – But if you say “I have always seen it like that,” then this “always” is not equally certain. And there is, of course, nothing remarkable about this if one says “understood” instead of “seen.”

### <span class="fac">[228\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/228.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1181.</span> Imagine you know that the sign 🖵 is a combination of a 🖵 and a 🖵. – – This is reminiscent of the dream phenomenon, which one describes in a dream narrative with the words: “and I knew that ‥․”. And it also has a similarity to what one calls “hallucination”.

### <span class="fac">[228\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/228.webp),[229\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/229.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1182.</span> It is as if a paradigm, a template, is present in my mind when I see the sign. But what kind of template? What does it look like? But not simply like the sign itself! – So, like the sign, seen in _that_ way? – But _how_ seen? How should I note the aspect? Well, how do we note it; how do we communicate about it? I might say: “The sign, as I see it, looks to the right.” I could even speak of a kind of visual center of gravity, – say: The center of gravity of the sign 🖵 is located _here_. Can I explain what I mean by that? No. – But I can compare my reaction with the reactions of others.

### <span class="fac">[229\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/229.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1183.</span> Am I always conscious of the blurriness of the edges of my field of vision? Should I say: “Almost never,” or “Never”?

### <span class="fac">[229\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/229.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1184.</span> In another space of thought – one might say – the thing looks different.

### <span class="fac">[229\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/229.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1185.</span> One could imagine a variation on a theme in music, which, perhaps phrased slightly differently, can be understood as a completely different kind of variation of the theme. (There are such ambiguities in rhythm.) Yes, what I mean can probably always be found, when a repetition makes the theme appear in a completely different light.

### <span class="fac">[229\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/229.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1186.</span> No aspect that is not (also) perception.

### <span class="fac">[229\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/229.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1187.</span> Suppose someone said to me: “Something has now changed in the _image_ – I can't express it any other way – although the form is the same as before. I can only say: before, it was a kind of 🖵, now it is a kind of 🖵”. If he said _that_, could I not still doubt that he has always, continuously, _seen_ the figure in that way and has not just never perceived it differently?

### <span class="fac">[229\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/229.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1188.</span> Imagine the child, when it learned the letter “R”, said to us: “I always see it as an ‘R’.” What could that communicate to us? – Yes, even if it said to us, “I always see it as a ‘P’ with an oblique support,” that would only tell us: that is how the child perceives it, that is how it explains the letter to itself, and so on. Only if it spoke of a change of aspect would we say: now it is that phenomenon ‥․.

### <span class="fac">[229\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/229.webp),[230\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1189.</span> If someone says “I always see it _like this_”, then he must specify the “_like this_”. Suppose he does so by tracing the lines of the figure in a certain order, or in a certain rhythm. That would be _similar_ to saying: “I always follow the figure with my eyes _like this_”. And in that case, it could, of course, be that his memory deceives him.

### <span class="fac">[230\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1190.</span> If he says “I (now) see the figure _like this_” and traces it in a certain way, then that must be both a description and, as it were, the seeing itself. But if he says “I _have_ always seen it like that”, then that means that he has never seen it _differently_, and in that he may be mistaken.

### <span class="fac">[230\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1191.</span> No, the paradigm was not constantly present in my mind – – but when I describe the change of aspect, then I describe it by means of paradigms.

### <span class="fac">[230\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1192.</span> “I have always seen it _like this_” – with this one actually wants to say: “I have always understood it _like this_, and _this_ change of aspect has never taken place.”

### <span class="fac">[230\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1193.</span> “I have never seen it _like this_, but always _like this_.” But from that _alone_ there is still no proposition. The field is still lacking.

### <span class="fac">[230\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1194.</span> “I have always seen it with _this_ aspect.” But you must also say, with _which_ one. And as soon as you _add that_, it is no longer as if you had always done it. “I have always seen this letter with a grumpy aspect.” One could ask: “Are you sure that it was _always_ so?” That is to say: was the grumpiness always striking to you?

### <span class="fac">[230\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1195.</span> And what about ‘seeing the sign’? Does this happen in a moment, or does it last?

### <span class="fac">[230\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1196.</span> “When I look at it, I always see the face of his father.” Always? – But surely not just in _moments_! This aspect can persist.

### <span class="fac">[230\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1197.</span> Imagine someone saying: “I now always see it in _this_ context.” –

### <span class="fac">[230\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1198.</span> Absolute and relative hearing: Here is something similar: I hear the transition from one tone to another. But after a short time, I can no longer recognize a tone as the higher or lower of those two. And it would also not make sense to talk about such “recognition”; if, namely, there were no criterion of _correct_ recognition.

### <span class="fac">[231a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1199.</span> It is almost as if ‘_seeing_ the sign in _this_ connection’ were an echoing of a thought.

### <span class="fac">[231a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1200.</span> To say of a real or painted face “I have _always_ seen it as a face” would be odd; but not: “It was always a face for me, and I never saw it _as something else_.”

### <span class="fac">[231a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1201.</span> If, for example, I once see 🖵 as a T with an added stroke, then it is as if the _grouping_ changes. But if one asks me: “So you used to see this figure always with the grouping of an F?” then I could not say that it was so.

### <span class="fac">[231a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1202.</span> If someone says: “I am talking about a visual phenomenon in which the visual image, namely its organization, actually changes, although the shapes and colors remain the same,” then I can reply: “I know what you are talking about; I _also want to say_ what you are saying.” – So I do not say: “Yes, the phenomenon that we are both talking about is really a change of organization ‥‥”, but “Yes, this talking about the change of organization, etc., is the expression of the experience that I also mean.

### <span class="fac">[231a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1203.</span> “The organization of the visual image changes.” – “Yes, I also want to say that.” This is analogous to when someone says, “Everything around me seems unreal” – and another replies: “Yes, I know this phenomenon. I would like to express it in the same way.”

### <span class="fac">[231a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1204.</span> “The organization of the visual image changes” does not have the same kind of application as: “The organization of this association changes.” _Here_ I can describe _how it is_ when the organization of our association changes.

### <span class="fac">[231a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1205.</span> “It never occurred to me that one could see the figure _like that_”: does this imply that it did occur to me, or that I knew that one could see it _like that_, as I always saw it?

### <span class="fac">[231a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1206.</span> I hear a tone – so I don’t hear how loud it is? – Is it correct to say: when I hear the tone, I must be conscious of its degree of loudness? – It is different when its intensity changes.

### <span class="fac">[231a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231a.webp),[231b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231b.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1207.</span> At first glance, it would _seem_ like this: someone realizes that one can see an F as a T with an appendage; he says “Now I see it as a T, etc., now again as an F.” From this, it seems to follow that he sees it the second time as he always saw it before his discovery. – That is, if it made sense to say, “Now I see it again as an F,” it would also have made sense _before_ the change of aspect to say “I always see the letter F as F.”

### <span class="fac">[231b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231b.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1208.</span> If I had always heard a sentence in the same tone (and often), would it be correct to say that I must have been conscious of the tone? If that is the same as saying that I heard it in that tone and always repeat it in that tone, then I am conscious of the tone. But I don’t have to know that there is such a thing as a ‘tone’; the tone never needs to have _struck_ me; I never need to have _listened_ to it. The concept of tone may be completely unknown to me. The ‘_separation_’ of the tone from the sentence does not need to have taken place for me. So I have not learned a language-game with the word “tone.”

### <span class="fac">[231b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231b.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1209.</span> When a child learns the letters, it doesn’t learn to see them _this_ way and not another. Should I now say that later, when the aspect changes, people realize that they have always seen a letter, e.g., an R, in the same way? – Well, that _could_ be the case, but it is not. No, _we don’t say that_. Even if someone said something like, for him, the letter ‥‥ always had this and that appearance, he would admit that in many cases, when looking at the letter, he did not ‘think’ of an appearance.

### <span class="fac">[231b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231b.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1210.</span> Should I now say that a ‘way of seeing’ is associated with a letter for us? Certainly not, unless it means something similar to: an appearance is associated with a letter.

### <span class="fac">[231b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/231b.webp),[232\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/232.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1211.</span> Think of the concept of “handwriting.” One can say, “This is an interesting handwriting of the letter ‥‥” – but does everyone understand what “handwriting” means when they have learned to write a letter? Do I mean: can someone pay attention to the handwriting of an S if he doesn’t even know that there are different handwritings of a letter? – Or am I just playing with words here? You must only not have too narrow a concept of ‘experience.’ Ask yourself, for example: can _someone_ find an utterance to be _vulgar_ if he has never had other examples before him?

### <span class="fac">[232\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/232.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1212.</span> “This script is unpleasant to me.” Can a script be ‘unpleasant’ to someone who is just learning to read and write? – It may perhaps repel him in some sense. Only for someone who can already think all sorts of things about a script does it make sense to say that a script is unpleasant to him.

### <span class="fac">[232\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/232.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1213.</span> Would it be conceivable that instructions could be given for two identical sections of a piece of music, instructing us to _listen_ to it _this_ way the first time and _that_ way the second time, without this having any influence on the performance? For example, the piece of music could be written for a music box, and the two identical sections could be played at the same volume and tempo – only to be _understood_ differently each time. Now, even if a composer has never written such an instruction, could not a critic write it? Wouldn't such an instruction be comparable to a title in program music (“Dance of the Peasants”)?

### <span class="fac">[232\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/232.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1214.</span> Only, of course, if I say to someone, “Listen to it _this_ way,” he must now be able to say, “Yes, now I understand; now it really makes sense!” (Something must click.)

### <span class="fac">[232\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/232.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1215.</span> What concept of sameness, identity, do we have? You know the use of the word “same” when it comes to the same colors, the same sounds, the same shapes, the same lengths, the same feelings, and you decide whether _this and that case_ should be included in this family or not.

### <span class="fac">[232\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/232.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1216.</span> What is so objectionable about the idea that we study the use of a word, point out errors in the description of this use, etc.? Above all, one asks: how could _this_ be so important to us? It depends on whether one calls a ‘false description’ one that does not agree with the sanctioned linguistic usage, or one that does not agree with the practice of the person describing it. Only in the second case does a _philosophical conflict_ arise.

### <span class="fac">[232\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/232.webp),[233\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/233.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1217.</span> Less objectionable is _the idea_ that we form a false image of thinking, for example. For here one says to oneself: we are dealing with thinking at least, not with the word “thinking”. So, we form a false image of thinking. – But _of what_ do we form a false image; how do I know, for example, that you are forming a false image of _that_, of which I also form a false image? Let us assume that our image of thinking is a person who rests his head in his hand and talks to himself. Our question is not “Is this a correct image?” but: “How is this image used as an image of _thinking_?” Not: “We have formed a false image” – but: “We are not familiar with the use of our image, or our images”! And therefore not familiar with the use of our word.

### <span class="fac">[233\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/233.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1218.</span> Well, – but this word is only interesting insofar as it actually has a quite specific use for us, i.e. it already refers to a certain phenomenon! – That is true. And that means: we are not dealing with an improvement of grammatical conventions. – But what does it mean: “We all know to which phenomenon the word ‘thinking’ refers”? Does it not simply mean: we can all play the language-game with the word “thinking”? It only creates confusion to call thinking a ‘phenomenon’; and further confusion to say “we are forming a false image of this phenomenon”. (“One could more easily say ‘a false concept’.”)

### <span class="fac">[233\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/233.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1219.</span> If we are dealing with the use of the word “five”, then we are, in a certain sense, dealing with that to which the word ‘corresponds’; only this way of expressing it is primitive, presupposes a primitive understanding of the use of a word.

### <span class="fac">[233\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/233.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1220.</span> A ‘language-game’: One lets a person choose an aroma, e.g. that of coffee, from a drawing. One says to him: “Coffee smells like this: 🖵” and now one orders him to bring the liquid that smells _like that_. – Now I assume that he would actually bring the right one. So I would have a means of giving commands to a person by means of something _sign-like_. ((Connection with the nature of the rule, the technique, mathematics, e.g. real numbers.)) This also has to do with: (“The hen ‘calls’ the chicks to her.”)

### <span class="fac">[233\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/233.webp),[234\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/234.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1221.</span> “One cannot describe the aroma of coffee.” But could one not imagine that one could? And _what_ would one have to imagine in order to do so? One can ask the person who says “One cannot describe the aroma”: “_With what_ do you want to describe it? With the help of which _elements_?”

### <span class="fac">[234\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/234.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1222.</span> We are not at all _prepared_ for the task of describing the use of the word “thinking”, for example. (And why should we be? What is the use of such a description?) And the naive idea that one has of it does not correspond at all to reality. We expect a smooth, regular contour, and we get to see a jagged one. Here one could really say that we have formed a false image. It is almost as if there were a noun, let us say “giant”, with the help of which one expresses all that we say with the adjective “large”. The image that would come to our minds at the word “giant” would be that of a giant. And now one should describe our _strange_ use of the word “large”, with this image before our eyes.

### <span class="fac">[234\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/234.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1223.</span> Macaulay says that poetry is an “imitative art” and immediately runs into major difficulties with this concept. He wants to describe it; but every image that presents itself to him is _inaccurate_, however obviously correct it may seem at first glance; and it seems so strange that one should not be able to describe what one knows so well. Here one tells oneself: “It _must_ be so! – even if I cannot immediately dismiss all objections.”

### <span class="fac">[234\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/234.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1224.</span> It is quite conceivable that someone knows a city well, i.e., can find the shortest way from any place in the city to any other place with certainty, – and yet is completely unable to draw a map of the city. As soon as he tries, he only produces _completely false_ things. (Our concept of ‘instinct’.)

### <span class="fac">[234\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/234.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1225.</span> First of all, the person attempting the description lacks any system. The systems that come to his mind are inadequate; and suddenly he seems to be in a wilderness instead of in the well-tended garden that he knew so well. Rules do come to his mind, but reality shows nothing but exceptions.

### <span class="fac">[235\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1226.</span> And the rules of the foreground make it impossible for us to recognize the rules in the background. For if we keep the background together with the foreground, we only see unpleasant exceptions, i.e., _irregularity_.

### <span class="fac">[235\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1227.</span> Do we say that _everyone_ who speaks meaningfully is thinking? For example, the builder in language-game No. 2? Could we not imagine the building and uttering of words, etc., in an environment in which we would not associate it with thinking? Because “thinking” is _related_ to “considering”.

### <span class="fac">[235\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1228.</span> “To perform a multiplication mechanically” (whether on paper or in one’s head) we probably say – – but “to consider something mechanically” contains a contradiction for us.

### <span class="fac">[235\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1229.</span> The expression, the behaviour, of considering. Of what do we say: It is considering something? Of the human being, sometimes of the animal. (Not of the tree, or the stone.) _A_ sign of considering is a hesitation in action. (Köhler.) (Not _every_ hesitation.)

### <span class="fac">[235\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1230.</span> Think of ‘considering’ in relation to ‘trying’. To ‘investigating’, to the expression of amazement; of failure and success.

### <span class="fac">[235\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1231.</span> What must a human being not do in order for us to say that he is _thinking_!

### <span class="fac">[235\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1232.</span> He cannot _know_ whether I am thinking, but I know it. What do I know? That what I am doing now is _thinking_? And with what do I compare it in order to know this? And can I not be mistaken in this? So all that remains is: I know that I am doing what I am doing. –

### <span class="fac">[235\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1233.</span> But it does make sense to say “He doesn’t know what I was thinking, because I didn’t tell him”! Is a thought also ‘private’ when I express it aloud in a soliloquy, when no one is listening? “Only I know my thoughts.” This means approximately: “I _can_ describe them, express them, _if_ I want to.”

### <span class="fac">[235\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/235.webp),[236\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/236.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1234.</span> “Only I know my thoughts.” – How do you know that? Experience has not taught you this. – What are you telling us by this? – You must express yourself poorly. “No! I am thinking something right now; tell me what it is!” So it was really an empirical proposition? No; because if I told you what you were thinking, I would only have _guessed_ it. How can it be decided whether I guessed it correctly? By your word, and certain circumstances: So I compare this language-game with _another_, in which the means of decision (verification) are different.

### <span class="fac">[236\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/236.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1235.</span> “I can’t do it here ‥‥” – Where _can_ I do it? In another game. (I can’t hit the ball through the goal in tennis.)

### <span class="fac">[236\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/236.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1236.</span> But isn't there a connection between the grammatical ‘privateness’ of thoughts and the fact that, in general, we cannot guess another's thoughts before he expresses them? There is, after all, a kind of thought-guessing in the sense that someone says to me, “I know what you were thinking now” (or “what you were thinking about”), and I have to admit that he has correctly guessed my thoughts. And this actually happens very rarely. I often sit in my classroom, without speaking, for several minutes, and thoughts go through my head; but none of my students could probably guess what I was thinking. But it would also be possible that someone guess it and write it down, as if I had expressed it. And if he showed me what he had written, I would have to say, “Yes, that’s exactly what I was thinking.” – And here, for example, the question would be undecidable: whether I am not also mistaken; whether I really had that thought, or, influenced by his writing, I now only _imagine_ that I had it. And the word “undecidable” belongs to the description of the language-game.

### <span class="fac">[236\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/236.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1237.</span> And wouldn't _this_ also be conceivable: I say to someone, “You were thinking now…” – He denies it. But I insist on my assertion, and finally he says, “I believe you are right; I must have been thinking that; my memory will deceive me.” And imagine that this is a quite ordinary occurrence!

### <span class="fac">[236\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/236.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1238.</span> “Thoughts and feelings are private” means approximately the same as “There is pretending,” or “One can conceal one's thoughts and feelings; yes, lie and pretend.” And it is the question of what this “There is” and “One can” means.

### <span class="fac">[236\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/236.webp),[237\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/237.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1239.</span> Under what circumstances, on what occasions, does one say, “Only I know my thoughts”? – When one could also have said, “I will not tell you my thoughts,” or “I keep my thoughts secret,” or “You cannot guess my thoughts.”

### <span class="fac">[237\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/237.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1240.</span> About what does one say that one _knows_ it, and in what respect do I know my thoughts? Doesn't one say of something that one knows it, that one can describe it correctly? And can one say this of one's own thoughts? If someone wants to call the words the “description” of the thought, instead of the “expression” of the thought, let him ask himself how one learns to describe a table. And that means only: one looks at how the description of a table, and how the description of thoughts, is judged as correct or incorrect; one should therefore consider these language-games in all their situations.

### <span class="fac">[237\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/237.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1241.</span> “The fact is that man only knows his own thoughts.” (“The fact is that only I know my own thinking.”) “And not even I” one could say.

### <span class="fac">[237\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/237.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1242.</span> “Nature has given man the ability to think in secret.” Imagine someone says, “Nature has given man the ability to speak audibly, but also inaudibly, in his mind.” So, that means, he can do the same thing in two ways. (As if he could digest visibly and invisibly.) Only, in thinking, speaking is better hidden than a process inside the body can be. – But what if I were to speak and everyone else were deaf? Wouldn't my speaking be just as well hidden? “Something is going on in the deepest secret of the mind.”

### <span class="fac">[237\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/237.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1243.</span> If someone _tells_ me what he thought, – has he really told me what he _thought_? Didn't the actual mental event remain undescribed? – – Wasn't _he_ the secret, of which I only give the other person a picture in my speech?

### <span class="fac">[237\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/237.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1244.</span> If I _tell_ someone what I _think_, – do I then know my thought better than my words represent it? Is it as if I know a _body_ and only show the other person a photograph?

### <span class="fac">[237\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/237.webp),[238\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/238.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1245.</span> “Man is given the ability to converse in complete privacy with himself; in a seclusion that is far more complete than that of a hermit.” How do I know that this is given to N.? – Because he says so and is reliable? – And yet we say: “I would like to know what he is thinking right now”; just as we might say: “I would like to know what he is writing in his notebook right now.” Yes, one could indeed say that and, as it were, take it for granted that he is thinking what he is writing in the notebook.

### <span class="fac">[238\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/238.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1246.</span> Would people who _regularly_ – perhaps by observing a person's larynx – could ‘read’ their thoughts, – would they also be inclined to speak of the complete solitude of the mind in conversing with itself? – Or: Would they also be inclined to use the _image_ of ‘complete seclusion’?

### <span class="fac">[238\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/238.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1247.</span> “I would like to know what he is thinking!” But now consider this – seemingly irrelevant – question: “What is so interesting about what is ‘in him,’ in his mind – assuming that something is going on?” (May the devil take what is going on in him!)

### <span class="fac">[238\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/238.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1248.</span> The comparison of thinking with a process taking place in secrecy is misleading in philosophy. _So_ misleading as the comparison of searching for the appropriate expression with the efforts of someone who wants to trace a _line visible only to him_ exactly.

### <span class="fac">[238\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/238.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1249.</span> What confuses us is that knowing the thoughts of another, viewed from _one_ side, is logically impossible, and seen from another side, is psychologically and physiologically impossible.

### <span class="fac">[238\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/238.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1250.</span> Is it correct to say that these two ‘impossibilities’ are so related to each other that the psychological impossibility (_here_) provides the image that then becomes the sign of the concept of ‘thinking’?

### <span class="fac">[238\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/238.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1251.</span> One cannot say that writing in a notebook, or monological speech, is ‘_similar_’ to silent thinking; however, one process can _replace_ the other for certain purposes (calculating in one's head can replace written calculation, for example).

### <span class="fac">[238\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/238.webp),[239\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/239.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1252.</span> Could there be people who always mutter to themselves while thinking, so that their thinking is accessible to others? – “Yes, but how could we know if they are not, in addition, thinking silently to themselves!” – But could it not be that assuming this would be just as senseless as assuming that the hair of these people is thinking, or that a stone is thinking? Does that mean that, if this were the case, we would only think that someone who has thoughts has thoughts hidden in his mind?

### <span class="fac">[239\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/239.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1253.</span> “I don’t know what you’re thinking. Tell me what you’re thinking!” – That is, “Speak!”

### <span class="fac">[239\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/239.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1254.</span> Is it therefore misleading to speak of the mind of a person, or of his spirit? _So_ little that it is perfectly understandable when I say: “My mind is tired, not just my intellect.” But don’t you still say that everything that can be expressed by the word “mind” can somehow also be expressed by words for the physical? I don’t say that. But even if that were the case, what would it _mean_? The words, as well as what we point to in their explanation, are only the instruments, and now it depends on their use.

### <span class="fac">[239\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/239.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1255.</span> Our knowledge of many languages does not allow us to take the philosophy that is contained in the forms of each of them very seriously. However, we are blind to the fact that we (ourselves) have strong prejudices for and against certain forms of expression; that this layering of several languages also gives us a particular image. That we, as it were, cannot arbitrarily replace _one_ form with another.

### <span class="fac">[239\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/239.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1256.</span> You must consider that there could be a language-game, ‘continuing a series of numbers’, in which no rule, no expression of a rule, is ever given, but learning happens only through examples. So that the idea of a justification through a picture, which forces us to proceed in this way, would be completely foreign to these people.

### <span class="fac">[239\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/239.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1257.</span> Example: the names that only have meaning in the company of their bearers, i.e., only used in this way. They therefore serve only to avoid constantly pointing. The example that always occurs to me is the designation of lines, points, angles in geometric figures with A, B, C, ‥․ a, b, ‥․ etc.

### <span class="fac">[239\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/239.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1258.</span> When reading: seeing the word-image: “I saw the word in passing” – this is a special experience, and cannot be represented by a film.

### <span class="fac">[240\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/240.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1259.</span> Imagine a mental illness in which one can only use and understand names in the presence of their bearers.

### <span class="fac">[240\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/240.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1260.</span> It could be that signs are used in such a way that the signs become useless (that one might destroy them) as soon as the bearer ceases to exist. In this language-game, the name would, as it were, have the object on a string; and when the object ceases to exist, one can throw away the name that worked together with it.

### <span class="fac">[240\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/240.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1261.</span> “I intend to go there”: _description_ of a state of mind, or _utterances??_? – If one imagines a model of the mind, then the sentence could be a description of the model in its present state. The person looks at his mind & says: ‥‥․. Is it a good, or a bad model? – How would this be decided? The question is: How would it be _used_ as a sign?

### <span class="fac">[240\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/240.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1262.</span> “I intend ‥‥” one could use this as an assertion: “I am doing something that is in accordance with this intention,” e.g., I pack for the trip, prepare for the trip in one way or another, through thoughts or actions. Thus, one could use a verb. Approximately corresponding to the expression “I act with the intention ‥‥.”

### <span class="fac">[240\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/240.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1263.</span> Description of my states of mind: the change of fear and hope, for example, “In the morning I was full of hope, then ‥‥.” Everyone would call this a description. But it is characteristic of this that there could be a parallel description of my behaviour.

### <span class="fac">[240\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/240.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1264.</span> Compare the expression of fear and hope with that of ‘belief’ that this and that will happen. – That is why hope and fear are also called “emotions,” but not belief (or the act of believing).

### <span class="fac">[240\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/240.webp),[241\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/241.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1265.</span> If I say: “The intention to do it became stronger from hour to hour” – one will call this a description. But then also _this_: “I intended it all along ‥.” Compare now “I believed in the law of gravity all along” with “I believed all along that I was hearing a faint whisper.” In the first case, “believing” is used similarly to “knowing.” (‘_Had_ one asked me, I _would_ have said ‥‥’). In the second case, we have an activity, a conjecturing, listening, doubting, etc. And although “believing” does not denote this activity, it is nevertheless it that makes us say that we are _describing_ a state of mind or a mental activity. – We could also put it this way: We form a picture of the person who believes all along that he is hearing a faint noise. But not one of the person who believes in the correctness of the law of gravity.

### <span class="fac">[241\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/241.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1266.</span> “I intend” (one could say) does not mean: “I am in the process of intending,” or “I am intending” (as one says, I am reading the newspaper). Rather: “I am in the process of planning my trip,” etc. We do not have a _single_ verb, but we could have one (and perhaps it actually exists in a little-known language) that expresses: “to act with the intention of doing this and that.”

### <span class="fac">[241\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/241.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1267.</span> “I intend…” is _never_ a description, but under certain circumstances a description can be extracted from it.

### <span class="fac">[241\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/241.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1268.</span> Talking to oneself. “What is happening here?” A wrong question! Not only can one not say what is happening—one cannot even say that one does not know what is happening—one cannot even say that one knows only this and that about it! But it is also wrong to say that it is simply a specific process that cannot be described except with these words. – The concepts of ‘description’ and ‘report’. One says: someone reports that he said to himself… In what way is this comparable to the ‘report’ that he said, for example…? Let us realize that _describing_ is a very _special language-game_. – We must dig up this hard foundation of our concepts.

### <span class="fac">[241\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/241.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1269.</span> Concepts _can_ facilitate or hinder nonsense; favor or inhibit it.

### <span class="fac">[241\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/241.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1270.</span> It is perfectly correct: one cannot imagine an explanation of ‘red’ or ‘color’. But not because what is experienced is something specific, but because it is the language-game.

### <span class="fac">[241\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/241.webp),[242\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/242.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1271.</span> “One cannot explain to a person what _red_ is.” – But if one could, would it not be what we call “red”? Let us imagine people who express an intermediate color, between red and yellow, for example, in a kind of binary decimal fraction _like_ this: R, LLRL, etc., where yellow is on the right and red on the left. – These people already learn in kindergarten to describe color tones in this way, to select colors according to these descriptions, to mix them, etc. They would behave towards us approximately as people with perfect pitch behave towards people who lack it. _They can do_ what we cannot do.

### <span class="fac">[242\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/242.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1272.</span> And here one would like to say: “But is that also imaginable? Yes, probably the _behaviour_! but also the inner process, the color experience?” And what one should say in response to such a question is difficult to see. Could those who do not have perfect pitch have suspected that there must also be people with perfect pitch?

### <span class="fac">[242\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/242.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1273.</span> If someone said, “Red is composite,” we could not guess what he is alluding to, what he intends to do with this sentence. But if he says, “This armchair is composite,” we may not immediately know what kind of composition he is speaking of, but we can immediately think of more than one sense for his statement. What kind of fact is this, to which I drew attention? In any case, it is an _important_ fact. – We are not familiar with any technique to which this sentence could refer.

### <span class="fac">[242\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/242.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1274.</span> We are describing here a language-game that we _cannot learn_.

### <span class="fac">[242\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/242.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1275.</span> “Then something completely different must be happening in him, something we do not know.” – _This shows us_ what we mean when we determine whether something different from, or the same as, what is in us is happening ‘in the other’. This shows us _what_ we mean when we judge inner processes.

### <span class="fac">[242\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/242.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1276.</span> “Red is not composite” – and what is red?! – We would simply like to point to something red; and we forget that, if this statement is to have any meaning, we must be given more than the ostensive definition. We do not yet understand what the meaning of a sentence of the form “X is not composite” is, when a word that has the use of our color words is substituted for X.

### <span class="fac">[242\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/242.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1277.</span> It is a fact: “Red” is not explained to one by words without reference to a color sample. Shouldn’t this be important?

### <span class="fac">[242\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/242.webp)</span>
