<span class="series-number">1278.</span> “How could one explain red to someone, since it is a particular sense impression, and only he knows it who has it (or has had it) – and explaining can only mean: to produce it in the other!” –

### <span class="fac">[243\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/243.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1279.</span> “Whoever has absolute pitch must have a different auditory experience than I do.” – And does everyone who has absolute pitch have the same experience? And if not, why must it be different from mine?

### <span class="fac">[243\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/243.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1280.</span> Imagine, in order to explain ‘red’ to someone, we show him a reddish brown, and say: “This color consists of yellow (we show pure yellow), black (we show it), and another color that is called ‘red’.” Suppose he is now able to select pure red from a number of color samples.

### <span class="fac">[243\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/243.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1281.</span> And note well: one does not point to red, but to something red. That is to say: the concept ‘red’ is not determined by pointing, and it is not only possible to interpret “red” as the name of a form, e.g., but also as a concept word that is _much closer_ to a color word.

### <span class="fac">[243\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/243.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1282.</span> The _use_ of a word is not: to _denote_ something.

### <span class="fac">[243\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/243.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1283.</span> Can _you_ imagine what the red-green colorblind person sees? Can you paint the picture of the room as he sees it?

### <span class="fac">[243\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/243.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1284.</span> “Whoever saw only gray, black and white, would have to be given something so that he would know what red, green, etc. is.” And what would have to be given to him? Well, the colors. So, for example, _this_, and _this_, and _this_. (Imagine, for example, that colored exemplars would have to be introduced into his brain, in addition to the merely gray and black ones.) But would this have to happen as a means to the end of future action? Or does this action itself include these exemplars? Do I mean: “Something would have to be given to him, because it is clear that he could not otherwise ‥‥” – or: His seeing behaviour _contains_ new components? Also: what would we _call_ an “explanation of seeing”? Should one say: Well, you know what “explanation” means; so use this concept here as well!

### <span class="fac">[243\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/243.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1285.</span> Can I say: “Look at it! so you will see that it cannot be explained.” – Or: “Drink in the color red, so you will see that it cannot be represented by something else!” – And if the other person agrees with me, does that show that he has absorbed the same thing as I have? – And what does our inclination to say this mean? Red seems to stand isolated. Why? What is this appearance, this inclination _worth_?

### <span class="fac">[244\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/244.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1286.</span> Think of the sentence “Red is not a mixed color” and its function. The language-game with colors is precisely characterized by what we can do and what we cannot do.

### <span class="fac">[244\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/244.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1287.</span> Red is something specific; but we do not see that when we look at something red. Rather, (_we see_) the _phenomena_ that we _differentiate_ by means of the language-game with the word “red.”

### <span class="fac">[244\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/244.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1288.</span> “Red is something specific,” that should mean as much as: “_This_ is something specific” – while pointing to something red. But in order for that to be understandable, one would already have to mean our _concept_ of ‘red’, the use of that sample.

### <span class="fac">[244\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/244.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1289.</span> If you are surprised by these things, be surprised by something else first! Namely, what description and report actually achieve. If you concentrate on that, then those other problems will fade away.

### <span class="fac">[244\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/244.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1290.</span> Primary colors. If, in other people, colors that we call mixed colors played the role of our primary colors, would we say that _their_ primary colors are, for example, this orange, this blue-red, this blue-green, etc.? Does the sentence “Red is a primary color” mean as much as: Red plays this and that role for us; we react to red, yellow, etc., in this and that way? – One does not usually think of it that way: that is, “Red is a pure color” is a sentence about the ‘essence’ of red; time does not enter into it; one cannot imagine that _this_ color could not be simple.

### <span class="fac">[244\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/244.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1291.</span> The color circle: The equal distances of the primary colors are arbitrary. Yes, the transitions might perhaps give us a more uniform impression if, for example, the point of pure blue were closer to the point of pure green than to the point of pure red. It would be very strange if the equality of the distances were in the nature of things.

### <span class="fac">[244\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/244.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1292.</span> “A reddish green does not exist” is related to the sentences that we use as axioms in mathematics.

### <span class="fac">[244\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/244.webp),[245\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/245.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1293.</span> People count and calculate: describe what they do! Should this description also include sentences such as: “He now understood how to continue the series” – or: “He is now able to perform any multiplication”? And is the sentence “He now saw the entire series of numbers before him in his mind” permissible? Such sentences can occur in the description; but can we not demand that their use be explained to us, so that no false or _irrelevant_ ideas creep in? Here the question is, for whom are we giving the description. Of whom do we say that he is able to perform any multiplication? How does one come to this _concept_ at all? And for whom, under what circumstances, will this description be important?

### <span class="fac">[245\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/245.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1294.</span> ‘Red, a degenerate green.’ If one sees a leaf of green turning red, one says that the green is sickly and completely degenerated in the red. One always sees, as it were, a face when one sees the red color. Could one not explain red as the ultimate degeneration of green?

### <span class="fac">[245\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/245.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1295.</span> “One cannot _explain_ to anyone what red is!” – How does one come up with this idea at all; on what occasion does one say this?

### <span class="fac">[245\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/245.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1296.</span> “Colors are something specific. They cannot be explained by anything else.” How is this instrument used? – Describe the game with colors! The naming of colors, the comparing of colors, the producing of colors, the connection between color and light and illumination, the connection of color with the eye, tones with the ear, and countless other things. Will not the ‘specificity’ of color show itself here? How does one show someone a color; and how, a tone?

### <span class="fac">[245\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/245.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1297.</span> When we think to ourselves: “Something is happening; that is certain.” But the _use_ of these words is actually just as unclear to us as the particular psychological sentences that we want to _explain_.

### <span class="fac">[245\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/245.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1298.</span> Instead of the irreducible, specific, indefinable: the fact that we act in this way, for example, we _punish_ certain actions, we _establish_ the facts in this way, we give commands, we give reports, we _describe_ colors, we are interested in the feelings of others. What can be accepted, what is given – one could say – are the facts of life.

### <span class="fac">[245\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/245.webp),[246\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/246.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1299.</span> We judge the motive of an act according to what the person who committed it tells us, according to the accounts of eyewitnesses, according to the background. Thus, we judge the motives of a person. But it does not seem odd to us that there is something like the ‘judgment of motives.’ That this is a quite peculiar language-game – that the table and the stone do not have motives. That there is also the question: “Is this a reliable way to judge a person’s motives?” – but we must already know what “the judgment of motives” means. There must already be a technique that we have in mind here, so that we can speak of a _modification of this technique_, which we call a more reliable judgment of a motive.

### <span class="fac">[246\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/246.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1300.</span> One judges the length of a rod, and one can search for and find a method to judge it more accurately, more correctly. So – you say – what we judge here is independent of the method of judging; one cannot explain what length _is_ with the help of the method of determining lengths. But whoever thinks this is making a mistake. What kind of mistake? – How strange it would be to say: “The height of the Himalayas depends on how one climbs it.” “Measure the length ever more accurately,” one wants to compare that to getting closer and closer to an object. But it is not clear in all cases what it means to “get closer and closer to the length of the rod.” And one cannot say: “You know what the length of a rod is; and you know what ‘determining it’ means; _therefore_ you know what it means to ‘determine the length ever more accurately.’” What it means to seek a more accurate determination of the length of the rod is clear under certain circumstances, and under certain circumstances it is not clear and requires a new determination. One does not learn what “determining the length” means by learning what length is and what determining is; rather, one learns the meaning of the word length, among other things, by learning what determining lengths is. ‘Refining the determination of lengths’ is a new technique that modifies our concept of length.

### <span class="fac">[246\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/246.webp),[247\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/247.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1301.</span> When one describes simple language-games for illustration, say, of what we call the ‘motive’ of an action, then one is always presented with more and more complicated cases to show that our theory does not yet correspond to the facts. Whereas more complicated cases are simply more complicated cases. For if it were a theory, one could indeed say: it is useless to consider these specific cases; they do not provide an explanation of the most important cases. The simple language-games, on the other hand, _play a completely different role_. They are poles of a description, not the basis of a theory.

### <span class="fac">[247\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/247.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1302.</span> “How does it come about that it seems to us that this colour impression that I have now is recognized by me as something specific, indivisible?” – Instead, ask how it comes about that we want to say this. And the answer _to that_ is not difficult to find. And it is also a strange question: why it seems to us that ‥․. For in this expression there is already a misunderstanding.

### <span class="fac">[247\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/247.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1303.</span> Suppose you were to describe how people learn to count (in the decimal system, for example). You describe what the teacher says and does, and how the student reacts. In what the teacher says and does, one would find, for example, words and gestures that are intended to encourage the student to continue a series; also words like “He can now count.” Should the description that I give of the process of teaching and learning include, in addition to the teacher’s words, my own judgment as well: that the student can now count, or: that the student now understands the system of number words? If I do not include such a judgment in the description, is it then incomplete? And if I include it, do I go beyond mere description? – Can I refrain from making those judgments with the justification: “_That is all that happens!_”

### <span class="fac">[247\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/247.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1304.</span> Shouldn’t I rather ask: “What does the description actually do? What is its purpose?” – What a complete and an incomplete description is, we certainly know in another context. Ask yourself: How are the expressions “complete” and “incomplete description” used? To give a complete (or incomplete) account of a speech. Does this also include giving an account of the tone of voice, the facial expressions, the sincerity or insincerity of the feelings, the intentions of the speaker, the effort involved in speaking? Whether this or that belongs to the complete description will depend on the purpose of the description, on what the recipient does with the description.

### <span class="fac">[247\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/247.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1305.</span> The expression “That is all that _happens_” defines what we call “happening.”

### <span class="fac">[247\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/247.webp),[248\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/248.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1306.</span> I make my judgment “The student can now count” for certain purposes. One then gives him, for example, a job. If you say, “Thus, this judgment is not part of the description of the learning, but a prediction,” then I reply: “You can see it that way or another.” You can say that you are describing the student’s state. –

### <span class="fac">[248\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/248.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1307.</span> Imagine red as seen as the pinnacle of all colors. The special role of the triad in our music. Our lack of understanding of the old church scales.

### <span class="fac">[248\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/248.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1308.</span> Under what circumstances would one say that these people perceive all colors as degrees of _one_ property?

### <span class="fac">[248\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/248.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1309.</span> Can you imagine that we always saw blue and red as the two extreme poles of a change from violet? One could then call red a very high violet and blue a very deep violet.

### <span class="fac">[248\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/248.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1310.</span> Or imagine a world in which colors almost always occur in rainbow-like transitions. So that one would perceive something as a green area, if it occurs exceptionally, as a _modification of a rainbow_.

### <span class="fac">[248\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/248.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1311.</span> Can I then say that if _these_ were the facts, people would have these concepts? But certainly not. Rather, this: do not think that our concepts are the only possible, or reasonable ones; if you imagine quite different facts from those that constantly surround us, then other concepts will naturally seem to you than ours.

### <span class="fac">[248\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/248.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1312.</span> Don’t believe that you hold the concept of color in yourself because you look at a colored object, however you look at it. (Just as little as you possess the concept of negative numbers by having debts.)

### <span class="fac">[248\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/248.webp),[249\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/249.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1313.</span> Let us assume that we know a people who have a completely different form of color statements than ours: we then mostly assume that it would be easy to teach these people our form of expression. And that, if they both master both forms of expression, they will recognize their difference as insignificant. (The gender of our main words). Is that so? Must it be so? Let us imagine that people have two different simple names for two shades of blue, and for them the colors are _very_ different, which they are not for us. How would this manifest itself? And let us also imagine the opposite: that for a people, red and blue would only differ ‘in degree’, not ‘completely different colors’. And what would be the criteria _for_ this? We say that in the musical scale, the same note is repeated after every 7 notes. What does it mean: “We _perceive_ it as the same”? Is it the case that calling it the same is only a linguistic _coincidence_?

### <span class="fac">[249\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/249.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1314.</span> The mentally deficient are imagined as a degenerate, essentially incomplete, as it were, tattered individual. Thus, under the guise of disorder, instead of a more primitive order (which would be a much more productive way of looking at it).

### <span class="fac">[249\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/249.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1315.</span> Counting, arithmetic, etc., in a closed system, just as a melody is complete. People, for example, count with the help of the notes of a particular melody; at the end of the melody, the number series comes to an end. – Should I say: of course, there are other numbers, only these people do not recognize them? Or should I say: there is another kind of counting – the one _we_ do – and these people do not know (do) it.

### <span class="fac">[249\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/249.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1316.</span> The concept of experience: similar to that of an event, a process, a state, something, a fact, a description and a report. Here we think, we stand on the hard ground, and deeper than all specific methods and language-games. But these highly general words also have a very vague meaning. They do indeed refer to a _multitude_ of specific cases, but this does not make them _more solid_, but rather makes them more fleeting.

### <span class="fac">[249\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/249.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1317.</span> Mental arithmetic is perhaps the only case in which imagination is regularly used in everyday life. That is why it is of particular interest. “But I _know_ that something has happened inside me!” And what? Wasn’t it that you did mental arithmetic? – So mental arithmetic _is_ something specific! First consider: how is the description “he does mental arithmetic,” “I do mental arithmetic” used at all? The difficulty one encounters is a vagueness in the criteria for the occurrence of the mental process. Could this be eliminated?

### <span class="fac">[250\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/250.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1318.</span> Can one _imagine_ mental arithmetic?

### <span class="fac">[250\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/250.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1319.</span> One can do audible arithmetic and mental arithmetic: could one also do something in one's head that one cannot do audibly, for which there is no audible equivalent? What if people had a designation for mental arithmetic that did not put it under the _activities_ and certainly not under that of arithmetic? They designate it, for example, as a _skill_. I assume they use images that are radically different from ours.

### <span class="fac">[250\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/250.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1320.</span> If, however, someone said: “Everything that happens is just that he reacts, behaves in such and such a way” – then there is again a gross misunderstanding. For did the one who said “I did the multiplication without writing, etc.”, in some sense _calculate_ – has this person talked _nonsense_, or reported something false? It is a different use of language than that of describing behavior. But one could certainly ask: what is the importance of this new use of language? What, for example, is the importance of the expression of intention?

### <span class="fac">[250\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/250.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1321.</span> “How, if someone had mental images of a certain intensity, clarity, of afterimages, for example; would these be images, or would they be hallucinations – even if he is fully conscious of the unreality of what he sees?” Above all: How do I know that he sees images of this clarity? He says so, perhaps. One difference would be that his images are ‘independent’ of him. What does that mean? – He could not dispel them through thought. For example, if I imagine the death of my friend, one could tell me “Don’t think about it, think about something else”; but one would not say that to me if I saw the event, for example, in a movie. And so I would answer the one who, in the assumed case, told me to not think about it: “I may or may not want to think about it – I _see_ it.”

### <span class="fac">[250\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/250.webp),[251\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/251.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1322.</span> Consider the use of the English words “this”, “that”, “these”, “those”, “will”, “shall”: It would be difficult to give rules for the use of these words. But it is possible to _understand_ it, so that you would then be inclined to say: “If one has the right _feel_ for the sense of these words, then one can also apply them.” One could also ascribe a peculiar meaning to these words in the English language. We see a kind of physiognomy in the use of the word.

### <span class="fac">[250a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/250a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1323.</span> Mental arithmetic on command. Do not let the combination of familiar words prevent you from examining the language-game from the ground up. Consider that one teaches someone mental arithmetic by commanding him to _calculate_! But must this be the case? Could it not be that, in order to get him to do mental arithmetic, I should not say “Calculate!”, but perhaps: “Do something _else_, but find the result.” Or: “Close your mouth and eyes and do not move, and you will learn the answer.” I want to say that one does not have to consider mental arithmetic from the point of view of _calculation_, although it is _essentially_ connected with calculation. Nor from the point of view of ‘action’. Because action is, for example, what one _imposes_ on someone.

### <span class="fac">[250a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/250a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1324.</span> I want to say: It is not necessary to interpret reactions that are different from ours, and therefore perhaps conducive to different forms of concept formation, as consequences, or manifestations, of (inner) processes that are of a different nature. It is not necessary to say: This involves different inner processes.

### <span class="fac">[250a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/250a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1325.</span> We have, on the one hand, his ability to communicate stages of calculation without perceptible calculation – and, on the other hand, the utterances he is inclined to make, such as: “I calculated in my mind.” The phenomena of the first kind _could_ lead us to a descriptive, pictorial description: “It is as if he calculates somehow and somewhere, and communicates stages of this calculation to us.” What he is inclined to say, we can take as a manner of expression of our language, or not. We could, for example, say to him: “You are not ‘calculating in your mind’! You are calculating _improperly_.” And now he says _this_ in the future.

### <span class="fac">[250a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/250a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1326.</span> “But I _know_ that I am _really_ calculating – even if it is not perceptible to others!” This could be regarded as a typical utterance of a mentally retarded person.

### <span class="fac">[251\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/251.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1327.</span> But if we clear up like this with the inner process – does only the outer one remain? – It is not only the language-game of describing the outer process that remains, but also the one that arises from the utterance. However our manner of expression may be; however it makes the relationship to ‘outer’ calculation.

### <span class="fac">[251\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/251.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1328.</span> If a topic, a turn of phrase, something suddenly speaks to you, then you do not need to be able to explain it. This particular gesture is suddenly also accessible to you.

### <span class="fac">[251\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/251.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1329.</span> Comparison of physical processes and states, such as digestion, breathing, etc., with mental ones, such as thinking, feeling, wanting, etc. What I want to emphasize is precisely the incomparability. Rather, I would say, the comparable bodily states would be: _speed_ of breathing, _irregularity_ of the heartbeat, _reliability_ of digestion, and the like. And of course, one could say that all of these characterize the behaviour of the body.

### <span class="fac">[251\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/251.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1330.</span> Imagine a tribe of people who do not say “he is in pain”, “we are in pain”, “the same thing is happening in him as in me”, “these people have the same mental experience”, etc.; but they talk about a mind and about processes in the mind, but say that they know absolutely nothing about whether two people, of whom _we_ might say they are in pain, really have the same thing, or something completely different; and they therefore say that these people have something unknown, and then in their manner of expression there follows a determination that is equivalent to our “they are in pain”. These people will then also not say: “If I believe that someone is in pain, then I believe that something specific is happening in him”, and so on. Must we see it at all in such a way that the pain signal and the description of pain-behaviour provide a conceptual unity? I want to ask: “Where is the conceptual element here, and where is the phenomenal element?” Must the _language_ contain a pain utterance? Let us imagine people with a finger language. Or people who only write, not speak. Would they have to possess the concept ‘pain’?

### <span class="fac">[251\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/251.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1331.</span> But is it easier to imagine that people do not have our concept of pain than that they do not have the concept of the physical body?

### <span class="fac">[252\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/252.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1332.</span> It is an important fact that we assume that it is always possible to teach people who have a different language from ours our language. That is why we say that their concepts are the same as ours.

### <span class="fac">[252\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/252.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1333.</span> “You start a sentence in which the verb is at the very end; you won’t tell me that you started speaking the sentence without having some idea of what the verb will be!” – And what does the idea consist of? And if someone really had no idea about it, and yet spoke fluent German! How would one find out whether he had this idea?

### <span class="fac">[252\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/252.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1334.</span> In what sense do we investigate the use of words? – Do we not also judge it? Do we not also say that this move is essential, that one is inessential?

### <span class="fac">[252\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/252.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1335.</span> One can describe measuring with a meter stick; how can one justify it? Is the concept ‘pain’ an instrument that man has made; and what is it used for?

### <span class="fac">[252\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/252.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1336.</span> Yes – how can one command someone to mean the same thing with these words _in that way_? Unless one commands him to use them in that way. –

### <span class="fac">[252\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/252.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1337.</span> Suppose you have to make a decision, by pressing one of a number of buttons. The decision that you make is indicated by a word that is on the button. It is then, of course, completely irrelevant what you experience when you see this word. If the word is, for example, “soft”, then you can mean it as an adjective, noun, or verb; the decision is not changed by that. And likewise, if you _pronounce_ the word as the decision. It certainly conveys the same thing to the other person, who is waiting for the decision.

### <span class="fac">[252\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/252.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1338.</span> But what if the decision is open to two interpretations, and the person who hears it gives it _one_ of them? He can do this either through his actions, or, as it were, in thought. If there were not an immediate need to act on the decision, he could also hear it and for the time being not interpret it at all. On the other hand, he could answer a _question_ with an interpretation. This would be a preliminary reaction.

### <span class="fac">[252\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/252.webp),[253\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/253.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1339.</span> It is indeed possible to pronounce the words of a certain situation in accordance with it and thus with a certain meaning, and yet to _think_ of a different meaning. So that the words, for me, have a meaning of their own, which is unconscious to the other person.

### <span class="fac">[253\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/253.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1340.</span> If asked, I will perhaps explain this meaning, and yet this explanation was not what I had in mind. So what did my state of mind have to do with the words of the explanation when I uttered the ambiguous word? In what way can these words correspond to it? There is obviously no fitting of the explanation to the phenomenon.

### <span class="fac">[253\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/253.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1341.</span> One can also intend an expression in one way while uttering it, and immediately afterwards, retrospectively, in another way.

### <span class="fac">[253\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/253.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1342.</span> It seems to us that the word in its two meanings has different illustrations associated with it; and one could give the word an illustration that is composed of both, but then it would not be one of the two illustrations appropriate to the word, or usual. This does not, of course, mean that whenever one uses the word, one of the two illustrations must be present, but only that, _if_ we illustrate the word, _one_ of the two images belongs to it, not both.

### <span class="fac">[253\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/253.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1343.</span> ‘If you had asked me, I would have given you _that_ answer.’ This describes a state; but not a ‘concomitant’ of my words.

### <span class="fac">[253\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/253.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1344.</span> Imagine people have the habit of doodling while speaking; why should what they produce in this way while speaking be less interesting than accompanying processes in their mind, and why should the interest in _these_ be of a different kind? Why does _one_ of these processes seem to give the words their own _life_?

### <span class="fac">[253\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/253.webp),[254\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/254.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1345.</span> Depending on whether he meant the word _this_ way or _that_ way, he expressed the one or the other intention. He had the one or the other intention. And no more can be said about the importance of this meaning. And it seems again that it is less important what happens when uttering the individual word (“bank,” for example) than what has happened in and before the whole sentence. As if the mind illustrated the whole sentence, not necessarily the one word. And yet, we must admit, the illustration must not be important. Why should so much depend on it? And how can it give the sentence a _definite_ life if language does not give it that? Why should it be clearer than word-language?

### <span class="fac">[254\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/254.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1346.</span> Now, the crucial point is that I can not only judge the meaning according to the context, but one can also ask about it, and the person answering does not derive the meaning from the context.

### <span class="fac">[254\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/254.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1347.</span> Is it self-evident that someone who can use the language is able to _explain_ the words that he understands, whose use he understands? We would, of course, be very surprised if someone understood the word “bank” but could not answer the question “What is a bank?” Is it not one thing to understand the sentence “Let's go for a little walk in the sun” – and another to be able to explain the word “sun”? – But must the person who understands this sentence not know what the sun looks like? Just as the person who understands the sentence “I am not in pain” must, for example, know how to inflict pain, and how someone in pain behaves, etc. –

### <span class="fac">[254\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/254.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1348.</span> Furthermore: if it is possible to strip the ambiguous word of _any_ ‘meaning’ by repeating it, why should not some people, when uttering it without context, usually do so without feeling any meaning? Or why should people not utter such a word with a kind of trembling meaning, when no context _holds_ it?

### <span class="fac">[254\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/254.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1349.</span> “But what do you do when you obey the command ‘Say ‥‥ and by that I mean ‥‥’?” – You are not doing _something else_. But also not: something specific.

### <span class="fac">[254\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/254.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1350.</span> In any case, this is not a language-game that one learns very much: to pronounce a word, in isolation, with this or that meaning. The basis is evidently that someone says that he can pronounce the word ‥‥ and, in doing so, intends one or the other of its meanings. This is easy if the word has two meanings; but can you also pronounce the word “apple” and mean “table” by it? – I could, after all, use a secret language in which it has that meaning.

### <span class="fac">[254\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/254.webp),[255\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/255.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1351.</span> “Give him this command and, by that, mean ‥‥!” “Tell him this and, by that, mean ‥‥!” That would be a remarkable command, one that one does not usually give. Or I say to someone, “Convey this message,” and later ask him, “Did you mean it in this way or that?”

### <span class="fac">[255\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/255.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1352.</span> But is the past tense then justified in the question? Yes, because I am contrasting a change of attitude with a constancy. I really don’t just want to know what he means now, but also what he meant. – One could, for example, ask, “What do you mean? And have you changed your attitude?” If the answer to this question is no, then he also meant it in the same way before, according to the explanation. I mean: the criteria for what happened in the past are different here than, for example, for the emergence of a picture.

### <span class="fac">[255\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/255.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1353.</span> So how am I supposed to describe this psychological phenomenon? That one can, on command, mean this or that by a word? That one imagines oneself as meaning it this way or that? Should I say that the word “mean” is used here in a different sense; that one should actually use a different word? Should I propose such a word? – Or is this precisely the phenomenon that we are using the word “mean” here, which we learned for a different purpose?

### <span class="fac">[255\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/255.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1354.</span> Is it a very primitive language-game in which one says, “When I heard this word, ‥‥ occurred to me”?

### <span class="fac">[255\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/255.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1355.</span> Instead of “I meant _this_ by the word,” one could also say, “The word stood for ‥‥.” And how could the word, when I uttered it, stand for this and not for that? And yet, it has precisely _this_ appearance. Is this then, in a way, an optical illusion? (As if the word _reflected_ the object that the explanation assigns to it.) And if this is an optical illusion, what do people lose who are not familiar with this illusion? They should lose very little.

### <span class="fac">[255\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/255.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1356.</span> The particular experience of meaning is characterized by the fact that we react with an explanation and the past tense: just as if we were explaining the meaning of a word for practical purposes.

### <span class="fac">[255\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/255.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1357.</span> The intention may change, and at the same time, an experiential content, but the intention was not an experience.

### <span class="fac">[255\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/255.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1358.</span> One of the fundamental principles of observation must surely be that I do not dis**turb** the phenomenon I am observing through my observation. That is to say, my observation must be usable, applicable to the cases in which no observation takes place.

### <span class="fac">[256\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/256.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1359.</span> Does this sudden realization – “Now I know!” – not correspond to a particular experience? No. Imagine someone who keeps exclaiming “Now I get it!” when they hadn’t understood anything; what are we to say about them? What experience did they have? It is not the particular ‘experiential content’ of the realization that gives it its special significance, and if someone says that they understood everything at that moment, that is not a description of an experiential content. – But why not? – I want to distinguish between a statement that “I saw the formula before me at that moment” and one such as “I grasped the method at that moment.” But not as if I wanted to say – “because one cannot grasp a method in a single moment.” One can, in fact, and it happens _very_ often. – I want to say: “‘Now I understand’ is a _signal_, not a description.” And _what_ is accomplished by saying this? Well, attention is directed to the origin of such a signal; the question of “How does one learn the words ‘Now I understand’ and how, for example, the description of an image?” comes to the fore. Because the word “signal” points to a process that is being signaled.

### <span class="fac">[256\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/256.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1360.</span> It is indeed the undeniable nature of it that favors the image: it is as if something is being described that only we see and not the other person, something that is therefore close to us and always accessible, but hidden from the other, something that lies _within_ us and that we become aware of by looking within ourselves. And psychology is now the teaching of this inner world.

### <span class="fac">[256\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/256.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1361.</span> If I want to say that our ‘utterances,’ with which psychology deals, are by no means all descriptions of experiential content, then I must say that what is called descriptions of experiential content is only a small group of those ‘unquestionable’ utterances. But what grammatical features characterize this group?

### <span class="fac">[256\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/256.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1362.</span> An experiential content is that which a picture can represent; a picture in its subjective meaning, if it states: “_This_ is what I see – whatever the object may be that produces this impression.” For the experiential content is the private _object_. – But how can pain then form such content? – Rather, temperature sensation. And the sense of hearing is even more closely related to vision; – but it is also quite different.

### <span class="fac">[257\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/257.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1363.</span> It is as if pain has a body, as if it were a thing, a body with form and color. Why? Does it have the shape of the painful body part? One might, for example, say: “I could _describe_ the pain if I only had the necessary words and elementary meanings for it.” One feels that one only lacks the necessary nomenclature. (James.) As if one could even paint the sensation, if only the other person understood this mode of expression. – And one can, in fact, describe the pain spatially and temporally.

### <span class="fac">[257\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/257.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1364.</span> If the expression of pain were only a scream, and its intensity depended only on the available breath, and not on the injury, – would we then also be inclined to regard pain as something observed?

### <span class="fac">[257\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/257.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1365.</span> Why do you think that the _other_ person’s pain is similar to their visual sensation? – Or, why do we group visual, auditory, and tactile sensations together? Because we ‘learn about the external world’ through them? Pain could be regarded as a kind of tactile sensation.

### <span class="fac">[257\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/257.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1366.</span> But what about my idea that we do not actually judge the positions and movements of our limbs on the basis of the feelings that these movements give us? And why should we judge the surface texture of objects in the same way, if this cannot be said of our movements? – What is the _criterion_ for that, that our _feeling_ teaches us this?

### <span class="fac">[257\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/257.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1367.</span> How does one determine whether fatigue (for example) is an unclearly localized bodily feeling?

### <span class="fac">[257\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/257.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1368.</span> One might say that “I believe…” cannot _actually_ be the present tense of “I believed.” Or: one would have to be able to use a verb in such a way that its past tense has the sense of “I believed,” but its present tense has a different sense than our “I believe.” Or also: there would have to be a verb, the third person of which in the present tense has the sense of “he believes,” but the first person of which has a different sense than “I believe.” But if that is the case, should there also be a verb, the first person of which says “I believe,” but the third person of which does not mean what we mean by “he believes”? So the third person would also have to be undeniable?

### <span class="fac">[257\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/257.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1369.</span> What if someone said, “I _know_ it will not rain, but I _believe_ it will rain”?

### <span class="fac">[257\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/257.webp),[258\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/258.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1370.</span> What do sensory experiences have in common? The answer that they teach us about the external world is both false and correct. It is correct insofar as it is supposed to indicate a _logical_ criterion.

### <span class="fac">[258\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/258.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1371.</span> Could one conceive of an “I have lied” that one infers from observing one’s own behaviour? Only if the other person also cannot make the _confession_ “I have lied.” Does “I have lied” describe an experience, or “I made this statement in good faith”? You must remember that I do not infer his good faith only from his behaviour, but also assume his word for it, which he does not base on self-observation.

### <span class="fac">[258\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/258.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1372.</span> How is it that I cannot infer from my statement “It will rain” that I believe it? Can I not draw any interesting conclusions from the fact that I said it? If the other person says it, I infer, for example, that he will take an umbrella. Why not in my own case? Of course, the temptation here is to say: in one’s own case, one does not _need_ to draw this conclusion from one’s words, because one can draw it from one’s state of mind, from one’s belief itself.

### <span class="fac">[258\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/258.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1373.</span> Why do I never infer my likely actions from my words? For the same reason that I do not infer my likely behaviour from my facial expression—for it is not so much that I do not infer my state of mind from my expression of emotion, but that I do not infer my later behaviour from that expression, as others who observe me do.

### <span class="fac">[258\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/258.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1374.</span> Someone who philosophizes often makes the false, inappropriate _gesture_ for a word.

### <span class="fac">[258\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/258.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1375.</span> If someone meets me on the street and asks, “Where are you going?” and I reply, “I don’t know,” he assumes that I have no specific _intention_; not that I do not know whether I will be able to carry out my intention. (Hebel.)

### <span class="fac">[258\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/258.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1376.</span> My super-ego could say to my ego: “It is raining, and the ego believes it,” and could continue: “So the ego will probably take an umbrella.” And how does the game continue?

### <span class="fac">[259\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/259.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1377.</span> Consider also the statement: “I will probably…”—where what follows is an arbitrary, not an involuntary action.

### <span class="fac">[259\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/259.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1378.</span> One says, for example: “One _feels_ conviction; one does not infer it from one’s own words or their tone.” But what does it mean to _feel_ conviction? It is true that one does not infer one’s own conviction from one’s own words, or from the actions that spring from it.

### <span class="fac">[259\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/259.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1379.</span> In answer to the question “Why do I not infer my likely behaviour from my words?”, one could say that it is as if, as a civil servant in a ministry, I do not infer the likely decisions of the ministry from its official _utterances_, because I am, after all, familiar with the origin, the genesis of these utterances and decisions. – This case could be compared to the one where I engage in self-talk, perhaps even in writing, which leads to my spoken utterances in conversation with others; and now I say: I will not infer my future behaviour from these utterances, but from the much more reliable documents of my inner life.

### <span class="fac">[259\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/259.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1380.</span> If I am angry, I don’t need to learn it from my behaviour. – But do I infer a likely action from my anger? One could, I believe, also put it this way: I do not observe my _actions_.

### <span class="fac">[259\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/259.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1381.</span> If I say to someone “I know that you will act in such and such a way”, then the best way to make this prediction come true is to persuade the other person to perform the action.

### <span class="fac">[259\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/259.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1382.</span> If I say to someone “You will now raise your hand”, then this prediction may be sufficient reason for it not to come true; unless it is a command, and the other person respects it.

### <span class="fac">[259\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/259.webp),[260\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/260.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1383.</span> “It is raining, and I believe that it is raining.” – Turning to the weather, I say that it is raining; then, turning to myself, that I believe this. – But what do I do when I turn to myself, what do I observe? Imagine I say “It is raining, and I believe that it will soon stop” – do I turn to myself in the second part of the statement? – Yes, if I want to find out whether _he_ believes it, then I must turn to him, observe him. And if I wanted to learn what I believe through observation, I would have to observe my _actions_, just as in the other case, I would observe his. Why then do I not observe them? Are they not interesting to me? They apparently are _not_. I almost never ask another person, who has observed me, whether he has the impression that I believe this or that: namely, in order to be able to infer my actions in the future in this way. Why should a truly good observer not be able to predict my behaviour more accurately from my words and actions than I can myself? But perhaps I will only act in such a way as he predicts, if he does not predict it to me.

### <span class="fac">[260\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/260.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1384.</span> If I say “I remember that I believed…”, then do not ask “What fact, what event did he remember?” (this has already been established) – but ask: “What is the purpose of this statement, how is it used?”

### <span class="fac">[260\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/260.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1385.</span> The sense of sight, the sense of hearing, the sense of touch can fail, so that I become blind, deaf, etc.; but what would be the equivalent in the realm of intention? And how would a person without imagination behave? Or one who cannot be sad and cheerful?

### <span class="fac">[260\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/260.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1386.</span> “Hope is directed towards the future” – but is there a feeling that is identical with the feeling of hope but is directed towards the present or the past? So to speak, the same mental movement, but with a different object? Ask yourself: what would be considered the criterion of the sameness of the mental movements here? Related to this: “Is the exclamation ‘Now I can!’ a special, specific exclamation?”

### <span class="fac">[260\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/260.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1387.</span> Even if I were to concede that I know more about my own belief than about the other person’s, I would still have to say that I can know _precisely that_ about myself, which I know about the other person, even if it is much more. – So I would have to be able to apply a verb to myself in the same way that the word “believe” is applied to the other person, even if it were superfluous. What prevents me from doing so?

### <span class="fac">[260\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/260.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1388.</span> The concept of the world of consciousness. We populate a space with sensations.

### <span class="fac">[260\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/260.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1389.</span> “The ideal clock would simply always show the time ‘Now’.” It is also related to language, which only describes my impressions in the present moment. Related to this is the primal statement, which is only an unarticulated sound. (Driesch.) The ideal name, which is the word “this.”

### <span class="fac">[261\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/261.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1390.</span> I would like to talk about a family tree of psychological concepts. (Is there a similarity here with a family tree of the different number concepts?)

### <span class="fac">[261\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/261.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1391.</span> The difficulty of giving up every theory: one must consider this and that, which seems so incomplete, as something complete.

### <span class="fac">[261\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/261.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1392.</span> Fear borrows the images of dread. “I have the feeling of impending doom.”

### <span class="fac">[261\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/261.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1393.</span> But what is the content, the content of consciousness of fear? The question is wrongly put.

### <span class="fac">[261\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/261.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1394.</span> “A picture (mental image, memory-image) of longing.” One thinks that one has already done everything by talking about a ‘picture’; for longing is precisely a content of consciousness, and its picture is something that is very similar to it, albeit less clear than the original. And one could indeed say of someone who is playing longing in the theater that he experiences, or has, a picture of longing: not as an _explanation_ of his actions, but as a description of them.

### <span class="fac">[261\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/261.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1395.</span> But would I not still say that the actor experiences something similar to real longing? Is there not something to what James says: that the emotional movement consists of the feelings of the body, and therefore, at least in part, can be reproduced by arbitrary movements?

### <span class="fac">[261\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/261.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1396.</span> Is it so unpleasant, so sad, to have the corners of one’s mouth pulled down, and so pleasant to have them pulled up? What is it that is so terrible about fear? The trembling, the rapid breathing, the feeling in the facial muscles? – If you say, “This fear, this uncertainty is terrible!” – could you continue: “If only this feeling in my stomach weren’t there!”?

### <span class="fac">[261\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/261.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1397.</span> The expression “This fear is terrible!” is similar to a groan, a shout. If asked “Why are you shouting?” – would we not point to the stomach, the chest, etc., as in the case of pain; but perhaps to what is causing the fear.

### <span class="fac">[261\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/261.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1398.</span> If fear is terrible, and if I am conscious of my breathing and a tension in my facial muscles in it, – does that mean that _these feelings_ are terrible to me? Could they not even mean relief?

### <span class="fac">[262\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/262.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1399.</span> Compare fear and anxiety with worry.

### <span class="fac">[262\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/262.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1400.</span> And what is _that_ for a description: “Eternal gloom descends ‥‥”? One could describe a pain in this way; yes, even paint it.

### <span class="fac">[262\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/262.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1401.</span> Is not the ‘content’ that with which one populates the space of sensation? That which changes, unfolds in space and time. If one speaks to oneself, for example, then they would be the imagined sounds (and perhaps feelings in the throat, or something similar).

### <span class="fac">[262\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/262.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1402.</span> Is lying a specific experience? Well, can I tell someone, “I will lie to you now” and then do it?

### <span class="fac">[262\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/262.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1403.</span> In what way am I conscious of the lie while I am lying? Only insofar as it does not only come to my consciousness later, and I later know that I have lied. Being conscious of the lie is a _capability_. This does not contradict the fact that there are characteristic feelings of lying.

### <span class="fac">[262\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/262.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1404.</span> Knowledge is not simply _translated_ into words when it is expressed. The words are not a translation of something else that was there before them.

### <span class="fac">[262\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/262.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1405.</span> One says, “I can tell from his tone that he does not believe what he is saying,” or I assume it because he has generally proven to be unreliable. How can I apply this to _myself_? Can I, for example, conclude from my tone that I will probably not act according to my words? (And yet the other person does.) Or can I conclude it from my past unreliability? The latter is more likely. But I do not judge the tone of my voice in the same way as the other person's. Yes, if I could see myself later, perhaps in a talking film, I might say, “I’m not quite sure about myself.”

### <span class="fac">[262\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/262.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1406.</span> But above all: I seem to have a substitute for all such conjectures, one that is more certain than they are. I _know_ that I do not believe what I say, and that gives me the best reason—I would like to say—to assume that I will not act according to my words. Yes; I simply have an _intention_ regarding my actions.

### <span class="fac">[262\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/262.webp),[263\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1407.</span> “I know that I am lying! What do I need to draw conclusions from my tone, etc.?” – But that is not the case. Because the question is: Can I draw the same conclusions from that ‘knowledge,’ such as about the future, can I make the same _application_ of it, as from the observed signs?

### <span class="fac">[263\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1408.</span> And is the intention always _completely_ clear? I say, for example, “It will be nice”—_partly_ because I believe it, _partly_ because I want to comfort the other person.

### <span class="fac">[263\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1409.</span> Hidden motives. “I know my own, I _assume_ his.” But what _interest_, what importance, do his hidden motives have for me? (Well, think about it.) And the ‘knowledge’ of my own hidden motives plays exactly the same role for _me_, as the assumption of his hidden motives does for _him_.

### <span class="fac">[263\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1410.</span> ‘Judging oneself.’ Of course, that exists. And I also sometimes conclude that the other person is in pain because he behaves in the way that I would in this case.

### <span class="fac">[263\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1411.</span> One could say: If I tell you my hidden motives, then I am simply telling you what you would assume if you assumed his hidden motives. That is: if you assume the hidden motives, as it were, as an active principle, and I express them, then you can use my utterance directly to describe that agent. My utterance explains exactly what he wants to explain.

### <span class="fac">[263\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1412.</span> “Why should I draw conclusions about my behaviour from my own words, if I already know what I believe?” And how does it manifest that I know what I believe? Does it not manifest in the fact that I do not draw conclusions about my behaviour from my words? That is the fact.

### <span class="fac">[263\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1413.</span> Why don’t I conclude from my tone that I am not really convinced of what I say? or of all that one concludes from the latter? – And if one answers, “Because I know my _conviction_,” then the question is, “How does that show itself?” Should I say, “In the fact that I do not doubt what it is”?

### <span class="fac">[263\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1414.</span> The _knowledge_ of the meter. Whoever _knows_ the meter hears it differently.

### <span class="fac">[263\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1415.</span> There are worrisome thoughts, but not toothache-inducing ones.

### <span class="fac">[263\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1416.</span> I am now whistling a tone, but also now a melody.

### <span class="fac">[263\[11\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/263.webp),[264\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/264.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1417.</span> We do not say: “I look angry; I just hope I will not commit an act of violence.” But the question is not: “How does that come about?”

### <span class="fac">[264\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/264.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1418.</span> The psychology of judgment. For even judgment has its psychology. It is important that one can think that every judgment begins with the word “I.” “I judge that ‥‥.” So is every judgment then about the one making the judgment? Not in so far as I do not want the main consequences to be drawn about _myself_, but about the object of the judgment. If I say “It is raining,” I generally do not want one to answer “So _that’s_ how it seems to you.” “We are talking about the weather,” I could say, “not about me.”

### <span class="fac">[264\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/264.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1419.</span> “But why is the use of the verb ‘to believe,’ its grammar, composed in such a strange way?” Well, it is not composed in a _strange_ way. It is only strange if one compares it with the grammar of the word “to eat,” for example.

### <span class="fac">[264\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/264.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1420.</span> “What will he probably do now,” I say, while watching him. Do I consider myself and say, “What will I probably do now”?

### <span class="fac">[264\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/264.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1421.</span> Suppose I move in a room, and have a screen in front of my eyes, on which I see myself, as an observer would see me. While moving in the room, I always only look at the screen and observe my actions on it. – What would be the difference between the two cases: a) I am guided by what I see on the screen, as by normal seeing of my surroundings – – b) I move _involuntarily_ and observe myself as a stranger. But do I not feel my movements? – But is this feeling not _happening_ to me, like any other sense impression?

### <span class="fac">[264\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/264.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1422.</span> Now then: the kinesthetic is a _different_, a special feeling. – But so are smell, hearing, etc. – Why does that make such a difference? “Innervation feeling” – that expresses what one wants to say: that it is like an _impulse_. But a feeling like an impulse?! What is an impulse? A physical picture. The picture of a jolt.

### <span class="fac">[264\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/264.webp),[265\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1423.</span> What is the difference between these two: Following a line involuntarily – – Following a line with intention. What is the difference between these two: Tracing a line with care and great attention – – Attentively observing how my hand follows a line.

### <span class="fac">[265\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1424.</span> Certain differences are easily indicated. One lies in the foreseeing of what the hand will do.

### <span class="fac">[265\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1425.</span> Is “I do my best” the expression of an experience? – _One_ difference: One says “Do your best!”

### <span class="fac">[265\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1426.</span> Does one say: “Give yourself this muscular feeling!”? And why not? – “This”? – “Which one? – – But can I not give myself a specific muscular feeling by simply moving my arm? – Try it! Move your arm, – and ask yourself what feeling you have evoked. If someone said to me, “Bend your arm and evoke the characteristic feeling,” and I bend my arm, then I would have to ask him now: “Which one did you mean? A slight tension in the biceps, or a feeling in the skin on the inside of the elbow joint?” Yes, I could, if someone gives me a command to move, do so, and then describe the sensations that it produces, and their specific location (which would almost never be the joint). And I would often also have to say that I felt _nothing_. But one must not confuse that with the statement that it was as if my limb were _numb_.

### <span class="fac">[265\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1427.</span> Do you read the page arbitrarily? And what is the _act_ here? – Someone can read on command, and stop reading. One can also imagine something on command. For example, recite a poem in one’s imagination, do a calculation. Do you _feel_, when imagining, whether you are imagining something arbitrarily or involuntarily? One can, on command, evoke thoughts, evoke images – but also, and this is something different, think something on command, imagine something.

### <span class="fac">[265\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1428.</span> Images, one might say, are voluntary; afterimages involuntary.

### <span class="fac">[265\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1429.</span> Involuntary is, _e.g._, the movement that one cannot prevent; or the one of which one knows nothing; or, the one that happens when one deliberately relaxes one’s muscles in order not to influence the movement.

### <span class="fac">[265\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1430.</span> When I see the other person eating, do I ask myself whether he is doing it voluntarily or involuntarily? One might say that I simply assume that it is happening voluntarily. What do I assume; that he feels it? And feels it in a certain way?

### <span class="fac">[266\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1431.</span> How do I know whether the child is eating, drinking, walking, etc. voluntarily or involuntarily? Do I ask him what he feels? No; eating, as everyone eats, _is_ voluntary.

### <span class="fac">[266\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1432.</span> If someone were to tell us, _he_ eats involuntarily, – what evidence would make me believe this?

### <span class="fac">[266\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1433.</span> If I raise my hand suddenly to protect my eye, – is the movement voluntary? – And do I feel it differently from a voluntary one?

### <span class="fac">[266\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1434.</span> The concept of ‘effort’. Do you feel the effort? Of course, you feel it. But don’t you also _make_ it? – What are the signs of effort? I lift a heavy weight with great effort. My muscles are tense, my face is contorted, my breath is held – – but do I do that; doesn’t it just happen to me? What if it just happened to me? How would this differ from the case of wanting? Would I perhaps speak differently? Would I say: “I don’t know what’s happening to me: my muscles are tense, my face, etc. etc.”? And if I said: “Now, relax your muscles,” he would answer “I can’t.” But what if someone said to me: “I feel that I _must_ do whatever I do,” and that he behaves like everyone else?

### <span class="fac">[266\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1435.</span> Isn’t saying that the kinesthetic feeling shows me the movement that is being made analogous to the view that a characteristic of pain shows me its location?

### <span class="fac">[266\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1436.</span> If someone wanted to represent pain with a _colored image_, – would he include a vocal sign in the image? And why not?

### <span class="fac">[266\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1437.</span> Isn’t sensation the measure of effort? I.e.: When I say “I am pulling harder now,” do I notice it in the degree of sensation? And what is to be said against this? One says to someone “Exert yourself more!” – not so that he feels more, but so that he achieves more.

### <span class="fac">[266\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1438.</span> Why does one feel that one can describe a tactile sensation (its content), paint it, but not a movement or position sensation?

### <span class="fac">[266\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1439.</span> Can you say, for example, that your position sensation is weak or strong? And your sensations during the movement of a limb can be stronger or weaker (or absent), but that is not a perception of the movement.

### <span class="fac">[266\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/266.webp),[267\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/267.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1440.</span> Movement sensations – these are sensations that are caused by movements – can be, for example, pain. How does one know that it is not these movement sensations that teach us how to move? What would be a sign that this is _the_ case?

### <span class="fac">[267\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/267.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1441.</span> Isn’t it an important fact that the theater presents us with colors and sounds, but not with tactile sensations? One could imagine the use of smells and temperature sensations, but not the use of tactile sensations.

### <span class="fac">[267\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/267.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1442.</span> Someone, who with apparent attention threads a needle and tells us that he is doing it _involuntarily_. How could he justify this statement?

### <span class="fac">[267\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/267.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1443.</span> One can be convinced of what one can know, – and one can also guess it. (Grammatical remark.)

### <span class="fac">[267\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/267.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1444.</span> Certain movements, along with their normal _environment_, are associated with intention, learning, trying, and acting. Movements for which it makes sense to say that they are sometimes voluntary and sometimes involuntary are movements in a specific environment.

### <span class="fac">[267\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/267.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1445.</span> A category of psychological phenomena (facts) would be ‘_seeds_’. But this word can just as easily be the expression of a misunderstanding as the word “tendency experience” (James). The word “board game move” also does not characterize a _type of movement_.

### <span class="fac">[267\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/267.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1446.</span> Translating from one language to another is a mathematical task, and translating a lyrical poem, for example, into a foreign language is entirely analogous to a mathematical _problem_. Because one can certainly pose the problem “How is this joke (for example) to be translated into the other language,” that is, replaced; and the problem _can_ also be solved; but there was no method, no system, for its solution.

### <span class="fac">[267\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/267.webp)</span>
