<span class="series-number">1447.</span> You know that you are lying; you know it when you lie. An inner voice, a feeling, tells me this? Could this feeling not deceive me? Does a voice always tell me this? And when does it speak? All the time? – And how do I know that I can trust it?

### <span class="fac">[267\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/267.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1448.</span> A lie has a particular environment. There is primarily a motive. An inducement.

### <span class="fac">[267\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/267.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1449.</span> The consciousness of lying is of the same category as the consciousness of intention.

### <span class="fac">[268\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/268.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1450.</span> Don’t forget: sight, hearing, smell, taste, etc., are only sensations because these concepts have _something_ in common – – just as one could group together drills, chisels, axes, and blowtorches, because they have _certain_ functions in common.

### <span class="fac">[268\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/268.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1451.</span> “Pain, tone, taste, smell have a certain color.” What does that mean? (Quality. Adjective.) A color can be green**ish** or blue**ish** – there is a mixture of colors; and so also a mixture of smells, sounds, tastes; qualitative intermediate stages. How does one distinguish qualitative from quantitative intermediate stages, i.e., from stages of ‘intensity’? Still bearable – no longer bearable, these are, for example, degrees of intensity. Imagine someone asking: “How can I know whether what I experience as different degrees, of loudness, for example, is experienced by another person as different qualities, comparable to different colors?” – Compare the reaction to a change in strength with the reaction to a change in quality.

### <span class="fac">[268\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/268.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1452.</span> I feel my arm and, strangely enough, I would like to say: I feel it in space in a certain position; as if the feeling of the body were distributed in space in the shape of the arm, so that in order to represent it, I would have to represent the arm, for example, in plaster, in its correct position.

### <span class="fac">[268\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/268.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1453.</span> Imagine a pencil point being brought into contact with my skin at some point; I can then say that I feel where it is. But do I feel _where_ I feel it? “How do you know that the point is now touching your thigh?” – “I feel it.” By feeling the touch, I know its location; but should I therefore speak of a sense of location? And if there is no sense of location, why should there be a feeling of position?

### <span class="fac">[268\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/268.webp),[269\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/269.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1454.</span> Yes, it is odd. My forearm is now lying horizontally and I would like to say that I feel this; but not in the way that I have a feeling that always accompanies this position (as one feels, for example, numbness, or plethora) – but as if the ‘feeling of the body’ of the arm is arranged or distributed horizontally, like a mist or dust particles distributed in space on the surface of my arm. So it is not really as if I feel the position of my arm, but as if I feel my _arm_, and the feeling has that and that _position_. That is to say, I simply _know_ how it lies – without knowing it, _because_… How I also know where I feel the pain – but I don’t know it _because_…

### <span class="fac">[269\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/269.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1455.</span> Consider: – “It is not true that I always believe the wrong thing. For example, it is raining now, and I believe it.” One could say of him: He speaks as if he were _two_ people.

### <span class="fac">[269\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/269.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1456.</span> Why do I have doubts about his intention, but not about my own? In what way do I know my intention without doubt? What, in a way, is the use of knowing my intention? What, namely, is the use, the function, of the utterance of intention? When, namely, is it an utterance of intention? Yes, when the act follows it, when it is a prediction. I make the prediction, the same one that the other makes from observing my behaviour, _without_ this observation.

### <span class="fac">[269\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/269.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1457.</span> When it comes to a ‘feeling of unreality,’ we are inclined to say: ‘All I know is that people often say, under certain circumstances, that they feel that everything around them is ‘unreal.’ We also know, of course, how the use of this word has been conditioned, and also something about their other utterances. That is all we know.’ – Why don’t we also speak like this when it comes to the utterances of pleasure, conviction, the arbitrariness and non-arbitrariness of movements?

### <span class="fac">[269\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/269.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1458.</span> What should I answer to someone who tells me that _he_ feels the position and movement of his limbs, that a _feeling_ tells him their position and movement? Should I say that he is lying, or that he is mistaken, or should I believe him? I would like to ask him _how_ a feeling teaches him this position, for example. Or better: how he _knows_ that his _feeling_ teaches him this.

### <span class="fac">[269\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/269.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1459.</span> (One says the _ordinary_ thing, – with the wrong gesture.)

### <span class="fac">[269\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/269.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1460.</span> Remember here again the feeling without justification and, apparently, without reason that a certain location must lie in _that_ direction. If this feeling did not mostly deceive us, one would speak here of a feeling-based _knowledge_. And the sources of this feeling can only be _conjectured_, or established empirically.

### <span class="fac">[269\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/269.webp),[270\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/270.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1461.</span> The most important thing here is that one can be aware of a difference, which is a categorical one, without being able to say what the difference consists in. This is the case in which one usually says that one recognizes the difference by means of introspection.

### <span class="fac">[270\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/270.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1462.</span> And yet it sounds too much like an appeal to introspection if I were to say: ‘Examine yourself – whether you really determine the position of your limbs by feelings in them!’ – And it would also be wrong, because the question is: how would this show itself if someone did it? Because if, after a self-examination, he assured me that it is so, or that it is not so, – how do I know whether I can trust him; I mean, whether he has also understood me correctly. Or also: How do I check whether I understand him?

### <span class="fac">[270\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/270.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1463.</span> Someone says to me: ‘I don’t know how I move my fingers, but I know that I spread them by the feeling in my webs.’ Here one would have to ask: Can you then not easily carry out the command ‘Spread your fingers’ with your eyes closed?

### <span class="fac">[270\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/270.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1464.</span> We feel our movements. Yes, we really _feel_ them; the sensation is not similar to a taste sensation or a heat sensation, but rather a tactile sensation: the sensation when skin and muscles are pressed, pulled, or shifted.

### <span class="fac">[270\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/270.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1465.</span> How can I use the feeling of movement in my movements? For how can I, before the movement has begun, select from all the muscles those that will give me the correct feeling of movement? – If it is a problem, “How do I know, when I don’t see the movement, that it has taken place, and how far it has gone?” – why is it not a problem: “How do I know how to initiate the, say, commanded movement?” (Russell once made a false remark about this.)

### <span class="fac">[270\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/270.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1466.</span> I can, for example, say that I now know that my finger is bent, but that I have no feeling in it; in any case, not one that I particularly associate with this position. If one were to ask me: “Do you feel anything that you would say you would not feel in the extended position; or is there a sensation missing that would be present in the other position?” – I would have to answer no.

### <span class="fac">[270\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/270.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1467.</span> “Is pleasure a sensation?” (I.A. Richards). This means something like: Is pleasure something like a tone or a smell? – But is a tone something like a smell? In what respect?

### <span class="fac">[271\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/271.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1468.</span> Whoever asks whether pleasure is a sensation probably does not distinguish between ground and cause, because otherwise he would notice that one has pleasure _in_ something, which does not mean that this something causes a sensation in us.

### <span class="fac">[271\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/271.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1469.</span> But pleasure, in any case, is associated with a facial expression, and although we do not see it in ourselves, we do feel it. And try thinking about something very sad with a facial expression of radiant joy!

### <span class="fac">[271\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/271.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1470.</span> It is possible that the glands of the sad person secrete differently from those of the cheerful person; also, that this secretion is the, or one of the, causes of sadness. But does it follow that sadness is a sensation caused by this secretion?

### <span class="fac">[271\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/271.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1471.</span> But the thought here is: “You _feel_ sadness – so you must _feel_ it _somewhere_; otherwise it would be a chimera.” But if you want to think that, just recall the difference between seeing and pain. I feel the pain in my hand – and the color in my eye? Just as we want to use a schema here, instead of simply noting what is actually in common, we see everything falsely simplified.

### <span class="fac">[271\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/271.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1472.</span> A smell can be very pleasant. Is the pleasantness in it only a sensation? Then the sensation of pleasantness would accompany the smell. But how would it _relate_ to it? Of course, the expression of pleasantness is, in _its_ nature, similar to the expression of a sensation, in particular of pain. But joy has no location; there are joyful thoughts, but not toothache-inducing ones. But – one might say – whether joy is a sensation, or _what_ it is, one must notice when one has it! – (And why especially when one _has_ it, and not when one _doesn’t have_ it?) Do you also notice the _essence_ of one when you eat _an_ apple, and the _essence_ of zero when you eat none?

### <span class="fac">[271\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/271.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1473.</span> Volition is connected with intentionality. And therefore also with resolve. One does not resolve to have a heart cramp and then have it.

### <span class="fac">[272\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/272.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1474.</span> One can induce a sneeze or a coughing fit, but not a voluntary movement. And the will does not induce the sneeze, nor does it induce walking.

### <span class="fac">[272\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/272.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1475.</span> Sensation, that is what one considers to be immediately given and concrete, what one only needs to look at in order to recognize it; what is really there. (The thing, not its messenger.)

### <span class="fac">[272\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/272.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1476.</span> “I know whether I am speaking in accordance with my conviction, or against it.” Thus, the conviction is the important thing. In the background of my utterances. What a _powerful_ image. One could paint conviction and speech (“from the deepest heart”). And yet, how little does this image show!

### <span class="fac">[272\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/272.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1477.</span> “The smell is wonderful!” Is there any doubt that it is the smell that is wonderful? So is it a property of the smell? – Why not? It is a property that ten is divisible by two, and also that it is the number of my fingers. But there could be a language in which people simply close their eyes and say “Oh, this smell!” and there is no subject-predicate sentence that is equivalent to it. That is just a ‘specific’ reaction.

### <span class="fac">[272\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/272.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1478.</span> Is what he says he has, & what I say I have, without us inferring this from any observation, the same as what we gather from observing the other’s **behaviour** & from his expression of conviction?

### <span class="fac">[272\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/272.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1479.</span> Can one say: I _infer_ that he will act as he _intends_ to act?

### <span class="fac">[272\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/272.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1480.</span> I infer the consequences of his conviction from the expression of his conviction; but not the consequences of my conviction from its expression.

### <span class="fac">[272\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/272.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1481.</span> Imagine an observer who, as if automatically, utters his observations. Yes, he hears himself speak, but as it were, he takes no notice of it. He sees that the enemy is approaching and reports it, describes it, but like a machine. What would that be like? Well, he does not act according to his observation. One could say that he expresses what he sees, but he does not _believe_ it. It doesn’t, as it were, penetrate him.

### <span class="fac">[272\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/272.webp),[273\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/273.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1482.</span> Why do I not infer a state from my own words, from which words and actions spring? Above all, I do not infer my probable actions from my words.

### <span class="fac">[273\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/273.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1483.</span> Asked “Will you act in that way?” – I consider _reasons_ and _counter-reasons_.

### <span class="fac">[273\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/273.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1484.</span> But consider: “I sometimes take the other person’s word for it – so I must at least sometimes take my own word for it, that I have such and such a conviction.” But if I report my observation in a quasi-automatic way, this report has nothing to do with my conviction. But I could trust myself, or my observing self, just as much as another person does. I could say: “I say ‘it is raining,’ so it probably is.” Or: “The observer in me says ‘it is raining,’ and I am inclined to believe him.” – Isn’t it so – or similar – when a person says that God has spoken to him, or through his mouth?

### <span class="fac">[273\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/273.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1485.</span> The _important_ insight is that there is a language-game in which I, _automatically_, make a communication, which can be treated by others in exactly the same way as a non-automatic one – – only here there will be no talk of ‘lying’ – a communication that I myself can receive as if it came from another. One could also call the ‘automatic’ statement, report, etc., an ‘oracle’. – But this, of course, means that the oracle must not use the words “I believe…”.

### <span class="fac">[273\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/273.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1486.</span> Where in logic does it say that an assertion must not be made in a trance?!

### <span class="fac">[273\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/273.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1487.</span> “If I look out, I see that it is raining; if I look inward, I see that I don’t believe it.” And what are we supposed to do with this communication?

### <span class="fac">[273\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/273.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1488.</span> “Suppose it is raining, and I don’t believe it” – if I assert what this assumption assumes, then, as it were, my personality splits. “Then my personality splits” means: then I am no longer playing the ordinary language-game, but a different one.

### <span class="fac">[273\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/273.webp),[274\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/274.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1489.</span> “The words ‘It is raining’ are written in his mind” – this is meant to say as much as (i.e., be replaceable by) “He believes that it is raining”. “The words ‘It is raining’ are written in my mind” – this means something like: “I cannot rid myself of the belief that …”, “The idea has taken hold of me that …”. Note, namely, that the words “I believe it is raining” and “It is probably raining” _**the same**_ can say: _insofar_ as, in certain contexts, it makes no difference which of the two sentences we use. (And free yourself from the idea that one is accompanied by a different mental process than the other!) The two sentences can say the same thing, although the first corresponds to “I believe…”, “He believes…”, etc., while the second does not. The first is simply formed with a different _Begriff_. That is: in order to say that it might rain, we do _not_ need the concept of “believe”, even if we can use it for that purpose. The concept that a sentence is ‘written in one’s mind’ is now a third concept, which in application partially overlaps with the others, partially does not. I mean that in order to form the statement “It might…”, one does not need the ‘odd’ concept of ‘believe’, although one can use it.

### <span class="fac">[274\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/274.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1490.</span> Also, consider: ‘It probably is raining and it is raining’ means nothing, and so does ‘It probably is raining and it is not raining.’ On the other hand, one can say ‘It seems to be raining and it is raining’ and also ‘It seems ‥‥ and it is not raining.’ And ‘It seems to be raining’ can have the same sense as ‘It probably is raining.’

### <span class="fac">[274\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/274.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1491.</span> How do I know that I am believing? ‥‥? Do I look _inward_? Yes, is it of _any use_ to me if I observe myself? Well, I might ask myself: “How much would I bet on this?”

### <span class="fac">[274\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/274.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1492.</span> Pretending. Feigning pain. It does not simply consist in uttering an expression of pain without feeling pain. There must be a motive for the pretense, i.e., a situation that is not simply describable. Pretending to be ill and weak in order to then attack the helper. – “But there is an _inner_ difference!” Of course; only “inner” is here a dangerous metaphor. – But the ‘proof’ that there is an inner difference is that I can _confess_ that I pretended. I confess an intention. Does it ‘follow’ that the intention was something inner?

### <span class="fac">[274\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/274.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1493.</span> The ‘truly infinite’ is a ‘mere word.’ It would be better to say: this expression provisionally creates only a picture, which is still up in the air; the application of which you still owe us.

### <span class="fac">[275\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/275.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1494.</span> An infinitely long row of balls, an infinitely long rod. Imagine that this is the subject of a kind of fairy tale. What application, even if only fictitious, could one make of this concept? The question now is not: can such a thing exist? But: what do we imagine? So let your imagination really run wild! You can have it now, however you want. You only need to _say_ how you want it. So just create a word-picture; illustrate it however you want – with drawings, with comparisons, etc.! You can, as it were, create a working drawing. And now the question is, how can one work according to it.

### <span class="fac">[275\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/275.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1495.</span> “But how can the human mind outstrip reality and even _think_ what cannot be verified?” – Why shouldn’t we _talk_ about what cannot be verified? We ourselves made it unverifiable. Does it create a false _appearance_? And how could it even only _seem_ that way? Do you want to say that this _state_ is not even a description? Well, then it is not a _false_ appearance, but rather one that deprives us of our bearings. So we ask: How is it possible?

### <span class="fac">[275\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/275.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1496.</span> As soon as the word was spoken, I wished I hadn’t said it. – How did my wish relate to the spoken word? I felt that the word was inappropriate as soon as I had spoken it. But the _signs_ I remember were only like faint hints. Small things from which I could perhaps have _guessed_ the intention, the wish, etc. There are shamanic occasions – situations – and shameful behaviour. Just as there are occasions and behaviour that evoke expectation.

### <span class="fac">[275\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/275.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1497.</span> When a cat lies in wait in front of a mouse hole – may one assume that it is thinking about the mouse? When a robber waits for his victim – does it necessarily involve him thinking about this person? _Must_ he consider this and that in the process? Compare someone who is doing this for the first time with someone who has done it countless times! (read)

### <span class="fac">[275\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/275.webp),[276\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/276.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1498.</span> There could be a verb that means: to express an intention through words, or other signs, aloud, or in thought. This verb would not be synonymous with our “to intend.” There could be a verb that means: to act in accordance with an intention; and this would also not be synonymous with “to intend.” Another could mean: to ponder an intention; or, to turn it over and over in one’s head.

### <span class="fac">[276\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/276.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1499.</span> When I prepare my coffee, I intend to drink it. If I now prepared it without this intention – would something be _**missing**_ from this action? Is something going on during normal activity that characterizes it as an action done with this intention? If someone asked me whether I intended to drink it, and I answered “yes, of course!” – would I be _saying_ something about my current state? _This_ is how I react in this case; and _that_ can be inferred from my reaction.

### <span class="fac">[276\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/276.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1500.</span> One can call a belief, wish, fear, hope, affection a state of a person; we can rely on this state in our behaviour towards this person, and infer his reactions from his state. And if someone says “I was all that time in the belief ‥‥”, “I harboured all my life the wish ‥‥”, etc., then he reports on a state, an attitude. – But if he says “I believe he is coming” (or simply “He is coming”) or “I wish you would come” (or simply “Please come”), then he acts, speaks, in accordance with _that_ state, and does not report that he is in it. But if that were true, then there should be a present form of those reports, i.e., on the one hand, the _utterance_ “I believe ‥‥”, and on the other hand, a _report_ “I am in the belief ‥‥”. And the same for the wish, the intention, fear, etc.

### <span class="fac">[276\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/276.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1501.</span> Someone could say: “I remember my state in those years very precisely; whenever I was asked ‥‥, I answered ‥‥; that was my attitude.”

### <span class="fac">[276\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/276.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1502.</span> There is a disgust **reaction** in me and in the other, there are also _feelings of disgust_. And in this respect, disgust, fear, affection, etc., are alike; but not hope, belief, etc.

### <span class="fac">[276\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/276.webp),[277\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/277.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1503.</span> _Gram_ endlessly repeats the sad thought. A thought can be sad, disgusting, delightful, etc.; but how does the expression show that it is this thought that we are reacting to in this way? How does one reject a thought?

### <span class="fac">[277\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/277.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1504.</span> Should I call the entire area of the psychological the area of ‘experience’? So, for example, all psychological verbs would be ‘experience verbs’. (‘Experience concepts’.) Their characteristic is that their third person is expressed on the basis of observations, but not the first person. That observation is observation of behaviour. A subclass of the experience concepts are the ‘experience concepts’. ‘Experiences’ have duration, a course; they can proceed uniformly or nonuniformly. They have intensity. They are not characteristics of thoughts. Imagination is experience. A subclass of the ‘experiences’ are the ‘impressions’. Impressions have spatial and temporal relationships to each other. There are mixed impressions. E.g. mixtures of smells, colours, sounds. ‘Emotional states’ are ‘experiences’, but are not ‘experiences’. (Examples: sadness, joy, grief, delight.) And one could distinguish ‘directed emotional states’ and ‘undirected emotional states’. The emotional state has duration; it has no location; it has characteristic experiences and thoughts; it has a characteristic _mimic_ expression. Thinking is talking under certain circumstances, and other things that correspond to it. Emotional states colour thoughts. A subclass of the ‘experiences’ are the forms of ‘conviction’. (Belief, certainty, doubt, etc.) Their expression is an expression of thoughts. They are not ‘colourings’ of thoughts. The directed emotional states could also be called “statements”. Surprise and fright are also statements, as are admiration and enjoyment.

### <span class="fac">[277\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/277.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1505.</span> But where do memory and attention belong? One can remember a situation or event in a _moment_. In this respect, the concept of remembering is similar to that of momentary understanding, of deciding.

### <span class="fac">[277\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/277.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1506.</span> My behaviour is sometimes the object of my observation, but still _rarely_. And this is connected with the fact that I intend my behaviour. Even when the actor in the mirror observes his own expressions, or the musician pays close attention to every note of his playing and judges it, it is still done in order to regulate his action accordingly.

### <span class="fac">[278\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/278.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1507.</span> What does it mean, for example, that self-observation makes my action, my movements, uncertain? I cannot observe myself without being observed. And I do not observe myself for the same purpose as I observe others.

### <span class="fac">[278\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/278.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1508.</span> When a child stamps its feet and cries in anger, who would say that it does so involuntarily? And why? Why does one assume that it does not do so involuntarily? What are the _signs_ of voluntary action? Are there such signs? – What are the signs of involuntary movement? It does not follow commands, as does voluntary action. There is a “Come here!”, “Go there!”, “Make this arm movement!”; but not “Let your heart beat fast!”

### <span class="fac">[278\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/278.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1509.</span> There is a specific interaction of movements, words, expressions, such as the expressions of unwillingness or willingness, that characterise the voluntary movements of a normal person. When one calls the child, it does not come automatically: There are, for example, the gesture “I don’t want to!” Or the joyful coming, the decision to come, the running away with the sign of fear, the effects of persuasion, all the reactions of play, the signs of consideration and its effects.

### <span class="fac">[278\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/278.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1510.</span> A melody went through my head. Was it voluntary, or involuntary? One answer would be: I could have stopped it, and sung it inwardly. And how do I know that? Well, because I can usually interrupt myself when I want to.

### <span class="fac">[278\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/278.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1511.</span> How could I prove to myself that I can move my arm voluntarily? Perhaps by saying to myself, “I will move it now,” and then it moves? Or should I say, “Simply by moving it”? But how do I know that I have done it and that it didn’t just move by chance? Do I feel it in the end? And what if my memory of previous feelings deceives me, and so it wasn’t the right, decisive feelings at all?! (And which are the right ones?) And how does the other person know whether _I_ moved my arm voluntarily? I might tell him, “Tell me what movement you want, and I will make it, in order to convince you.” – And what do you feel in your arm? “Well, the usual thing.” There is nothing unusual about the feelings; the arm is not, for example, numb (as if it had ‘fallen asleep’).

### <span class="fac">[278\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/278.webp),[279\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/279.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1512.</span> A movement of my body, of which I am not aware that it is taking place, or has taken place, will be called involuntary. – But what if I merely _try_ to lift a weight, so that the movement does not actually take place? What if someone were to strain involuntarily to lift a weight? Under what circumstances would one call _this_ behaviour ‘involuntary’?

### <span class="fac">[279\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/279.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1513.</span> Can not rest be just as voluntary as movement? Can the omission of movement be voluntary? What better argument against an innervation feeling?

### <span class="fac">[279\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/279.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1514.</span> “This look was not intended” sometimes means: “I didn’t know that I was looking like that,” or “I didn’t mean anything by it.”

### <span class="fac">[279\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/279.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1515.</span> It should not seem so obvious to us that memory shows us the past inner process just as much as the past outer process.

### <span class="fac">[279\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/279.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1516.</span> Imagination is voluntary, memory is involuntary, but recalling something to memory is voluntary.

### <span class="fac">[279\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/279.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1517.</span> What a remarkable concept ‘trying’, ‘endeavouring’ is; what one can ‘try to do’! (Remember, lift a weight, pay attention, think of nothing.) But then one could also say: what a remarkable concept ‘doing’ is! What are the relationships of kinship between ‘speaking’ and ‘thinking’, between ‘speaking’ and ‘talking to oneself’? (Compare the relationships of kinship between the types of numbers.)

### <span class="fac">[279\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/279.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1518.</span> One draws quite different conclusions from involuntary movement than from voluntary movement: this _characterizes_ the voluntary movement.

### <span class="fac">[279\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/279.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1519.</span> But how do I know that this movement was voluntary? – I don’t know it; I express it.

### <span class="fac">[279\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/279.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1520.</span> “I pull as hard as I can.” How do I know that? Does my muscle feeling tell me? The words are a signal; and they have a _function_. But do I not _experience_ anything? Do I not experience something? something specific? A specific feeling of exertion and of not being able to go any further, of reaching the limit? Of course, but these expressions do not say more than “I pull as hard as I can.”

### <span class="fac">[279\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/279.webp),[280\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/280.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1521.</span> But it is important that all these paraphrases exist! That one can describe the worry with the words “Eternal gloom descends.” I may never have sufficiently emphasized the importance of this paraphrasing. One depicts joy by a face illuminated with light, by rays emanating from it. Of course, this does not mean that joy and light are _similar_; but we associate – _regardless_ of why – joy with light. It _could_ be that this association is taught to the child when it learns to speak, that it is not _more natural_ than the sound of the words themselves – – enough, that it exists. (“Beethoven” and Beethoven's works.)

### <span class="fac">[280\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/280.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1522.</span> Is grief similar to a lead-gray sky?! And how can one find that out? By looking at the grieving person and the sky? Or does the grieving person say it? And is it then only true for _his_ grief, or for the grief of everyone?

### <span class="fac">[280\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/280.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1523.</span> But if someone says that _his_ grief is like a gray cloud, – should I believe it, or not? – One could ask him whether the two are similar _in something_, in a certain respect. (As, for example, two faces; or as a sudden, sharp pain is to a flare-up.) One can specify relationships – internal relationships and _connections_ – of what one calls ‘intensities’ in different sensations.

### <span class="fac">[280\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/280.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1524.</span> ‘a is between b and c, and closer to b than to c’ this is a characteristic relation between sensations of the same kind. That is to say, there is, for example, a language-game with the instruction “Create a sensation between _this_ and _this_, and closer to the first than to the second!” And also: “Name two sensations, between which _this_ lies.”

### <span class="fac">[280\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/280.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1525.</span> And it is important that, for example, with _gray_ one will get “black and white” as the answer; with _violet_ “blue and red”, with _pink_ “red and white”, etc.; but _not_ with _olive green_ “red and green”.

### <span class="fac">[280\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/280.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1526.</span> How does one recognize that the expression of joy is not the expression of a bodily pain? (An _important_ question.)

### <span class="fac">[280\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/280.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1527.</span> How does one know that the expression of pleasure is not that of a sensation?

### <span class="fac">[280\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/280.webp),[281\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/281.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1528.</span> To address a figure as this or as that. Do you always address the figure, while you see it, as this or that? Certainly: when asked what this figure represents, I would always say: “A rabbit”; but I am so little constantly conscious of this, as of the fact that this here is a real table. For if I always describe a picture as the picture of _this_ object, then also every object as a thing of this particular use, etc..

### <span class="fac">[281\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/281.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1529.</span> If someone notices for the first time that the picture is ambiguous, he might react with the exclamation: “Ah, a rabbit!” etc.; but if he now sees the picture constantly in one aspect, he would not continuously want to exclaim “Ah, a ‥․!”

### <span class="fac">[281\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/281.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1530.</span> I want to say that the natural, primitive, expression of the experience of the aspect would be such an exclamation; it could also be a lighting up of the eyes. (I notice something!)

### <span class="fac">[281\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/281.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1531.</span> If I say that I constantly see this figure red, that means that the description, that it is red – the description in words or through a picture – is constantly, without change, correct; in contrast, therefore, to _that_ case, in which the figure changes. – The temptation is indeed to describe the aspect with the words “I see it _like this_” without pointing to something. And if one describes a face with its gaze as an arrow, one wants to say: “I see this: 🖵 and not this:🖵”.

### <span class="fac">[281\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/281.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1532.</span> To constant seeing corresponds then the fact that this description, without change, is the correct one and _that_ only means that the aspect has not changed.

### <span class="fac">[281\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/281.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1533.</span> Talk of hallucination! – What could be stranger than that the _point_, the eye, seems to have direction!

### <span class="fac">[281\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/281.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1534.</span> If I think about the facial expression of this figure, – how do I do it, to think about the expression of 🖵 and not of 🖵?

### <span class="fac">[281\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/281.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1535.</span> If I think about the facial expression of this figure, I look at it – how do I do it: to look at the expression of 🖵, not the one of 🖵? And this symbolism, I believe, already contains everything.

### <span class="fac">[281\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/281.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1536.</span> It is as if one saw the picture: sometimes, together with _one_ group of pictures, at other times with another _group_. What does “it is as if one saw” mean here? This means something similar to: _this_ process could represent the actual one, if it had the right ‘multiplicity’.

### <span class="fac">[281\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/281.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1537.</span> It is – in contrast to Köhler – precisely a meaning that one sees.

### <span class="fac">[282\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/282.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1538.</span> One could say that one experiences the readiness for a certain group of thoughts. (The germ of them.)

### <span class="fac">[282\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/282.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1539.</span> It is as if the picture comes to rest in a certain position (or in another). As if it could, in fact, fluctuate, and then come to rest with _certain_ accents. One says: “I see it now (or, mostly) as _that_.” It is truly as if the strokes have now coalesced into _this_ and not some other form. Or as if they have fallen into _this_ and not some other hollow. And yet, it must only be a matter of describing the actual expression of our experience, which I am only paraphrasing with all these images; to say what the _essential_ thing about this expression is.

### <span class="fac">[282\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/282.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1540.</span> Could someone see the _figure_ _like this_, or _like that_, without being able to use it to arrive at _explanations_, etc.? Could someone, therefore, see it _like this_ & _like that_, without knowing what animal heads look like, what an eye is, etc.? And by that, of course, I don’t mean: “If such a person were capable of doing it, would they succeed?” Rather: “Doesn’t it require _these concepts_?”

### <span class="fac">[282\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/282.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1541.</span> I see the picture of a horse: I don’t just know that it is a horse, but also that the horse is running. So I can not only _spatially understand_ the picture, but I also _know_ what the horse is about to do. Imagine someone sees a picture of a cavalry attack, but doesn’t know that the horses are not standing still in their various positions! It is not about an _explanation_ of this understanding, for example, by claiming that the observer makes small running movements, or feels running innervations. What reason does one have for making assumptions of this kind, other than _the_ fact that it ‘must’ be so?

### <span class="fac">[282\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/282.webp),[283\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/283.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1542.</span> But what if one says “One _sees_ this painted horse running!” – Surely, one does not only want to say “I know that this represents a running horse.” One wants to say something _else_ with it. Imagine someone reacted to such a picture with a hand movement & the exclamation “Hui!” Does that not say approximately the same as: he _saw_ the horse running? He could also _exclaim_ “It’s running!” & that would not be the statement that it is running, nor that it _appears_ to be running. Just as one says: “Look how it runs!” – not in order to give the other person a communication, but it _is_ a reaction in which people _find_ themselves.

### <span class="fac">[283\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/283.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1543.</span> Understanding is similar to knowing more, thus a skill: but “I understand,” just like “I know more,” is an utterance, a signal.

### <span class="fac">[283\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/283.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1544.</span> I can experience a word adjectivally, or substantively. Do I know whether everyone, whether many, with whom I talk, have these experiences? Would it be important in order to know what they mean?

### <span class="fac">[283\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/283.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1545.</span> I hadn’t noticed that the same contour appeared in both pictures, because I had understood it in one picture _in one way_, in the other _in another way_. Only through a roundabout consideration did I realize that it was the same contour. – Is this a proof: that each time I saw something _different_? – It is important that the two aspects are incompatible with each other.

### <span class="fac">[283\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/283.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1546.</span> Is facial expression something visual? I could imagine a picture whose _expression_ is ambiguous. And that I would not recognize it in a _different_ environment. I might then say: “Ah yes, these are the same lines; but they look completely different here.” And I really _see_ that picture 🖵 and picture 🖵 are the same. I don’t just recognize it, let’s say, by measurement!

### <span class="fac">[283\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/283.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1547.</span> I see, you say, two different facial objects that only have something in common. For by emphasizing this, you only emphasize certain similarities at the expense of others. But this emphasis must now be grammatically justified.

### <span class="fac">[283\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/283.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1548.</span> How is it possible that the eye, this _point_, looks in one _direction_? – “_Look how it looks_!” (And in doing so, one looks oneself.) But one doesn’t say and do that continuously while looking at the picture. And what is this “Look how it looks!” – is it the expression of a sensation?

### <span class="fac">[284\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/284.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1549.</span> I never thought of bringing the two pictures _so_ into alignment, of comparing them _so_. For they suggest a different way of comparison. Picture 🖵 also has not the slightest similarity with picture 🖵, one might say – – although they are congruent.

### <span class="fac">[284\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/284.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1550.</span> “Now I know more” – I see that _that_ is a forehead and _that_ is a beak. This line is forehead-like, this point eye-like. But how can the _visual impression_ of a line be forehead-like? And what is it that makes me say that the visual impression itself has this property? – Well, that it is not a thought, not an interpretation, that it is permanent, like the visual impression.

### <span class="fac">[284\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/284.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1551.</span> Let us try to describe that people have intentions! What would such a description look like? _For whom_ would it be a description? Ask yourself this: What purpose should it serve?

### <span class="fac">[284\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/284.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1552.</span> One can very ‘clearly’ talk to oneself in one’s imagination, while conveying the information of speech through humming (with the lips closed). Movements of the larynx also help. But the remarkable thing is that one then _hears_ the speech in one’s imagination, and not just, as it were, feels its skeleton in the larynx.

### <span class="fac">[284\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/284.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1553.</span> It is _essential_ to ‘imagining’ that the concepts of sense perception are used in its expression. (The sentence “I hear and I do not hear ‥‥” could be used as an expression of auditory imagination. A use for the form of contradiction.) A main characteristic that distinguishes imagination from sense impression and hallucination is that the one imagining does not relate to the imagination in an observing way, that is, that the imagination is arbitrary.

### <span class="fac">[284\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/284.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1554.</span> Imagine a conversation, one of whose partners is yourself, so that you yourself speak in the imagination. What you yourself say, you will probably feel in your body (larynx, chest). But this only describes, does not define, the activity of speaking in the imagination.

### <span class="fac">[284\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/284.webp),[285\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/285.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1555.</span> The feeling of the uncanny. How does it manifest itself? The _duration_ of such a ‘feeling’. How, for example, does an interruption of the feeling look? Would it be possible, for example, to alternate between having it for one second and not having it? Is not one of its characteristics also a characteristic way of progression (beginning and end), which distinguishes it, for example, from a sense perception?

### <span class="fac">[285\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/285.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1556.</span> Speaking the music. Don't forget that a poem, even if written in the language of communication, is not used in the language-game of communication. Couldn't one imagine that someone who has never known music and comes to us and hears someone play a pensive Chopin, would be convinced that this is a language and that one only wants to keep the sense secret from him. In word-language there is a strong musical element. (A sigh, the tone of voice of a question, of proclamation, of longing, all the countless _gestures_ of tone.)

### <span class="fac">[285\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/285.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1557.</span> “One should look for nothing behind the phenomena; they themselves are the teaching.” (Goethe.)

### <span class="fac">[285\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/285.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1558.</span> I observe his face closely. Why? What does it teach me? Whether he is sad or cheerful, for example. But why am I interested in that? Well, if I get to know his mood, it is as if I were getting to know the state of a body (his temperature, for example); I can draw all sorts of conclusions from that. And that is why, in the same case, I do not observe my own face. If I observed myself, my face would no longer be a reliable index; and I could not draw conclusions from it, even if it were for someone else.

### <span class="fac">[285\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/285.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1559.</span> Being ashamed of a thought. Is one ashamed of having spoken the sentence to oneself in one's imagination? Language simply has a multiple root; it has roots, not _one_ root.

### <span class="fac">[285\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/285.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1560.</span> “The thought stood before my mind at that moment.” – And how? – “I had this image.” – Was the image the thought? No, because if I had only communicated the image to someone, he would not have grasped the thought.

### <span class="fac">[285\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/285.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1561.</span> The image was the key. Or it _appeared_ as the key.

### <span class="fac">[285\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/285.webp),[286\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/286.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1562.</span> How do visual impressions differ from auditory impressions? – Should I answer: “That cannot be said; but whoever sees and hears knows that they are _totally_ different”? Could one imagine that in a person _one_ particular visual impression would be the same as _one_ particular auditory impression? so that he could receive this one impression through his eye and through his ear? Would he perhaps point to a picture and play a note on the piano and tell us that _these_ two are identical? And would we believe him? And why not? Would we believe him that the ‘affection of the mind’ is the same in both cases? And if we believed it, how could we use the fact?

### <span class="fac">[286\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/286.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1563.</span> The family tree of psychological phenomena: I do not strive for _exactness_, but for surveyability.

### <span class="fac">[286\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/286.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1564.</span> What holds together the bundle of ‘sensory impressions’ are their relations to one another. What is ‘red’ is also ‘sweet’ and ‘hard’ and ‘cold’, and ‘sounds’ when one strikes it. In the language-game with these words, it does not originally mean “This _appears_ red”, but “This _is_ red” (hard, etc.). Our agreement is essential to the language-game. But it is different with “pleasant,” “unpleasant,” “beautiful,” “ugly.” Pain is in some ways analogous to the other sensory impressions, in some ways different. There is a facial expression, exclamations, gestures of pain (as of joy), signs of _rejection_, a reception characteristic of pain, but not one characteristic of the color red. Bitterness is related to pain in this respect. One could imagine a pressure without a sensory organ. One could hear, and thus learn pretty much all the language-games with the words for auditory impressions, without having ears, and without one knowing ‘_with what_’ one hears. The fact that one hears with one’s ears is only shown in relatively rare instances. Yes, it could be that one hears, as we all do, and one only later realizes that one’s _ears_ are deaf. The _content_ of the experiences. One would like to say “_Thus_ I see red,” “_Thus_ I hear the sound that you strike,” “_Thus_ I feel pleasure,” “_Thus_ I experience sadness,” or also “_This_ is what one experiences when one is sad; _this_, when one is happy,” etc. One would like to populate a world, analogous to the physical one, with these _thus_ and _this_. But this only makes sense where there is a picture of the _experienced_, to which one can point in these _statements_.

### <span class="fac">[286\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/286.webp),[287\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/287.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1565.</span> If only _one_ person had ever made a bodily movement, could the question be whether it was voluntary or involuntary?

### <span class="fac">[287\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/287.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1566.</span> “When I exert myself, I am _doing_ something; I do not merely have a sensation.” And so it is; for one is commanded: “Exert yourself!” and he can express the intention “I will now exert myself.” And when he says “I can’t anymore!” – this does not mean “I can no longer endure the feeling in my limbs – the pain, for example.” – On the other hand, one _suffers_ from the exertion, as from pain. “I am completely exhausted” – whoever said this, but moved as freshly as ever, would not be understood.

### <span class="fac">[287\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/287.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1567.</span> The aspect is subject to the will. I cannot see something as not red when it appears blue to me, and it does not make sense to say “See this as red,” but rather “See this as…”. And that the aspect (at least to a certain extent) is arbitrary seems to be essential to it, as is the idea that it is. I mean: the arbitrariness does not seem to me (but why?) to be merely an addition; as if one said “This movement can, as a matter of experience, also be brought about _thus_.” That is: it is essential that one can say “See it now _thus_!” and “Imagine…!” For this is connected with the fact that the aspect does not teach us anything about the ‘outer world.’ One can teach the words “red” and “blue” by saying “This is red and not blue”; but one cannot teach someone the meaning of “figure” and “ground” by pointing to an ambiguous figure.

### <span class="fac">[287\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/287.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1568.</span> We do not learn to know images and only later learn to control them with our will. And, of course, it is completely false to think that we control them, as it were, by means of our will. As if the will governed them, as commands can govern people. As if the will were an influence, a force, or even: a primary _action_, which is then the cause of the perceptible external actions.

### <span class="fac">[287\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/287.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1569.</span> Is it correct to say: what makes an action voluntary are the psychological phenomena in which it is embedded? (The psychological environment.) Are, for example, my normal walking movements “voluntary” in a _non-potential_ sense?

### <span class="fac">[287\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/287.webp),[288\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/288.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1570.</span> A child stamps its feet in anger: is this not voluntary? And do I know anything about its movement sensations when it does this? _Stamping in anger is voluntary._ Coming when called, in normal circumstances, is voluntary. Involuntary walking, strolling, eating, speaking, singing would be (an) act of walking, eating, speaking, etc. in abnormal circumstances. For example, _unconscious_: when one acts as in a state of anesthesia; or when the movement takes place, and one knows nothing of it as soon as one closes one's eyes; or when one cannot stop the movement, no matter how hard one tries; etc..

### <span class="fac">[288\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/288.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1571.</span> No assumption seems to me more natural than that associating, or thinking, is not associated with a process in the brain; so that it would be impossible to infer thought processes from brain processes. I mean this: when I speak or write, I assume that a system of impulses emanating from my brain is associated with my spoken or written thoughts. But why should the _system_ continue in a more central direction? Why shouldn't this order, as it were, emerge from chaos? The case would be similar to that – that certain plant species reproduce by seed, so that a seed always produces the same plant species from which it was produced – that _nothing_ in the seed of the plant that grows from it corresponds to the plant that grows from it; so that it is impossible to infer the properties or structure of the plant that grows from it from the properties or structure of the seed – that one can only do this from its _history_. Thus, an organism could also, as it were, arise causelessly from something completely amorphous; and there is no reason why this should not also be the case with our thoughts, that is, with our speaking or writing, etc. _actually_.

### <span class="fac">[288\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/288.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1572.</span> It is therefore probably possible that certain psychological phenomena cannot be physiologically investigated _because_ there is nothing physiological that corresponds to them.

### <span class="fac">[288\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/288.webp),[289\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/289.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1573.</span> I saw this man years ago; now I see him again, I recognize him, I remember his name. And why must there now be a cause for this remembering in my nervous system? Why must something, whatever it may be, have been stored _in some form_ there? Why _must_ it have left a trace? Why shouldn't there be a psychological regularity for which there is _no_ physiological counterpart? If this upsets our notions of causality, then it is time for them to be upset.

### <span class="fac">[289\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/289.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1574.</span> The prejudice for psycho-physical parallelism is also a product of the primitive conception of grammar. For if one allows causality between psychological phenomena that is not physiologically mediated, one thereby thinks one is admitting the existence of a nebulous mental essence.

### <span class="fac">[289\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/289.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1575.</span> Must the verb “I believe” have a past tense? Well, if instead of “I believe he will come” we always said “He is likely to come” (or something similar), but still said “I believed…”, then the verb “to believe” would have no _present_. It is characteristic of the way we are accustomed to look at language that we believe that there must be uniformity, symmetry, in the end; instead of thinking, on the contrary, that it _cannot_ exist.

### <span class="fac">[289\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/289.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1576.</span> Imagine this phenomenon: If I want someone to remember a text that I read aloud to him, so that he can repeat it to me later, I must give him paper and a pencil; and while I speak, he writes lines, signs on the paper; if he is to reproduce the text later, he follows those lines with his eyes and says the text. But I assume that his record is not _writing_, it is not connected to the words of the text by rules; and yet he cannot reproduce the text without this record; and if something is changed in it, or it is partially destroyed, he gets stuck in the ‘reading’, or speaks the text uncertainly, or unreliably, or cannot find the words at all. – But that could be imagined! – What I called the ‘record’ would then not be a _reproduction_ of the text, not a translation, so to speak, into another symbolism. The text would not be _recorded_ in the record. And why should it be recorded in our nervous system?

### <span class="fac">[289\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/289.webp),[290\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/290.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1576. (A)</span> Why shouldn't a natural law connect the initial and final state of a system, while leaving the state in between unspecified? (Just don't think of _causation_!)

### <span class="fac">[290\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/290.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1577.</span> What one calls a change in thinking is, of course, not only a change in speaking, but also in acting.

### <span class="fac">[290\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/290.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1578.</span> One sees the terminology, but not the _technique_ of its application.

### <span class="fac">[290\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/290.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1579.</span> One says “He seems to be in terrible pain,” even when one has no doubt that the appearance is not deceiving. Why then does one not say “I seem to be in terrible pain,” for this too must at least make _sense_? I could say that during a rehearsal; and also “I seem to have the _intention_ to…,” etc. etc. Everyone will say: “Of course I don’t say that; because I _know_ whether I am in pain.” I am usually not _interested_ in whether I seem to be in pain; because the conclusions that I draw from this impression in the other, I do not draw for myself. I do not say: “I am groaning terribly, I must go to the doctor”; but rather “He is groaning terribly, he must…”.

### <span class="fac">[290\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/290.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1580.</span> If this makes no sense: “I know that I am in pain” – nor this: “I feel my pain,” – then it also makes no sense to say: “I do not care about my own groaning, because _I know_ that I am in pain” – or “because I _feel_ my pain.” But it is true: “I do not care about my groaning.”

### <span class="fac">[290\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/290.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1581.</span> I conclude from the observation of his behaviour that he must go to the doctor; but I do not draw this conclusion for myself from the observation of my behaviour. Or rather: I sometimes do this too, but _not_ in analogous cases.

### <span class="fac">[290\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/290.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1582.</span> It helps here if one considers that it is a primitive behaviour to care for the painful spot of the other, to treat it, and not only one’s own – i.e. to pay attention to the other’s pain-behaviour, as well as to not pay attention to one’s own pain-behaviour.

### <span class="fac">[290\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/290.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1583.</span> What does the word “primitive” mean here? Probably that the behaviour is _pre-linguistic_: that a language-game is based _on it_, that it is the prototype of a way of thinking and not the result of thinking.

### <span class="fac">[291\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/291.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1584.</span> “Misguided” can be said of an explanation, such as this: we cared for the other because, by analogy with our own case, we believed that he also had a painful experience. – Instead of saying: Learn, therefore, from this particular chapter of our _conduct_ – from this language-game – what function “analogy” and “belief” have _in it_.

### <span class="fac">[291\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/291.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1585.</span> “How is it that I see the tree upright, even when I tilt my head to the side, and thus the image on my retina is that of a slanted tree?” How is it, then, that I still refer to the tree as upright under these circumstances? – “Well, I am conscious of the tilt of my head, and therefore apply the necessary correction to my perception of visual impressions.” – But doesn’t this mean confusing the primary with the secondary? Imagine we knew _nothing_ about the inner workings of the eye – would this problem even _arise_? We are, in fact, not applying any corrections _here_; this is merely an explanation. Perhaps – but now that the structure of the eye is known – _how is it_ that we act, react in this way? But must there be a physiological explanation here? How, if we were to leave it at that? – But that is not how you would speak if you were examining the behaviour of a machine! – Well, who says that, in this sense, a living being, the animal body, is a machine? –

### <span class="fac">[291\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/291.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1586.</span> One can notice a change in a face and describe it with words, saying that the face has taken on a harder expression – and yet be unable to describe the change with spatial terms. This is immensely important. – Perhaps someone might now say: when one does this, one is not describing the change in the face, but only the effect it has on oneself; but why should a description using terms of shape and colour not _also_ be this?

### <span class="fac">[291\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/291.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1587.</span> One can also say “He made _this_ face,” or “His face changed _like this_,” by imitating it – and still be unable to describe the change in any other way. ((There are, after all, many more language-games than Carnap and others can imagine.))

### <span class="fac">[291\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/291.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1588.</span> The consciousness that ‥‥ can disturb me in my work; knowledge does not.

### <span class="fac">[291\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/291.webp),[292\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/292.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1589.</span> How do I know that a dog hears something constantly, constantly receives visual impressions, experiences joy, fear, pain? What do I know about the ‘contents of experience’ of a dog?

### <span class="fac">[292\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/292.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1590.</span> Are _colours_ really siblings? Are they different only in colour, and not also in kind? Are sight, hearing, taste really siblings? Do not look only for similarities in order to justify a concept, but also for connections. The father passes on his name to the son, even if the latter is completely different from him.

### <span class="fac">[292\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/292.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1591.</span> Compare a terrible fright and a sudden, sharp pain. It is the sensation of pain that is terrible – but is it the sensation of fright? If someone collapses in my presence – is this only the cause of a highly unpleasant momentary sensation _in me_? And how can this question be answered? Does the person who reports the terrible event complain about the sensations, the stoppage of breath, etc.? If one wants to help someone get over the fright – does one treat the body? Does one not rather calm the frightened person by talking about the event, the cause?

### <span class="fac">[292\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/292.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1592.</span> Someone who pretends to be sad in the study will certainly become conscious of the tensions in his face. But really grieve, or follow a sad scene in a film, and ask yourself whether you were conscious of your face.

### <span class="fac">[292\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/292.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1593.</span> One connection between moods and sensory impressions is that we use mood terms to describe sensory impressions and images. We say that a theme, a landscape, is sad, cheerful, etc. But it is, of course, much more important that we describe the human face, the action, the behaviour, by means of all mood terms.

### <span class="fac">[292\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/292.webp),[293\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/293.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1594.</span> Consciousness in the other’s face. Look at the other’s face & see the consciousness in him & a certain **tone** of consciousness. You see in him, on him, joy, indifference, interest, emotion, dullness, etc. The light in the other’s face. Do you look in _yourself_ to recognize the anger in _his_ face? It is just as clear there as in your own breast. (And what does one want to say now? That the other’s face encourages me to imitate, & that I thus feel small movements & muscle tensions in myself, & that the sum of these is _mine_? Nonsense. Nonsense – because you are making assumptions instead of merely describing. Whoever has explanations swirling in his head neglects to focus on the most important facts.)

### <span class="fac">[293\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/293.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1595.</span> Knowledge and having an opinion do not have facial expressions. There probably is a tone, a gesture of conviction, but only when something is said with that tone, with that gesture.

### <span class="fac">[293\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/293.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1596.</span> “Consciousness is as evident in his face and behaviour as in myself.”

### <span class="fac">[293\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/293.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1597.</span> What would it mean to be mistaken in thinking that he has a mind, consciousness? And what would it mean to be mistaken and to have none myself? What would it mean to say “I am not conscious”? – – But don’t I really know that consciousness is in me? – So I know it, and yet the statement that it is so has no purpose? And how remarkable that one can learn to communicate about this matter with other people!

### <span class="fac">[293\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/293.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1598.</span> One can pretend to be unconscious; but also _conscious_?

### <span class="fac">[293\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/293.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1599.</span> How would it be if someone seriously told me that he really doesn’t know whether he is dreaming or awake? – Can this situation exist: Someone says “I think I’m dreaming now”; he really wakes up soon after, remembers that utterance in the dream and says “So I was really right!” – – This story can only _mean_: Someone dreamed that he said that he was dreaming. Imagine, someone unconscious says (perhaps in anesthesia) “I am conscious” – would we say “He must know”? And if someone spoke in his sleep, “I am sleeping,” – would we say “He is quite right”? Does someone tell an untruth when he says, “I am not conscious”? (And the truth, when he says it unconsciously? And how, if a parrot said “I don’t understand a word,” or a gramophone “I am just a machine”?)

### <span class="fac">[293\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/293.webp),[294\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/294.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1600.</span> Imagine, in a daydream, I let myself say “I am just fantasizing,” would that be _true_? Imagine, I write such a fantasy, or story, a fantasized dialogue, and in it I say “I am fantasizing” – – but when I write it down, – how does it show that these words are words of fantasy and that I have not stepped out of the fantasy? Wouldn’t it really be possible that the dreamer, as if stepping out of the dream, speaks in his sleep “I am dreaming”? It would probably be conceivable that such a language-game existed. This is connected with the problem of ‘meaning’. For I can write in a dialogue “I am healthy” and not _mean_ it, even though it is true. The words belong to this language-game and not to that one.

### <span class="fac">[294\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/294.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1601.</span> ‘True’ and ‘False’ in a dream. I dream that it is raining and that I say “It is raining” – – on the other hand: I dream that I say “I am dreaming.”

### <span class="fac">[294\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/294.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1602.</span> Does the verb “to dream” have a present tense? How does a person learn to use it?

### <span class="fac">[294\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/294.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1603.</span> A language-game analogous to a fragment of another. A space projected into limited pieces of space.

### <span class="fac">[294\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/294.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1604.</span> Suppose I had an experience similar to waking up, and I found myself in a completely different environment, with people assuring me that I had been asleep. Suppose further that I insist that I did not dream, but that I lived in some way outside my sleeping body. What is the function of this assertion?

### <span class="fac">[294\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/294.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1605.</span> “‘I am conscious,’ that is a statement for which no doubt is possible.” Why shouldn't this say the same thing as this: “‘I am conscious’ is not a sentence”? One could also say: What harm is there in someone saying that ‘I am conscious’ is a statement that allows no doubt? How could I contradict him?
