Suppose someone said this to me – why shouldn't I get used to not answering him, instead of starting an argument? Why shouldn't I treat his words as if he were whistling or humming?

### <span class="fac">[294\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/294.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1606.</span> “Nothing is as certain as that I am conscious.” Why then shouldn't I leave it at that? This certainty, like a great force whose point of attack does not move, which therefore does no work.

### <span class="fac">[294\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/294.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1607.</span> In a game of dice, someone rolls a 5, then a 4, and says, “If only I had rolled a 4 instead of the 5, I would have won!” The condition is not physical, but only mathematical, because one could answer: “If you had rolled a 4 first, who knows what you would have rolled afterward!”

### <span class="fac">[295\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/295.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1608.</span> If you now say “The use of the subjunctive is based on the belief in a law of nature,” then one can reply: “It does not rest on this belief; it and this belief are on the same level.”

### <span class="fac">[295\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/295.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1609.</span> Fate is in contrast to the law of nature. One wants to understand the law of nature and use it, but not fate.

### <span class="fac">[295\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/295.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1610.</span> The concept of the ‘fragment’. It is not easy to describe the use of this word, even in passing.

### <span class="fac">[295\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/295.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1611.</span> When we want to describe the use of a word, is it not similar to when one wants to paint a portrait? I see it clearly; the expression of these features is well known to me; and if I were to paint it, I would not know where to begin. And if I actually paint a picture, it is completely inadequate. If I had a description in front of me, I would recognize it; I might also notice errors in it. But the fact that I can do that does not mean that I could have given the description myself.

### <span class="fac">[295\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/295.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1612.</span> Two objects ‘belong together’. One teaches a child to ‘arrange’ things, and accompanies the activity with the words “These belong together.” The child also learns this expression. The child could also arrange the things with the help of these words and certain gestures. But the words can also be merely an accompaniment to the action. A language-game. Imagine such a game without words, but with the accompaniment of music that fits the actions.

### <span class="fac">[295\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/295.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1613.</span> “Put it _here_” – as I indicate the place with my finger – this is an _absolute_ indication of location. And, whoever claims that space is absolute might argue as follows: “There is, after all, a _place: here_.”

### <span class="fac">[295\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/295.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1614.</span> ‘The experience of similarity’. Think of the language-game: “Recognize similarities,” or “Indicate similarities,” or “Arrange things according to their similarity.” Where is the special experience here? The special experiential content that one is looking for?

### <span class="fac">[295\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/295.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1615.</span> The duration of sensation. Compare the duration of a sound sensation with the duration of the tactile sensation that teaches you that you are holding a ball in your hand; and with the “feeling” that teaches you that your knees are bent. And here we have another reason why we might want to say that the sensation of posture has no content.

### <span class="fac">[296\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/296.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1616.</span> Philosophical Investigations: conceptual investigations. The essential thing about metaphysics: that it does not clearly distinguish between factual and conceptual investigations. The metaphysical question always appears to be a factual one, although the problem is a conceptual one.

### <span class="fac">[296\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/296.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1617.</span> But what does a conceptual investigation do? Is it one of the natural history of human concepts? Well, natural history describes, let's say, plants and animals. But could it be that plants have been described in every detail, and now someone comes along who sees analogies in their structure that were not seen before? That he thus creates a new order in these descriptions. He says, for example: “Do not compare this part with that, but rather with this!” (Goethe wanted to do something like this) and in doing so, he does not necessarily speak of _descent_; nevertheless, the new arrangement _could_ also give a new direction to the scientific investigation. He says “Look at it _this_ way!” – and this can now have various advantages and consequences.

### <span class="fac">[296\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/296.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1618.</span> Why do we count? Has it proven to be practical? Do we have our concepts, e.g., the psychological ones, because it is advantageous? – And yet we have _certain_ concepts precisely because of that, we have introduced them for that reason.

### <span class="fac">[296\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/296.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1619.</span> One should not believe that it is a simplification to consider seeing with one eye instead of seeing with both eyes; if one is clear that one does not feel seeing in the eyes. The idea of the visual object is much more difficult to carry out for two-eyed seeing. Because what is the two-eyed ‘visual image’? ‘The portrait of what one really _sees_’, ‘of the visual impression itself’.

### <span class="fac">[296\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/296.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1620.</span> It seems to me: If I only had the right colors and things at my disposal, I could _exactly_ represent what I see. And to a certain extent, this is indeed the case. And that account of what I have in front of me, and the description of what I see, have the same form. – But they _leave out_, for example, the wandering of the eye, and also, for example, the reading of a script in the field of vision and every aspect of what is seen.

### <span class="fac">[296\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/296.webp),[297\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1621.</span> If what you are looking at is a large panel, or a flat wall with a figure on it, then a picture of this figure can be considered an accurate description. If the figure is, for example, F, what more can one want than that it be accurately drawn; and yet there is still a completely different description that is not contained in the drawing. The same is true if the figure is a face.

### <span class="fac">[297\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1622.</span> What is, in _one_ sense, a slight inaccuracy in the description, is, in another sense, a large one.

### <span class="fac">[297\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1623.</span> Active and passive. Can one command it, or not? This may seem like a far-fetched distinction, but it is not. It is similar to: “Can one (_logical_ possibility) decide to do it, or not?” – And that is: How is it surrounded by thoughts, feelings, etc.?

### <span class="fac">[297\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1624.</span> What would a society of all deaf people look like? What, a society of ‘mentally deficient’ people? _Important question_! What, then, is a society that has never played many of our usual language-games?

### <span class="fac">[297\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1625.</span> To be conscious of a sameness of colors in a picture, or that _this_ color is darker than that one. Am I conscious all the time, when listening to this piece, that it is by ‥‥? When is one _conscious_ of a fact?

### <span class="fac">[297\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1626.</span> Love is not a feeling. Love is tested; pain is not.

### <span class="fac">[297\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1627.</span> I see something _in different contexts_. (Is this more related to imagining than to seeing?)

### <span class="fac">[297\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1628.</span> It is as if one had applied a concept to what one sees and now sees it along with that concept. This concept itself is hardly visible, but it spreads an _ordering veil over the objects_.

### <span class="fac">[297\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1629.</span> “What do you see?” (language-game) – – “What do you _really_ see?”

### <span class="fac">[297\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1630.</span> Let us imagine seeing as something mysterious, without any physiological explanation. –

### <span class="fac">[297\[11\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/297.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1631.</span> Various descriptions are given in answer to the question “What do you see?” – If someone now says, “I see the aspect, the organization, just as well as the shapes and colors,” what does that mean? That one includes all of this in ‘seeing’? Or that there is a great similarity here? – And what can I say about it? I can point out similarities and dissimilarities.

### <span class="fac">[298\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1632.</span> Could one not consider it madness if a person recognizes a drawing as a portrait of N.N. and exclaims, “That is Mr. N.N.!” – “He must be mad,” one says of him, “He sees a piece of paper with black lines on it and thinks it is a person!”

### <span class="fac">[298\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1633.</span> ‘Seeing the figure _as_ ‥‥’ has something occult, something incomprehensible. One might say: “Something has changed, and yet nothing has changed.” – – But do not try to explain it! Rather, consider the rest of seeing as occult as well.

### <span class="fac">[298\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1634.</span> The expression of that experience remains: “I see it as a mountain,” “I see it as a wedge,” “I see it with this base and this point, but turned over,” etc. And the words “mountain,” “wedge,” “base,” “turned over” are also only strokes or sounds – _with a use_.

### <span class="fac">[298\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1635.</span> Think of a depiction of a face from the front and in profile at the same time, as in some modern pictures. A depiction in which a movement, a change, a shifting of the gaze is included. Does such a picture not actually depict what one sees?

### <span class="fac">[298\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1636.</span> “I forgive you.” Can one say, “I am engaged in forgiving you”? No. But that does not mean that there is not a process that one could also call “forgiving” – but does not call it that – I mean, the working through of the inner conflict that can lead to forgiveness.

### <span class="fac">[298\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1637.</span> I would like to say: There are aspects that are _primarily_ determined by thoughts and associations, and others that are ‘purely visual’ and occur automatically and change, almost like afterimages.

### <span class="fac">[298\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1638.</span> What Köhler does not address is the fact that one can look at the figure 🖵 this way or that, that the aspect, at least to a certain extent, is subject to the will.

### <span class="fac">[298\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1639.</span> I can pay attention to the course of my pain; but not in the same way to that of my belief, or knowledge.

### <span class="fac">[298\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1640.</span> The observation of duration can be continuous or interrupted. How do you observe your knowledge, your opinions? and on the other hand, an afterimage, a pain? Is there a continuous observation of my ability to perform the multiplication ‥‥?

### <span class="fac">[298\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1641.</span> [→ (())] One _could_ explain that from the fact that the aspect is related to eye movement.

### <span class="fac">[298\[11\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/298.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1642.</span> Analogy to the opposition of ‘value’ and ‘limit value’ of a function. ((important))

### <span class="fac">[299\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/299.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1643.</span> The fact that the aspect is subject to the will is not a fact that does not touch its essence. For what if we could see things arbitrarily red or green? How would one then learn to use the words “red” and “green”? There would not be primarily a ‘red object,’ at most one that one sees more easily as red than as green.

### <span class="fac">[299\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/299.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1644.</span> Isn't what Köhler says roughly: “One could not take something to be this or that if one could not see it as this or that”? Does a child begin by seeing something in a certain way before it learns to take it to be this or that? Does it first learn to answer the question “How do you see this?” and only then “What _is_ this?” –

### <span class="fac">[299\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/299.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1645.</span> Can one say that one must be able to grasp the armchair visually as a whole in order to be able to recognize it as a thing? – Do I grasp that armchair visually as a thing, and which of my reactions show that? Which reactions of a person show that he recognizes something as a thing, and which show that he sees something as a whole, as a thing?

### <span class="fac">[299\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/299.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1646.</span> One could imagine it like this: One examines in what way a child depicts plane figures, if one has not taught it any kind of depiction, and if it has never seen spatial objects.

### <span class="fac">[299\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/299.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1647.</span> I learn to describe what I see; and in doing so, I learn all sorts of language-games. –

### <span class="fac">[299\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/299.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1648.</span> Not “How can I describe what I see?” – but: “What does one _call_ ‘description of what is seen’?” And the answer to _this_ question is: “Very different things.”

### <span class="fac">[299\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/299.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1649.</span> Köhler says that very few people see the figure 4 in the drawing 🖵 on their own, and that is certainly true. How does a person differ from normal people, who, in his description of plane figures, or when he copies them, deviates radically from the norm, in that he uses different ‘_units_’ when copying and describing? That is, how will this person also differ from normal people in other things?

### <span class="fac">[299\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/299.webp),[300\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/300.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1650.</span> A person could have great drawing talent, I mean the talent to draw objects, a room for example, very accurately, and could still repeatedly make small mistakes against _sense_; so that one could say “He does not grasp an object as an object.” He would, for example, never make a mistake like the painter Klecksel, who paints two eyes in profile. No _knowledge_ would tempt him.

### <span class="fac">[300\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/300.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1651.</span> The seductive concept is: “the _complete_ description of what one sees.”

### <span class="fac">[300\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/300.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1652.</span> Always eliminate the private object by assuming: It changes constantly; but you don't notice it because your memory constantly deceives you.

### <span class="fac">[300\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/300.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1653.</span> “Whoever sees something, sees something specific” – but that doesn't mean anything. It is as if one wanted to say: If no depiction resembles the visual impression, it at least resembles itself.

### <span class="fac">[300\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/300.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1654.</span> Someone could correctly redraw the figure when asked “What do you see here?” but answer “no” to the question “Do you see a 4,” even though he himself formed it when redrawing.

### <span class="fac">[300\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/300.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1655.</span> What do I communicate to the person to whom I make the communication that I now see the ornament _this_ way? (Strange question.) – That is, “In which language-game is this sentence used?” – “What do we do with this sentence?”

### <span class="fac">[300\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/300.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1656.</span> Let us assume that certain aspects can be explained by eye movement: Then one would like to say that these are purely of an optical nature; and there must therefore be a description for them that does not have to make use of analogies from other areas. Then one could replace the command “See this as ‥‥!” with: “Let the gaze wander in this way,” or something similar.

### <span class="fac">[300\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/300.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1657.</span> But it is not true that an experience, which can be demonstrably related to eye movement, can be produced by it, and therefore can be described by a sequence of visual images. (About as much as the person who imagines a tone imagines a sequence of air pulsations.)

### <span class="fac">[300\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/300.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1658.</span> Hold the drawing of a face upside down, & you cannot recognize the facial expression of the face. Perhaps you can also see that it is laughing, but not exactly _how_ it is laughing. You could not imitate the laugh, or describe its character more precisely. And yet the inverted picture can represent the object very accurately.

### <span class="fac">[300\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/300.webp),[301\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/301.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1659.</span> One must consider that seeing _in this way_ can have a similar effect as changing what is seen, e.g., by bracketing, underlining, grouping in one way or another, etc., & that seeing _in this way_ is, in this sense, similar to imagining. Surely no one would deny that underlining, bracketing can favor recognizing a similarity.

### <span class="fac">[301\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/301.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1660.</span> It is clear that only the one who sees the ambiguous picture as a rabbit will be able to imitate the facial expression of the rabbit. If he sees the picture in _this_ way, then this will enable him to judge a certain similarity.

### <span class="fac">[301\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/301.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1661.</span> One will also only be able to correctly estimate certain dimensions if one sees the picture in _this_ way.

### <span class="fac">[301\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/301.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1662.</span> Consider that one can say: “You must hear this melody _in this way_, & then also _play_ it accordingly.”

### <span class="fac">[301\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/301.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1663.</span> Could there not be people who cannot calculate in their heads & cannot learn to read silently, but who are otherwise intelligent people & are not in any sense ‘mentally deficient’?

### <span class="fac">[301\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/301.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1664.</span> There is no doubt that one often evokes an aspect through an eye movement, through a movement of the gaze.

### <span class="fac">[301\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/301.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1665.</span> But how strange! one might say – if one can discover a kind of composition – how is it possible to _see_ it?! – – How is it possible to know with a single stroke what one wants to say? Is this not just as remarkable?

### <span class="fac">[301\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/301.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1666.</span> Is the appearance of the aspect more strange than my memory of a certain real person, of whom I have a memory-image? Yes, there is even a similarity between the two. For one also asks here: How is it _possible_ that I have a mental image of _him_, & there is no doubt that it is _his_ image?

### <span class="fac">[301\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/301.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1667.</span> Philosophy often solves a problem only by saying: There is as little difficulty _here_ as _there_. Only by conjuring up a problem where there was none before. It says: “Is it not just as remarkable that ‥‥” & leaves it at that.

### <span class="fac">[301\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/301.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1668.</span> How does one follow the command “Imagine Mr. N!”? How does one know that the command has been followed? How does one know that one has followed it? What is the _state_ of imagining useful for here? – I mean, it is similar to seeing an aspect.

### <span class="fac">[302\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/302.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1669.</span> I see it (the chessboard) now in this way. It is as if you had given me this schematic drawing. E.g. 🖵 The figure _as_ which I see the other is not uniquely determined.

### <span class="fac">[302\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/302.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1670.</span> Imagine a triangle in a film, shown rotating around a point 🖵 and then standing still. And now it could be as if this temporal environment is still present in the image of the resting triangle. “Hanging,” I would say. But does this correspond to anything? Yes, indeed! But that only means that I am not lying, & that the _expression_ of the aspect has a use. “_What_ application?!” you must always ask.

### <span class="fac">[302\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/302.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1671.</span> One could regard the chessboard drawing as a technical drawing, according to which pieces are to be manufactured that will form the chessboard. One can now use this drawing in various ways; & one can also see it in various ways, corresponding to such uses.

### <span class="fac">[302\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/302.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1672.</span> Suppose one explained it in such a way that the aspect arises through various images and memories superimposed on the visual image. Of course, I am not interested in this explanation as an explanation, but as a logical possibility, that is, conceptually (mathematically).

### <span class="fac">[302\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/302.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1673.</span> “The green I see there is _leaf-like_. Those things there are _eye-like_.” (Which things are they?)

### <span class="fac">[302\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/302.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1674.</span> It seems here that the object of seeing is what cannot be the object of seeing. As if one said, one sees sounds. (But one really does say, one sees a vowel yellow, or brown.)

### <span class="fac">[302\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/302.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1675.</span> How could association be a lasting state? How could I associate this kind of object with these lines for five minutes?

### <span class="fac">[302\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/302.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1676.</span> What convinces me that the other person sees the ordinary picture in three dimensions? – That he says so? Nonsense – how do I know what he means by this assurance? Well, that he is familiar with it; he uses the expressions on the picture that he applies to space; he behaves in front of a landscape picture as he would in front of a landscape, etc. etc..

### <span class="fac">[302\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/302.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1677.</span> I can never know whether he really sees it. Well, then I cannot know it about myself either. Because how do I know that I now call the same thing the same as before, and that I call the same thing “the same”?

### <span class="fac">[303\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/303.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1678.</span> Well, how does it all look in the third person? And what applies to the third person, then, however strange it may seem, also applies to the first.

### <span class="fac">[303\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/303.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1679.</span> Suppose you think of a physiological explanation for the fact that I see _one_ (A) as a variation of the _other_ (B). It could turn out that when I see A as B, certain processes take place on my retina that would otherwise occur when I actually see B. And this could now explain many things in my behaviour. One could, for example, say that I behave in front of A as if I were seeing B, as I do not usually do when I do not see A as B. But this explanation of my behaviour is superfluous for us. I simply take the behaviour as it is, just like a process on the retina or in the brain. I mean: the physiological explanation is initially apparently a help, but then turns out to be merely a catalyst for thoughts. I only introduce it in order to get rid of it immediately.

### <span class="fac">[303\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/303.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1680.</span> Don’t think that you know in advance what “state of seeing” means in this case! Let the meaning be _**taught**_ to you by the use.

### <span class="fac">[303\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/303.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1681.</span> Could I have explained the phenomenon of imagination if I had been told: a person with open eyes sees something that is not in front of him, and at the same time sees what is in front of him, and the two visual objects are not in each other’s way?!

### <span class="fac">[303\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/303.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1682.</span> And it would of course be quite wrong to say: “And yet the strange thing happens” or “the unbelievable thing”. Rather, what happens is _not_ strange and is only wrongly seen as strange.

### <span class="fac">[303\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/303.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1683.</span> The old view of the role of _perception_ in mathematics. Is this perception simply the seeing of the complexes in different aspects?

### <span class="fac">[303\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/303.webp),[304\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/304.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1684.</span> Must one not distinguish purely optical aspects from other aspects? That they are very different from each other is clear: the dimension of depth sometimes appears in their _description_, sometimes not; sometimes the aspect is a certain ‘grouping’, but if one sees lines as a face, then one has not only visually grouped them together; one can see a schematic drawing of a cube as an open box, or as a solid body, lying on its side, or standing; the figure 🖵 can be seen not only in two, but in many different ways.

### <span class="fac">[304\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/304.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1685.</span> One hangs pictures, photographs of landscapes, interiors, people, and does not look at them as blueprints. One likes to look at them as the objects themselves; one smiles at the photograph as one smiles at the person it depicts. We do not learn to understand a photograph as we understand a blueprint. – Of course, it would be possible that we first have to learn with difficulty to understand a certain type of depiction in order later to be able to use it as a natural picture. This laborious learning would later only be _history_, and we would now look at the picture in the same way as we look at our photograph now.

### <span class="fac">[304\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/304.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1686.</span> There might also be people who do not understand or see photographs as we do; who understand that in this way a person can be depicted, who could roughly judge his forms according to a photograph, but who do not _see_ the picture as a picture. _How_ would that manifest itself? What would we consider as a manifestation of that? Perhaps this is not easy to say. These people might not enjoy photographs as we do. They would not say “Look how he smiles!” and so on; they would often not immediately recognize a person from the picture; they would have to _learn to read_ the photograph and _read_ it; they would have difficulty recognizing two good photographs of the same face as pictures of slightly different poses.

### <span class="fac">[304\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/304.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1687.</span> If someone told me that he had looked at the figure for half an hour without interruption as an inverted F, then I would have to assume that he had continuously _thought_ about this interpretation, _occupied_ himself with it.

### <span class="fac">[304\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/304.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1688.</span> It is as if the aspect is something that only flashes, but does not remain; and yet this must be an _intelligible_ observation, not a psychological one.

### <span class="fac">[304\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/304.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1689.</span> When the aspect changes, one experiences the second phase in an acute way (corresponding to, for example, the exclamation “Oh, it is a…!” and here one is indeed _occupied_ with the aspect). In a chronic sense, it is only the way in which we treat the figure again and again.

### <span class="fac">[304\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/304.webp),[305\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/305.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1690.</span> ‘Thing’ and ‘background’ are visual terms, like red and round – does Köhler want to say? The description of what is _seen_ includes the indication of what is thing and what is background, just as much as the indication of color and shape. And the description is just as incomplete if it does not say what is thing, what is ground, as it is if color or shape is not specified. I see one thing as immediately as I see the other – he wants to say. And what is there to object to that? First: how this can be recognized – whether through introspection, and whether everyone must agree on it. Because it is obviously about the description of the _subjectively seen_. But how does one learn to reproduce the subjective by means of words? And what can these words mean to us? Suppose, instead of words, it was a question of pictorial reproduction; and to the words “thinglike” and the like, in this reproduction, there would correspond the sequence, the order, in which we _produce_ the drawing. (I assume we could draw extraordinarily quickly.) And now someone says: “The description of what is seen includes the sequence as well as colors and shapes.” – What would that mean? One could say: there are reasons to include not only the drawn picture but also the phrasing while drawing in the pictorial description of what is seen. These reactions of the describer would somehow belong together. In one respect, they belong together, in another they do not.

### <span class="fac">[305\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/305.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1691.</span> If one thinks of currents in the retina (or something similar), one would like to say: “So the aspect is seen as well as form and color.” But how could such a hypothesis help us to this conviction? Well, it goes against the _tendency_ to say here that we _see_ two different objects. But this tendency, if it is to be justified, must have its basis elsewhere.

### <span class="fac">[305\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/305.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1692.</span> The expression of the aspect is the expression of a conception (thus, a way of acting, a technique); but used as a description of a state.

### <span class="fac">[305\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/305.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1693.</span> If it seems that there is no place for such a _logical form_, then one must seek it in another dimension. If there is no place for it here, then it is simply in another dimension.

### <span class="fac">[306\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/306.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1694.</span> In this sense, there is also no room on the number line for imaginary numbers. And that means: the **application** of the concept of an imaginary number is fundamentally different from that of a number, for example; it is different in a way that the mathematical operations alone do not _reveal_. One must therefore, in order to create space for them, descend to their application and then find that they occupy a kind of _unforeseen_ different place.

### <span class="fac">[306\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/306.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1695.</span> If this constellation is constantly and continually a face for me, then I have not designated an aspect with it. For this would mean that I always encounter it as a face, treat it as a face; while the peculiarity of the aspect is that I see something into a picture. So one could say: I see something that is not there at all, that is not in the figure, so that it surprises me that I can see it (at least, if I reflect on it later).

### <span class="fac">[306\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/306.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1696.</span> If the seeing of an aspect corresponds to a thought, then it can only be an aspect in a _world_ of thoughts.

### <span class="fac">[306\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/306.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1697.</span> When I describe an aspect, the description presupposes concepts that do not belong to the description of the figure itself.

### <span class="fac">[306\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/306.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1698.</span> Is it not remarkable that, when describing a visual impression, one so rarely includes the wandering of the gaze in the description?! It is hardly ever included, if the object is small, e.g. a face; although even there the gaze is constantly in motion.

### <span class="fac">[306\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/306.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1699.</span> The aspect can suddenly change, and then a new way of seeing follows. One is, for example, conscious of the facial expression, contemplates it.

### <span class="fac">[306\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/306.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1700.</span> I can, for example, look at a photograph and engage with the expression of the face, let it, as it were, sink in, without saying anything about it to myself, or to another. I let the eyes of the photograph speak to me. I perhaps see the picture for the first time, as a real face. ‘Engage with the expression’. Don't ask “What is going on here?”, but “What does one do with this expression?”

### <span class="fac">[306\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/306.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1701.</span> We only become conscious of the aspect in the change. As if someone is only conscious of the change of key, but has no absolute pitch.

### <span class="fac">[307\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/307.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1702.</span> If one does not recognize the Mediterranean Sea on a map when it is colored differently, this does not show that a different visual object is actually present here. (Köhler's example). It could at most provide a plausible reason for a particular _mode of expression_. It is not the same thing to say “This shows that two different things are actually seen here” – and “Under these circumstances, it would be better to speak of ‘two different objects of vision’”.

### <span class="fac">[307\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/307.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1703.</span> The fact that one can evoke an aspect through thoughts is extremely important, although it does not solve the main problem. Yes, it is as if the aspect is an unarticulated afterglow of a thought.

### <span class="fac">[307\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/307.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1704.</span> I hear two people talking, I don't understand what they are saying, but I hear the word “bank”. Now I assume that they are talking about money. (This may turn out to be right or wrong). Have I then heard the word “bank” in that meaning? On the other hand: someone is speaking in a kind of game, using ambiguous words without connection; I hear “bank” and I hear it in that meaning. It is almost as if the latter is a worthless remnant of the first process.

### <span class="fac">[307\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/307.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1705.</span> Why shouldn't there be a strong _tendency_ to use a certain word in our utterance? And why shouldn't this word nevertheless be misleading when we reflect on our experience? I mean: why shouldn't we want to say “see”, even though the comparison with seeing is not entirely accurate in some respects? Why shouldn't we be impressed by an analogy, to the detriment of all the differences? But one cannot appeal to the words of the utterance for this. The physiological consideration only confuses things here, because it distracts from the logical, conceptual problem.

### <span class="fac">[307\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/307.webp),[308\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/308.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1706.</span> The confusion in psychology cannot be explained by the fact that it is a “young science.” Its state is not comparable to that of physics, for example, in its early days. Rather, it is comparable to certain branches of mathematics (set theory). For there is, on the one hand, a certain experimental method, and on the other hand, conceptual confusion, just as in some parts of mathematics there is conceptual confusion and methods of proof. But while in mathematics one can be _fairly_ certain that a proof will be of importance, even if it is not yet fully understood, in psychology one is by no means certain of the fruitfulness of the experiments. Rather, there are problematic issues and experiments that one regards as the method of solving the problems, even if they completely overlook what is troubling us.

### <span class="fac">[308\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/308.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1707.</span> One could be tempted to believe that there is a certain way of pronouncing dates, a certain tone of voice, or something similar. For a number, for example, a house number like 1854, can have something date-like in itself for me. One could believe that our experience is that of a certain state of mind that prepares it for a certain activity; this can be compared to the position of the body before a jump. Here is a very tempting mistake. It is an empirical fact that _this_ position is a frequent or useful preparation for _this_ activity. But we have not learned that this feeling, this experience, is a useful preparation for the application of the figure, number, etc. Expressions such as “It is as if the future use is already trembling in the experience,” “It is as if we are already innervating the muscles for this particular activity,” etc. etc., are only paraphrased _utterances_ of the experience. (As if one said, “The love for ‥‥ is glowing in my heart.”) – Here we have, by the way, a hint of the origin of the sensation of innervation, which is supposed to constitute the consciousness of the act of will.

### <span class="fac">[308\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/308.webp),[309\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/309.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1708.</span> I say when recognizing a person: “Now I see it – it is the same features, only ‥‥” – and this is followed by a description of the actual changes. – Imagine I say “The face is rounder than it was” – should I say that it is a peculiarity of the visual image, the visual impression, that shows this to me? Of course, one will say: “No; here a visual image comes together with a memory.” But how do these come together? Yes – it is _as if_ two images were being compared here. But two images are not being compared; and even if they were, one would still have to recognize one of them as that of the earlier face.

### <span class="fac">[309\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/309.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1709.</span> I can say: I see that this figure is contained in that one, but I cannot see it in it. This description is probably suitable for this figure, but I still cannot _see_ the figure in accordance with the description. And “seeing” does not mean “recognizing at a glance” here either. For it could very well be that someone is not able to see one figure in the other at first glance, but that he could do so _after_ he has recognized the containment, as it were, piece by piece.

### <span class="fac">[309\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/309.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1710.</span> If I communicate to him by means of the two pictures that one figure is contained in the other, or if I recognize that this is so, then I am not communicating to him that I see the one in the other. What is the difference between the two communications? (Their verbal expression need not differ.)

### <span class="fac">[309\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/309.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1711.</span> I cannot see the figure 🖵 as a combination of 🖵 and 🖵 that are pushed together so that they partially overlap, so that the middle field, as it were, counts double. If someone were to say that he can see the figure in this way, could I understand it? Could I believe it? Should I say that this is possible – even if something like this has never occurred to me? Would I have to say, “You just mean something different by ‘seeing’ than I do”? – And if I assumed that, what would I now know, what could I do with it? (Of course, a physiological use is conceivable again.)

### <span class="fac">[309\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/309.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1712.</span> This also includes the question, “What would someone communicate to me who said that he could _see_ a regular 50-sided figure as such?” How would one test his statement? What would be accepted as a test? It seems to me that it could be the case that one would accept _nothing_ as confirmation of this statement.

### <span class="fac">[309\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/309.webp),[310\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/310.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1713.</span> “For me, it is now _this_ ornament.” This “this” must be explained by reference to a _class_ of ornaments. One could say, for example, “These are white bands on something black.” Yes – there is no other way to explain it. Although one might say, “There must be a simpler expression for what I see!” And perhaps there is. Because one could use the expression “stand out” for everything. One can say, “These parts stand out.” And now one can imagine a primitive reaction of a person who does not express this in words, but rather points to the “stand-out” parts with his finger _and with a special gesture_. But this primitive expression would not yet be _equivalent_ to the verbal expression “white band ornament.”

### <span class="fac">[310\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/310.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1714.</span> But it would also be possible that a large number of expressions, concepts, would be completely synonymous for someone in this case. And should one say in _this_ case that the described aspect is purely visual?

### <span class="fac">[310\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/310.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1715.</span> But the question is: why should the primitive reaction of pointing with the finger be called an expression of “seeing” it this way? One could not call it that without further qualification. Only if it is combined with other expressions.

### <span class="fac">[310\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/310.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1716.</span> Suppose someone always expresses “seeing” it this way through a memory! He says, for example, that the figure now reminds him of this, now of that, which he once saw. What could I do with _this_ communication? Can something remind me of this object for half an hour? Unless I engage with this memory.

### <span class="fac">[310\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/310.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1717.</span> If it is the case that there is a meaning-experience, but this is something secondary, how can it seem so important? Does this come from the fact that this phenomenon corresponds to a certain primitive interpretation of our grammar (logic of language)? Just as one often imagines that the memory of an event must be an inner image, and indeed such an image sometimes really _exists_.

### <span class="fac">[310\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/310.webp),[311\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/311.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1718.</span> However blurred my visual image may be, it must nevertheless have _a certain_ blurriness; it must be a specific visual image. This probably means that it must be capable of a precise description, whereby the description itself would have to have the same vagueness as what is being described. – But now, take a look at the image and give a description that fits in this sense! This description should actually be an _image_, a drawing! But here, it is not a matter of a blurred copy of a blurred image. What we see is unclear in a completely different sense. And I believe that one might lose the desire to talk about a private visual object if one thought more often about this image. The mode of depiction that is otherwise possible is precisely not possible here.

### <span class="fac">[311\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/311.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1719.</span> If I say, “He sat down on the bench in the park,” it is certainly difficult to think of a bank counter, to imagine one; but that does not prove that one otherwise imagined a different kind of bench. It might, for example, be easy for us to draw certain images while speaking that correspond to the speech, and very difficult to draw images that contradict the intention, or the context of the speech. But that would not prove that we always draw images while speaking.

### <span class="fac">[311\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/311.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1720.</span> If I now, while considering this question, simply utter the sentence “You must put the money in the bank” and mean it in this way – does this mean that the same thing happens in me when I utter the sentence as when I say the sentence to someone on a real occasion with this meaning? What could justify such an assumption? At most, that I say afterward, “I meant the word ‥‥ in the meaning ‥‥.” And here we are dealing with a kind of optical illusion! For what justifies me in the practical use to make this statement is not a process that accompanies the speaking. Although processes can accompany the speaking that point to this meaning. (The direction of the gaze, for example.)

### <span class="fac">[311\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/311.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1721.</span> The difficulty is in knowing one’s way around the concepts of ‘psychological phenomena,’ in being able to move among them without always running up against an obstacle. That is, one must _master_ the kinship and differences of the concepts. Just as one masters the transition from one key to another, modulating from one to the other.

### <span class="fac">[311\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/311.webp),[312\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/312.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1722.</span> “I have now uttered the word ‥‥ with the meaning ‥‥” – How do you know that you have done it? What if you were mistaken? How did you learn to utter it with that meaning? Whoever says, “I have now spoken the word in _this_ meaning,” is playing a completely different language-game than the one who tells me that he meant that in that report, or command. And now, is it essential, or inessential, that he also uses the word “mean” in the first case? If it is essential, then this first language-game is, as it were, a reflection of the second. Just as one might call the chess game on stage a reflection of an actual chess game.

### <span class="fac">[312\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/312.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1723.</span> Playing chess in one’s imagination with another: Both players play in their imagination and agree that _this_ one has won, this one has lost. They can then both reproduce the game from memory, write it down, tell it. – Think of tennis played in this way. It would be possible. Of course, it would not be an exercise for the muscles. (Although one could also imagine that.) – – The important thing is that even when ‘playing tennis in one’s imagination’ one can say, “_I succeeded_ in hitting the ball ‥‥.”

### <span class="fac">[312\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/312.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1724.</span> I could dream about a chess game, but perhaps the dream only showed me one move of the game. Nevertheless, I would have dreamt: I played a game of chess. One would then say, “You didn’t really play it; you only dreamt it.” Why shouldn’t one also say, “You didn’t really mean the word in that way; you only dreamt it”?

### <span class="fac">[312\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/312.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1725.</span> In court, for example, it could be a question of how _one_ intended a word, and it can also be inferred from certain facts that one intended it _that_ way. It is a question of _intention_, but could that other, dreamt _meaning_ also have this importance?

### <span class="fac">[312\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/312.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1726.</span> But what is it like: When I read a poem, or expressive prose, especially when I read it aloud, something happens during the reading that does not happen when I read the sentences only for their information. I can, for example, read a sentence with more or less emphasis. I try to hit the tone exactly. In doing so, I often see a picture, as it were an illustration, before me. Yes, I can even give a _word_ a tone that brings out its meaning, almost as if the word were a picture. One could imagine a way of writing in which certain words are replaced by pictorial signs and thus highlighted. Yes, this sometimes happens when we underline a word or formally place it on a pedestal in the sentence. (“‥‥ there lay a something ‥‥”)

### <span class="fac">[313\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/313.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1727.</span> When I pronounce this word in expressive reading, it is, as it were, _filled_ with its meaning. And now one could ask: “How _can_ this be?”

### <span class="fac">[313\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/313.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1728.</span> “How can this be if meaning is what you believe?” The use of a word cannot accompany or fill the word. And now I can answer: My expression was used figuratively. But the picture _forced itself upon_ me. _I mean_: the word was filled with its meaning. How I come to want to say that could perhaps be explained. But why shouldn’t I also ‘_want to say_’: I pronounced the word (in isolation) with _this_ meaning?

### <span class="fac">[313\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/313.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1729.</span> Why should a certain technique of using the words “meaning,” “to mean,” and others not lead me to use these words in a figurative, improper sense? (Just as I say that the sound _e_ is yellow.) But I do not mean: it is a _mistake_—I did not really pronounce the word with this meaning, but only imagined it. It is not like that. I am not just imagining that chess is being played in “Nathan” either.

### <span class="fac">[313\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/313.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1730.</span> Thinking in terms of physiological processes is highly dangerous for clarifying the conceptual problems in psychology. Thinking in physiological hypotheses sometimes presents us with false difficulties, sometimes false solutions. The best remedy for this is the thought that I don’t even know if the people I know actually have a nervous system.

### <span class="fac">[313\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/313.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1731.</span> The case of ‘experienced meaning’ is _related_ to that of seeing a figure as this or that. We must describe this conceptual relatedness; we do not say that in both cases the same thing is actually present.

### <span class="fac">[313\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/313.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1732.</span> “If you write your F like this 🖵, do you mean it as a ‘shifted’ F, or as a mirror F? – _Do_ you want it to face to the right, or to the left? – The second question obviously does not refer to a process that accompanies the writing. In the first question, one _could_ think of such a process.

### <span class="fac">[314\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/314.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1733.</span> “I see that the child wants to touch the dog, but is not quite daring.” How can I see that? Is this description of what is seen on the same level as a description of moving shapes and colors? Is there an interpretation involved? Well, consider that one can also imitate a person who wants to touch something, but is not daring! And what one imitates is behaviour. But one will perhaps only be able to imitate this behaviour in a broader context.

### <span class="fac">[314\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/314.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1734.</span> One could also say: What this description says will manifest itself in some way in the movement and the rest of the child's behaviour, but also in the spatial and temporal environment.

### <span class="fac">[314\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/314.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1735.</span> Should I now say that I actually ‘see’ the timidity in this behaviour – or in the facial **expression**? Why not? But this does not deny the difference between two concepts of what is perceived. A picture of the face could depict the facial features very accurately, but not the expression correctly; but it could also be that the expression is similar and the features are not well captured. “Similar expression” brings faces together in a completely different way than “similar anatomy”.

### <span class="fac">[314\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/314.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1736.</span> The question is of course not: “Is it correct to say ‘I see his sly wink’?” What should be right or wrong about that, other than the use of the German language? We will also not say: “The naive person is perfectly right when he says he sees the facial expression!”

### <span class="fac">[314\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/314.webp),[315\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/315.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1737.</span> On the other hand, one would like to say: Can we really ‘see’ the expression, the shyness of the behaviour, etc., in _not quite the same sense_ as we see the movement, the shapes, and the colours? What is the matter with that? (Of course, this question cannot be answered physiologically.) Now, one simply says that one sees the movement and also the dog’s joy. If one closes one’s eyes, one cannot see either the one or the other. But if one says of someone that he has seen everything there is to _see_, that is, he could accurately reproduce the dog’s movement in some way, then he would not have to recognize the dog’s joy. If the ideal representation of what has been seen is the photographically (metrically) accurate reproduction in the image, then one could say one wants to say: “I see the movement, & somehow notice the joy.” But consider in what sense we learn to use the word “to see”. We certainly say that we see this person, this flower, while our visual image – the colours and shapes – is constantly changing and varies within the widest limits. Now, that is simply how we use the word “to see”. (Don’t think that you can find a better use for it – a phenomenological one!)

### <span class="fac">[315\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/315.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1738.</span> Do I now learn the meaning of the word “sad” – when applied to a face – in exactly the same way as the meaning of “round” or “red”? No, not quite the same, but similar. (I also react differently to the sadness of the face than to the redness.)

### <span class="fac">[315\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/315.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1739.</span> Look at a photograph; ask yourself whether you see only the distribution of darker and lighter patches, or also the facial expression! Ask yourself what you see: Would it be easier to represent it through a description of that distribution of patches, or through a description of a human head; and if you now say that the face is smiling, is it easier to describe the corresponding position and shape of the facial features, or to smile yourself?

### <span class="fac">[315\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/315.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1740.</span> “What I see cannot be the expression, because the recognition of the expression depends on my knowledge, my understanding of human behaviour in general.” But is this not merely a historical statement?

### <span class="fac">[315\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/315.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1741.</span> Is it _here_ that I seem to perceive a ‘fourth dimension’? Well, yes & no. But it is not odd. From which you should learn that what occurs to one while philosophizing is not necessarily odd. We assume: the word ‥‥ should actually be used _in this way_ (this use occurs to us as a prototype), & then we find the normal use highly odd.

### <span class="fac">[315\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/315.webp),[316\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/316.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1742.</span> “What I actually _see_ must be what comes about in me through the effect of the object.” – What comes about in me is then something like a copy, something that one could look at again, have before oneself. Almost like a _materialization_. And this materialization is something spatial and must be describable entirely in spatial terms. It can indeed smile, but the concept of friendliness does not belong to its representation, but is _foreign_ to it (even if it can serve it).

### <span class="fac">[316\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/316.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1743.</span> For example, if someone were able to copy this picture exactly, – should I say that he does not see everything that I see? And he doesn’t even have to refer to the head as a head, or as something spatial; and even if he does, the expression need not say anything to him. And if he now speaks to me, – should I say that I see more than the other? I _could_ say that.

### <span class="fac">[316\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/316.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1744.</span> But a painter can paint an eye so that it stares; so its staring must be describable through the distribution of color on the surface. But whoever paints it does not have to be able to describe this distribution.

### <span class="fac">[316\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/316.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1745.</span> Understanding a piece of music – understanding a sentence. One says that I do not understand an idiom like a native speaker if I know its meaning, but, for example, do not know what kind of people use it. In such a case, one says that I do not know the exact nuance of the meaning. But if one were to think that one experiences something different when uttering the word if one knows this nuance, then this would again be incorrect. But I can, for example, make countless transitions that the other cannot make.

### <span class="fac">[316\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/316.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1746.</span> One would like to say: “The mental life of a person cannot be described; it is so incredibly complicated and full of hardly graspable experiences. It largely resembles a brewing of colored mists, in which every form is only a transition to other forms, to other transitions.” – Yes, just take the visual experience! Your gaze wanders almost _incessantly_: how could you describe it? – And yet I describe it! – “But this is only a very crude description; it only describes your experience in its broadest strokes.” – But is this not precisely what I _call_ a description of my experience? How do I arrive at the concept of a kind of description that I cannot possibly give?

### <span class="fac">[316\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/316.webp),[317\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/317.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1747.</span> Imagine you are looking at flowing water. The picture of the surface changes continuously. Lights and darks appear everywhere and disappear. What would I call a ‘precise description’ of this image? I would call nothing that. If someone says that it cannot be described, one can reply: You do not know what would be called a description. For even the most accurate photograph, for example, you would not recognize as an _accurate_ representation of your experience. Accuracy does not exist in this language-game. (Namely, as a knight does not exist in the game of chess.)

### <span class="fac">[317\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/317.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1748.</span> The description of the experience does not describe an object. It can make use of the description of an object. And this object is sometimes the one that one looks at, sometimes (photograph) not. The impression – I would like to say – is not an object.

### <span class="fac">[317\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/317.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1749.</span> We learn to describe objects, and through this, in another sense, our sensations.

### <span class="fac">[317\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/317.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1750.</span> I look into the eyepiece of an instrument and draw, or paint, a picture of what I see. Anyone who looks at it can say: “So that is how it looks” – but also “So that is how it appears to you”. I could call the picture a description of what is being looked at, but also a description of my visual impression.

### <span class="fac">[317\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/317.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1751.</span> “The impression is blurred” – ‘so the object is blurred in my consciousness’.

### <span class="fac">[317\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/317.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1752.</span> One cannot contemplate the impression; therefore, it is not an object. (Grammatical.) For one does not contemplate the object in order to change it. (That is actually what people mean by it: the objects existed ‘independently of us’.)

### <span class="fac">[317\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/317.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1753.</span> “The armchair is the same, whether I look at it or not” – that _doesn’t_ necessarily have to be true. People often become embarrassed when one looks at them. “The armchair continues to exist, whether I look at it or not.” That could be an experiential proposition, or it could be understood grammatically. But one can also simply think of the conceptual difference between sense impression and object in this connection.

### <span class="fac">[317\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/317.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1754.</span> German nouns in small print with certain modern poets. A German noun in small print looks strange; one has to read it attentively in order to recognize it. It is supposed to seem _new_ to us, as if we are seeing it for the first time. – But what is interesting about that? This, that the impression cannot initially be described more precisely than by words like “strange”, “unfamiliar”. Only later, as it were, do analyses of the impression follow. (The reaction of shrinking back from the strangely written word.)

### <span class="fac">[318\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/318.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1755.</span> We teach someone the meaning of the word “uncanny” by connecting it with a certain behaviour in certain situations (but not: by calling the behaviour that). He now says in such situations that he feels uncanny; and sometimes also that the word “ghost” has something uncanny about it. – In what way was the word “uncanny” originally the designation of a feeling? If someone shrinks back from going into a dark room, why should I call this and similar things an utterance of a feeling? For “feeling” does, after all, make us think of sensation and sense impression, and these again are the objects that our mind has directly before it. ((I want to make a logical step here, which is very difficult for me.))

### <span class="fac">[318\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/318.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1756.</span> “What do I know about the feelings of the other, and what _do_ I know about my own?” means that experience, when understood as an _object_, would fall outside of consideration.

### <span class="fac">[318\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/318.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1757.</span> Can anything be more remarkable than that the _rhythm_ of the sentence should be of importance for its accurate understanding!

### <span class="fac">[318\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/318.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1758.</span> It is as if someone is communicating something to us, who utters the sentence as a communication, but also the sentence as a mere _example_.

### <span class="fac">[318\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/318.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1759.</span> It is clear that the descriptions of the impressions have the form of the description of ‘_external_’ objects – with certain deviations. (_A certain vagueness_, for example.) Or also: insofar as the description of the impression resembles the description of an object, it is a description of an object of perception. (Therefore, the contemplation of two-eyed seeing should somewhat disturb anyone who speaks of the visual object.)

### <span class="fac">[318\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/318.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1760.</span> “Thinking is a mysterious process, the full understanding of which we are still far from.” And now one carries out experiments. Apparently, without being consciously aware of _what_ the mysteriousness of thinking consists of for us. The experimental method does _something_; the fact that it does not solve the problem is attributed to the fact that it is still in its early stages. It is as if one wanted to determine through chemical experiments what is matter and what is mind.

### <span class="fac">[318\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/318.webp),[319\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/319.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1761.</span> Whoever describes the visual impression does not describe the edges of the visual field. Is this an imperfection of our descriptions? If I close my left eye and then turn my eyes as far to the right as I can, I still see an object ‘out of the corner of my eye’. Yes, I could give an offhand description of this impression. I could also create a drawing of it, and it might show darkness and a dark, fading edge: but only _the_ person who knows in what situation it is to be used could properly understand and use this picture. That is to say: he could now also close one eye, look as far as possible to the right, and say that he sees it in the same way, or deviates in this or that way.

### <span class="fac">[319\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/319.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1762.</span> The fact that we _operate_ with certain concepts, but not with others, merely shows how different the conceptual tools are (and how little reason we have to assume any uniformity here).

### <span class="fac">[319\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/319.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1763.</span> Turing's ‘machines’. These machines are the _people_ who calculate. And what he says could also be expressed in the form of _games_. And indeed, the interesting games would be those in which one arrives at nonsensical instructions according to certain rules. I am thinking of games similar to the “racing game”. One might receive the command “Continue in the same way,” when this makes no sense, for example, because one gets into a circle; for this command only makes sense in certain places. (Watson.)

### <span class="fac">[319\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/319.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1764.</span> A variant of Cantor's diagonal proof:

Let N = F(k,n) be the form of the laws for the development of decimal fractions. _N_ is the n-th decimal place of the k-th development. The law of the diagonal is then: N = F(n,n) = Definition F'(n). It is to be proven that F'n cannot be one of the rules F(k,n). Suppose it is the 100th. Then the rule for the formation of

F'(1) 🖵 F(1,1)

of F'(2) 🖵 F(2,2), etc.

But the rule for the formation of the 100th place of F'(n) will be F(100,100); that is to say, it only tells us that the 100th place should be equal to itself, and is therefore for n = 100 _not_ a rule. The rule of the game is “Do the same as ‥‥!” – and in the specific case, it now becomes “Do the same as what you are doing!”

### <span class="fac">[320\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/320.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1765.</span> The concept of ‘ordering’ rational numbers, for example, and the ‘impossibility’ of ordering irrational numbers in the same way. Compare this with what one calls ‘ordering’ digits. Likewise, the difference between ‘assigning’ a digit (or nut) to another and ‘assigning’ all integers to the even numbers; etc. Everywhere shifts in concepts.

### <span class="fac">[320\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/320.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1766.</span> The description of what is subjectively seen is closely or remotely related to the description of an object, but does not function as a description of an object. How does one compare visual impressions? How do I compare my visual impressions with those of another?

### <span class="fac">[320\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/320.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1767.</span> We do not see the human eye as a receiver; it does not seem to let something in, but rather to send something out. The ear receives; the eye looks. (It casts glances, it flashes, it shines.) One can frighten with the eye, not with the ear or nose. When you see the eye, you see something emanating from it. You see the eye's gaze.

### <span class="fac">[320\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/320.webp),[320a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/320a.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1768.</span> “If you would only get rid of your physiological prejudices, you would find nothing wrong with the fact that the gaze of the eye can also be _seen_.” I also say that I see the look that you cast at the other person. And if one wanted to correct me and say that I actually don’t _see_ it, I would consider that to be foolish. On the other hand, I have not _ad**mitted**_ anything with my way of speaking, and I contradict the person who tells me that I see the look ‘just as’ the shape and color of the eye. Because ‘naive speech,’ i.e., our naive, normal way of speaking, does not contain a theory of _seeing_ – it does not show you a _theory_, but only a _concept_ of seeing.

### <span class="fac">[320a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/320a.webp),[321\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/321.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1769.</span> And if someone says, “I don't actually see the looking, but only shapes and colors,” does that contradict the naive way of speaking? Does he say that the one who said he saw my gaze was wrong, that he saw that this person's eyes were staring, looking into the void, etc.? But certainly not. So what did the purist want to do?
