Does he want to say that it would be more correct to use a different word here instead of the word “to see”? I believe he only wants to draw attention to a distinction between concepts. How does the word “to see” combine the perceptions? I mean: it can combine them as perceptions _with the eye_; for we do not feel seeing _in the eye_. But actually, it seems that the one who insists on the _correctness_ of our normal way of speaking is saying: that everything is contained in the visual **impression**; that the _subjective eye_ has both form and color, as well as movement, as well as **expression** and gaze (direction outward). That the gaze, so to speak, is not felt _elsewhere_. But that does not mean: 'elsewhere than in the eyes', but: elsewhere than in the visual image. But what if it were different? For example, if I said: “I see in this eye the and the shapes, colors, movements – that is, it is now looking friendly,” as if I were drawing a conclusion. – So one could say: The location of the _perceived_ gaze is the _subjective_ eye, the visual image of the eye, itself.

### <span class="fac">[321\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/321.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1770.</span> First of all, I can very well imagine someone who sees a face very precisely, who can, for example, accurately portray it, but who does not recognize its smiling expression as a smile. To say that his seeing is deficient would seem absurd to me. And to say that his subjective visual object does not smile, even though it has all the colors and shapes of mine, is equally absurd.

### <span class="fac">[321\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/321.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1771.</span> That is, we are drawing a conceptual boundary here (and it has nothing to do with physiological opinions).

### <span class="fac">[321\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/321.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1772.</span> The sheen or the reflection: when a child paints, he will never paint it. Yes, it is almost _amazing_ that they can be represented by ordinary oil or watercolor paints.

### <span class="fac">[321\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/321.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1773.</span> Whoever sees that someone is reaching out to touch something, but is hesitant to do so, sees, in a certain sense, the same thing as someone who can imitate the movement of the hand in all its details, or represent it in drawings, but cannot interpret it.

### <span class="fac">[321\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/321.webp),[322\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/322.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1774.</span> If someone says: the shape, the color, the organization, the expression, are all, evidently, (for everyone without prejudice) properties, features, of the subjectively seen, the immediate visual object, – then the word “evidently” betrays him. “Evidently” it is, because everyone admits it; and he only admits it through linguistic usage. Thus, one is justifying a statement by means of an _image_.

### <span class="fac">[322\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/322.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1775.</span> That image is only an _illustration_ of the methodology of our language. If we are all really inclined to find this image apt, then it has some psychological interest, but it does not replace a conceptual investigation.

### <span class="fac">[322\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/322.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1776.</span> One can call “methodology” _two_ things: a description of the activities that one calls, for example, “measuring,” a branch of human natural history that will make the concepts of measuring, accuracy, etc., understandable in their variants; or else a branch of applied physics, the doctrine of how to best (most accurately, most conveniently, etc.) measure this and that under these and those circumstances.

### <span class="fac">[322\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/322.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1777.</span> I tell him, “Change your attitude in this way: ‥‥” – he does it; and now _something_ in him has changed. ‘Something’? His attitude has changed; and this change can now be described. Calling the attitude ‘something in him’ is misleading. It is as if we can now dimly see or feel a something that has changed and is called ‘the attitude’. While everything is clearly apparent – but the words ‘a new attitude’ do not denote a sensation.

### <span class="fac">[322\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/322.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1778.</span> What does the description of an ‘attitude’ look like? One says, for example: “Look away from these spots and also from this small irregularity, and see it as an image of ‥‥!” “Imagine that away! Would you also be uncomfortable without this ‥‥?” One would say that I am changing my visual image – as if by blinking, or by obscuring a detail. This “looking away from ‥․” plays a very similar role to, for example, creating a new image.

### <span class="fac">[322\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/322.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1779.</span> Well then, – and these are good reasons for saying that we have changed our visual impression through our attitude. That is, these are good reasons for limiting the concept of ‘visual impression’ in this way.

### <span class="fac">[322\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/322.webp),[323\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/323.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1780.</span> The word “organization” is very compatible with the concept of ‘relatedness’. It seems that there are a number of simple modifications of the visual impression here, which are all actually ‘_optical_’. But one can indeed do quite different things in various aspects, besides separating and combining parts, or suppressing and emphasizing.

### <span class="fac">[323\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/323.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1781.</span> I can “combine” a certain _specific characteristic_ of the process of copying a drawing. I can then say that one combines the figure in the pictorial representation – or in the description – in _this_ way, organizes it in this way. (Of course, this would create difficulties in some cases; e.g., in the case of the hare-duck.)

### <span class="fac">[323\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/323.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1782.</span> One can say that I can combine lines when copying, but also simply through _attention_. Similar to how I can do arithmetic in my head, as well as on paper.

### <span class="fac">[323\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/323.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1783.</span> Can Gestalt psychology classify the different organizations that can be introduced into the unorganized visual image; can it specify once and for all the possible _types_ of modifications that our nervous system is capable of producing? If I see the dot as an eye looking in _this_ direction, – into which system of modifications does this aspect fit? (System of shapes and colors.)

### <span class="fac">[323\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/323.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1784.</span> It is, I believe, misleading when Köhler describes the spontaneous aspects of the figure 🖵 with the following: the lines that belong to the same arm in one aspect now belong to different arms. This sounds as if it is again a matter of combining these radii. Whereas the radii that used to belong together also belong together now; only they outline ‘an arm’ once and a space once.

### <span class="fac">[323\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/323.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1785.</span> Yes, you can say: the description of what you see, of your visual impression, does not consist only of what the copy shows, but also of the statement, for example, that you see this ‘solid’, the other ‘as a space’. It is precisely here that it depends on _what we want to know_ when we ask someone what he sees.

### <span class="fac">[323\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/323.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1786.</span> “But I can obviously _bring together_ elements (lines, for example) in _seeing_!” But why does one call it “bringing together”? Why does one need a word here – _wesentlich_ – that already has a different meaning? (It is of course similar to the case of the word “mental **arithmetic**”.)

### <span class="fac">[323\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/323.webp),[324\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1787.</span> If I say to someone: “Combine these lines (or something else)!” what will he do? Well, various things, depending on the circumstances. Perhaps he should count them two by two, or put them in a drawer, or look at them, etc.

### <span class="fac">[324\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1788.</span> Is the drawing itself, which you look at, organized? And if you see it organized in this way or that, do you see more than is actually there?

### <span class="fac">[324\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1789.</span> “Organize these things!” – What does that mean? Perhaps: “arrange them.” It could mean: bring order into them, – or also: get to know them, learn to describe them; learn to describe them through a system, through a rule.

### <span class="fac">[324\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1790.</span> The question is once again: What do I communicate to someone through the words “I am now combining the lines with my gaze in _this_ way”? One can also ask: For what purpose do I say to someone “Combine these lines with your gaze in _this_ way!” – There is once again a similarity with the instruction “Imagine _that_!”

### <span class="fac">[324\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1791.</span> Every thought still bears the eggshells of its origin. You can tell by its behavior in a struggle with what it has grown up with. What views does it testify to, from which it then had to break away.

### <span class="fac">[324\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1792.</span> The picture does not organize itself under our gaze.

### <span class="fac">[324\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1793.</span> It is perhaps important to consider that I can see and understand a figure today in one way, and tomorrow in another, and no ‘shift’ need have taken place. I could, for example, perceive and use an illustration in a book today in a certain way, and tomorrow encounter the same illustration on a later page, where it is to be understood differently, without me noticing that it is again the same figure.

### <span class="fac">[324\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1794.</span> Could one demonstrate one's reliability by saying: “It is true; and see, I believe it!”

### <span class="fac">[324\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1795.</span> Could one say that a conception, a technique, is reflected in an experience? Which only means: We use the expression we have learned for a technique in an expression of experience (not: as a designation of an experience).

### <span class="fac">[324\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1796.</span> Why should a way of speaking not be responsible for an experience?

### <span class="fac">[324\[11\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/324.webp),[325\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/325.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1797.</span> Would it make _sense_ to ask a composer whether one should hear a _figure_ _in this way_ or _in that way_, if that did not also mean whether one should _play_ it in this way or that?

### <span class="fac">[325\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/325.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1798.</span> Memory: “I still see us sitting at that table.” – But do I really have the same visual image – or one of the ones I had at the time? Do I also certainly see the table and my friend from the same point of view as at the time, so not myself? – My memory-image is not evidence of that past situation; as a photograph would be, taken at the time, which now testifies to me that it was like that at the time. The memory-image and the memory-words are on the same level.

### <span class="fac">[325\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/325.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1799.</span> Why should one not exclude sentences that contradict themselves: not because they contradict themselves, but because they are useless? ### <span class="fac">[325\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/325.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1800.</span> You must take the idea seriously that there could really be a word in a language that designates pain-behavior, and not pain.

### <span class="fac">[325\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/325.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1801.</span> He asks, “What did you mean by the word?” – I answer the question and add: “If you had asked me earlier, I would have given the same answer; my answer was not an interpretation that has occurred to me now.” Did it occur to me before? No. – And how could I then say: “If you had asked me earlier, I would have …”? What did I conclude from? From nothing. What do I communicate to him when I utter this conditional? Something that can sometimes be important.

### <span class="fac">[325\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/325.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1802.</span> For example, he now knows that no change in sensation has occurred in me. It also makes a difference whether I say that I just said the words to myself, without intending anything by them; or that I intended the person and the thing with them. Many things depend on this. It is also not irrelevant whether someone says to me, “I love you,” because the words of a poem are going around in his head, or whether he says it to confess his love to me.

### <span class="fac">[325\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/325.webp),[326\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/326.webp)</span>

### <span class="fac">[326\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-245/326.webp)</span>
